# A05 — AUDIT: Market data, instruments and external reference data

**Status:** DRAFT — the AUDIT of topic A05 (Document 04, section 4, step 6), for ChatGPT's review
**Date:** 2026-11-15
**Revision history:** First version, 2026-11-15 (fieldwork of 15-Nov-2026, on the AUDIT requirements of ChatGPT's A05 investigation direction of 14-Nov-2026, begun after ChatGPT's review of the A05 STUDY passed on 15-Nov-2026 and Rama's go-ahead of the same day).
**Purpose and scope:** The comparison of what the legacy code actually does with what was intended, for the entire A05 scope of the Master System Investigation Index: the live feed and its tick intake, with the published `kiteconnect` 5.1.0 library side; the candle store and the live one-minute candles, with the live candle writer; the candle calculations of `core/candle_math.py` (Rama's decision B); historical candle access from the trading process (Rama's decisions C and D); the instrument cache and the instrument master it loads; the instrument refresh, its external reference files and the alias tool; the daily candles script, the `candles` table and the candle CSV; the F&O ban list; and, across them, the configuration A05 reads, the producers and consumers of every market-data artefact, the duplication across the market-data code, the documents and records against the code and the observability handoffs, with every normal, error, empty, malformed, timeout, pacing, reconnect, restart, concurrency and shutdown flow, at the fixed baseline. It audits the STUDY's 37 AUDIT points, its 13 UNKNOWN items, its duplication rows, its findings on documents and records, the 33 items handed to A05 and the A05 scope beyond them, records each result (MATCH, ISSUE or UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) with its evidence, and decides nothing.
**Source:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch `main`, commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (Source Inventory, SRC-10), read from the local read-only clone on 15-Nov-2026; nothing in it was written or run.
**Related documents:** Documents 04, 06, 07, 08 and 10; the Master System Investigation Index (entry A05, coverage item 4, Appendix A); the Decision Register, D-07, D-12, D-17, D-21, D-23 to D-25, D-28 and D-30 to D-33; the Topic Completion Register, entry A05 (Rama's decisions A to G of 14-Nov-2026); ChatGPT's A05 investigation direction of 14-Nov-2026, its corrected direction of the same day and its review of the A05 STUDY of 15-Nov-2026 (not kept as files, D-17); the A05 STUDY (`A05_STUDY_2026-11-15.md`) and its evidence; `evidence/A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`; the published `kiteconnect` 5.1.0 library, read from PyPI as data only (Rama's decision G of 14-Nov-2026; provenance in the STUDY's evidence part P02); the A01 to A04 AUDITs and RECONCILED TOPIC RECORDs, the Open Issues Register and the Audit Findings Register.

## 1. Purpose, scope, exclusions, baseline and method

### 1.1 Purpose

This is the AUDIT of topic A05 (Document 04, section 4, step 6; Document 07, section 5). For every A05 area and flow it compares the actual behaviour, established by the STUDY and re-checked here against the code, with the intended behaviour found in the admitted sources, and records matches, issues and UNKNOWN items (Document 04, section 5.4). It classifies; it does not decide. Every issue is raised with Rama after ChatGPT's review (Document 04, section 5.5); no treatment and no V2 design is proposed here.

### 1.2 Scope

The scope is the STUDY's (its sections 1.2 and 3 to 15, and its source file inventory): the nine A05 paths of the Master System Investigation Index, `data/live_feed.py`, `data/candle_store.py`, `core/instrument_cache.py`, `core/candle_math.py`, the historical-data integration call sites in `main.py`, `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py`, `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py`, `scripts/find_symbol_aliases.py` and `scripts/refresh_instruments.py`; the published `kiteconnect` 5.1.0 source as text (the WebSocket client and the REST methods A05 code calls); the tracked reference files under `config/reference_data/`, `config/symbol_aliases.yaml` and `config/index_universe.yaml`; and the A05 side of the boundary files that other topics own (the market-data call sites of `main.py`, the configuration keys A05 reads, the `candles` and `fno_ban` tables, `sr_detector/fetch.py` and `sr_detector/models.py` as boundary evidence, the consumers of ticks, candles and instrument data, the heartbeat and alert helpers and the schedule declarations). Section 2 shows the coverage by part and file, by flow, by STUDY AUDIT point, by STUDY UNKNOWN item, by duplication row and for the documents and records.

On the live feed, wherever subscriptions, ticks or live candles arise in this AUDIT: No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.

### 1.3 Exclusions and ownership boundaries

- Callers' business logic belongs to the owner topics: orchestration, wiring and `main.py` as a file to A01; configuration sources, schemas, defaults and precedence to A02; the database schemas, persistence, connections, storage format and write discipline to A03; the broker adapter, authentication and the broker-wide rate limiter to A04; the alias consumer and screening to A06; signal and strategy use to A07; technical-analysis use and `sr_detector/` to A08 (the fetch orchestration and the row-to-Candle conversion included; Rama's decision C); sizing to A09; order placement and the exit-retry path to A10; exit and trade management to A11; end-of-day workflows to A12; the kill switch to A13; alerting (`alerts/`) to A14; data-quality telemetry, reports and the dashboard to A15; schedule declarations, the cron registry, the heartbeat helper and the invocation map to A16; deployment and installed packages to A17; the regime and PB-01 consumers to A18; tests to A19; pre-flight checks to A20 (Master System Investigation Index). This AUDIT records the market-data contract that the consumer receives and how the consumer uses it at the interface, names the owner, and hands the rest over (each part's section .5, and section 14). A finding inside another topic's code is a handoff and is not counted as an A05 ISSUE; A05 records and counts its own part.
- The 33 inherited items keep their original owners, IDs, classifications, statuses and treatments (Rama's decision A of 14-Nov-2026; D-30 to D-33; D-13): A05 records its own side only (section 4). An AGREED new-system treatment is a Phase 2 input, never evidence of what the legacy system was meant to do. A03 U-25 is carried as an A08-owned pointer only (Rama's decision E). A03 G-19 is cited by its ID only.
- Credentials (D-07 and Rama's standing instruction): no credential-validity, credential-history or exposure investigation is made, and no alert, issue or recommendation about it; `config/accounts.csv`, `.env` files, `*.local.yaml` overlays and token files were never opened; credential variable names are recorded as configuration only (section 18, security restriction). The functional audit of what the A05 code does with the credentials it is given (how the feed and the scripts receive and use them) is in scope.
- Scheduling: cron and systemd declarations are boundary facts; no declaration is taken as proof that a job ran.
- Tests belong to A19 and are cited only as evidence of intent or behaviour (HISTORICAL), never as proof of runtime behaviour.
- Paper-only paths are not audited (live-only project); they are marked where a Live and a paper branch split.
- The published `kiteconnect` 5.1.0 source is read as data only: a statement from it is a fact about the published 5.1.0 text, not about the package installed on the legacy machine, what the broker's server sent in the legacy period or what production exercised. Twisted and autobahn were not read.
- No repair of the legacy system, no treatment and no V2 design.

### 1.4 Baseline

Every line reference is to commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (D-21). Before and after the work of every part on 15-Nov-2026, `git rev-parse HEAD` in the clone returned it and `git status --short` was empty (each part's section .1 and section .7), and the same held before and after the cross-part work (section 18).

### 1.5 Method

- Rama approved the use of read-only helpers for A05 on 14-Nov-2026, at most two at a time (decision F; Topic Completion Register, entry A05), as in A01 to A04. The audit was done by AI helpers under the same read-only rules as the STUDY: one for the intended-behaviour sources (the evidence file `evidence/A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`) and nine audit parts, each with its own prefix: LF (the live feed and tick intake, with the published library side), CA (the candle store and live one-minute candles, with the live candle writer), CM (candle calculations, `core/candle_math.py`), HA (historical candle access from the trading process), IN (the instrument cache), IR (the instrument refresh, the reference files and the alias tool), DC (the daily candles script, the `candles` table and the candle CSV), FB (the F&O ban list) and XC (cross-cutting: the configuration read by A05, producers, consumers and duplication, documents and records, observability handoffs). Each helper saved its work after every record batch, and the work in progress was backed up to the temporary branch `wip/a05-fieldwork` of algocore_system under Rama's standing go-ahead of 14-Nov-2026 (never merged; deleted by Rama when A05 no longer needs it).
- Each part and the intent-sources evidence file was drafted by one helper and checked by a second, which re-read every record against the code and the cited sources. A cross-part check then completed the intent-sources evidence file with the sources that the parts had cited beyond it (IS-140 to IS-211, its section 9), checked the nine parts against each other for consistency of facts, wording, inherited items, ownership, coverage and cross-references, and mapped every part's citations of the added sources to their IS numbers. No result, count or classification was changed by the cross-part work. Claude assembled this document and made the assembly decisions recorded in sections 1.8 and 2.3; the assembled document's own sections (the header, sections 1 to 4 and 14 to 18 and Appendix A) were then checked by a further helper against sections 5 to 13, the evidence files, the STUDY and the registers, which corrected listings and cross-references only (section 18).
- Each part re-read every cited line at the baseline with `git show`, `git grep`, `git log` and `git blame` (hashes and dates only). Python behaviour was tested once with generic code written by a helper (no legacy code and no legacy import), on Python 3.13.18 (part LF, T1); such results are INTERPRETATION. The published `kiteconnect` 5.1.0 source was read as text through the STUDY's evidence part P02; what depends on the installed library, Twisted or autobahn is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED unless the repository itself shows it. Tests were read as documents only. Nothing in the clone was written, run or imported, and no broker, API or database call, deployment script or cron job was run. Every repository-wide search excluded the account file, environment files, local overlays and token files.
- Intended behaviour was sought in every admitted source: the module, method and function docstrings, comments, log and alert texts of the A05 paths; `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` (G6, G7, RL2, the Module 26 block LF1 to LF18, SE8 and SS7); the module instructions (Module 26 and MODULE 38), the instrument-refresh task instruction (TASK3) and the v2 design specification; the legacy design, decision, audit and dormancy documents (among them the 25-Jul-2026 dormancy decision and the 01-Aug-2026 integrity audit); the system map; the configuration files and their comments; `config/expected_managers.yaml`; commit messages; the five existing technical records; and the approved A01 to A04 records and Rama's recorded decisions. Every legacy source is HISTORICAL. Where sources conflict, both are recorded and none is chosen; where no source states the intent, the record says "not established" and audits the code on its own (Document 04, section 5.3).

### 1.6 Record fields, results, classifications and the KNOWN DEFECT rule

- Record fields (Document 04, section 5.4; Document 10, section 7): area and flow; issue category (Document 04, section 5.3); intended behaviour and its source (path and lines or key, with its date), or "not established"; the actual code path; the actual behaviour and the effective value; the evidence (STUDY evidence statement IDs and code lines); the result and its classification; the severity or impact where established; the owner topic; the cross-reference to the STUDY point or question and to any inherited item; and whether Rama's intent or decision is needed. Issues and UNKNOWN items are blocks (each part's section .3); matches are table rows with the same information in short form (each part's section .4).
- Results, as in the A01 to A04 AUDITs (their section 1.6): MATCH (the code does what the sources intend); ISSUE (a difference from intended behaviour; a conflict between sources that the code resolves one way; or, where intent is not established, a defect or weakness that the code itself demonstrates, of a kind that Document 04, section 5.3 lists, the basis being named in the record); UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (the answer needs runtime evidence that does not survive, or intent is not established and the code demonstrates no such defect or weakness). A lack of intent evidence alone does not make an ISSUE. An item with a MATCH part and an ISSUE part is counted as ISSUE. WITHDRAWN records would keep their IDs with a cross-reference note and not be counted; none was withdrawn in A05. Severity words ("minor", "latent") are used only where the record gives their basis.
- Classifications (Document 04, section 6): FACT, INTERPRETATION, HISTORICAL, KNOWN DEFECT, OPEN QUESTION and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- The KNOWN DEFECT rule is the A01 AUDIT's, section 1.6, applied unchanged: (a) the code implements a mechanism for the case; (b) by the language or library semantics that mechanism fails for certain whenever the case occurs; (c) the case is one that the code or an intent source says it handles, and it can arise under the documented set-up; (d) the outcome contradicts every intent source found. Each record that gives or considers KNOWN DEFECT states which conditions hold and which fails. Every KNOWN DEFECT is provisional until ChatGPT's review and the issue discussion with Rama. A prior topic's KNOWN DEFECT keeps that topic's classification (A01 C9, whose A05 side is IN-01, stays A01's and is not counted again as A05's).

### 1.7 Identifiers and conventions

- LF-nn, CA-nn, CM-nn, HA-nn, IN-nn, IR-nn, DC-nn, FB-nn and XC-nn are this AUDIT's items in parts LF (section 5), CA (6), CM (7), HA (8), IN (9), IR (10), DC (11), FB (12) and XC (13). Within each part the issue and UNKNOWN items are numbered first and the match rows after them; an item added in the check continues the sequence. These two-letter prefixes do not collide with the one-letter item prefixes of A01 (C, S, W, M, B), A02 (L, V, T, F, K, X) or A03 (D, H, N, P, Q, R, U, Y, E, G, J, Z), nor with the two-letter prefixes of A04 (CL, AU, TT, OA, RD, CS, RL, MO, PR, CG). A one-letter prefix of a prior topic followed by a letter can look alike (A01 C in "CA" and "CM"; A02 L in "LF", F in "FB" and X in "XC"; A03 D in "DC" and H in "HA"), but no prior ID has the form of two letters, a hyphen and a number except A04's, and no A05 prefix is one of A04's; prior topics' IDs are always written with their topic (for example A01 C14, A03 D-05, A04 CL-09), and an A05 ID never is. They are not the legacy labels (for example LF5, IC12, RI14, FIX-092, BL-20, M-D1, X8) of the legacy documents.
- Inside sections 5 to 13, a reference to "section 1" to "section 7" without a document name means that part's own subsection (for example, inside section 9, "section 5" is section 9.5). A part's own lower-level subsections are written with their full number in this AUDIT (for example "section 9.2.5"), and a reference to another part names the part or gives this AUDIT's section number (for example "part XC, section 2" or "section 13.2.4"). Every other section reference names its document, or is to this AUDIT where the number has a dot and is outside 5 to 13.
- IS-, IC-, CD- and RS-numbers are those of the evidence file `evidence/A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md` (written `A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md` in the parts: its sources, conflicts between sources, statements that only the code contradicts and statements attributed to Rama or the operator); the short names of legacy documents (FEED, STORE, CACHE, CMATH, REFRESH, DAILY, FETCH, ALIAS, SYSCFG, SMAP, PATHS, TASK3, M38, DORMANCY, MD1, INTEGRITY-AUDIT, EXPECTED and the others) are those of that file, section 1.4. Pxx-Snnn are statement IDs of the STUDY's evidence files. "18.1 point n" is the STUDY's AUDIT point n, "18.2 item n" its UNKNOWN item n, and "STUDY question n" its question n (section 19 of the STUDY).
- The helpers' synthetic test is named T1, without a hyphen; it is not an item ID.
- Security: credential values, tokens, account identifiers, personal names, e-mail addresses, IP addresses, fingerprints and absolute home paths are never written; per-account names are written with `<ACCOUNT_ID>`, and passages that hold such material are cited by path and line only.
- Line references are written "L123" or "L123 to L130"; dates as 2026-11-15 or 15-Nov-2026.

Where one behaviour appears in two parts, the part that owns the mechanism keeps the count and the other cites it (for example the heartbeat wrapper's FAILED status for any exit, an A16 helper fact, counted in IR-05 for the refresh and cited by the match rows FB-12 and DC-15); related records that are distinct carry "related" cross-references.

No result was changed at assembly.

The ISSUEs that rest on a recorded conflict between sources classify without him, and the choice between the sources is a treatment question (section 15). Every other ISSUE goes to Rama for treatment after ChatGPT's review.

### 2.2 By flow

An item is listed under a flow only when its record's "Area and flow" or "Actual behaviour and effective value" field (for a match row, its area and actual-behaviour cells) names that route; the KNOWN DEFECT condition clauses and the other fields are not used. Selected by script on those fields, with the keywords that each flow's description names, and read for loose matches (a keyword met in another sense or in passing is not counted as the route: for example the `TIMEOUT` signal status, "overlaps" between sector files, a "silent" fallback or skip, an `is_synthetic` column or a synthetic candle's volume named in passing, a census reported at shutdown, and `resample`'s and the calculations' records under timeout or concurrency; an item left with no route is under "Normal"); an item can be listed under several flows, so the row counts do not add up to the totals, and the selection is a reading aid, not a classification. "Normal" holds the items that name none of the other routes.

## 3. Intended-behaviour sources and conflicts

The inventory is the evidence file `evidence/A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`, committed with this AUDIT (drafted by a helper, checked by a second, then completed in the cross-part work with the sources the parts had cited beyond it). It holds 211 sources by area (IS-01 to IS-211; IS-138 and IS-139, added in the check, stand in area (a); IS-140 to IS-211, added in the cross-part work, stand at the end of their area tables, IS-209 to IS-211 in area (b)): (a) the live feed and tick intake, 38; (b) the candle store and live one-minute candles, 30; (c) candle calculations, `core/candle_math.py`, 18; (d) historical candle access from the trading process, 23; (e) the instrument cache and `config/instruments.csv`, 20; (f) the instrument refresh and the external reference data, 12; (g) the alias tool and the symbol aliases, 5; (h) the daily candles script, the `candles` table and the candle CSV, 21; (i) the F&O ban list, 15; (j) market-data configuration and health signals, 14; (k) the existing technical records, 8; (l) the approved prior-topic records and Rama's recorded decisions, 7. It also holds 17 conflicts between sources (IC-01 to IC-17), 38 numbered statements that only the code contradicts (CD-01 to CD-38; CD-07 and CD-34 withdrawn, 36 counted), 14 statements attributed to Rama or the operator (RS-01 to RS-14), its dispositions of the 37 STUDY 18.1 points (its section 6.1) and of the 39 STUDY questions (its section 6.2), and what it could not establish (its section 7).

The source families searched: `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` at its market-data hits (G6, G7, RL2, the Module 26 block LF1 to LF18 in full, SE8 and SS7); the module docstrings, locked-decision blocks, comments, log texts and alert texts of the nine A05 paths, and, at the boundary only, `sr_detector/fetch.py` and `sr_detector/models.py` (A08) and the consumers' call sites; the configuration declarations (committed values only; A02 owns the files); the legacy documents at every A05 hit (Module 26, MODULE 38, TASK3, the v2 design specification, PRELIVE, DORMANCY, MD1, the integrity audit, the system map, `PATHS.md` and others); commit messages; the five existing technical records; and the project's own records (the A01 to A04 RECONCILED TOPIC RECORDS and AUDITs at the 33 inherited items, the Open Issues Register and the Topic Completion Register). No legacy source attributes an A05 intent statement to Rama (its section 5).

The 17 conflicts are listed below with the items whose records rely on them; none is chosen here. Where a later source names and replaces an earlier one, the evidence file says so; the code's side is stated in the records.

| Conflict | Behaviour or key | Sources (X against Y) | Items |
|---|---|---|---|
| IC-01 | A full tick queue | drop the oldest tick with a WARNING (LF5, the docstring, M26) against a soft kill "instead of dropping ticks" through an injected kill switch (FIX-029; later, names the dropping it replaces); the code follows neither | LF-01 |
| IC-02 | Gap handling on reconnect | G6: discard partials, "Backfill the cache via REST historical API for missed minutes" against LF7 and LF16: notify the store once, discard partials, no backfill named (one commit) | CA-11 |
| IC-03 | The window in which the tick-age watchdog alerts | "during market hours" (the feed's comment, `main.py`) against "inside the entry window" (the `_watchdog_loop` docstring) | LF-09 |
| IC-04 | Whether the tick feed may start | the 25-Jul-2026 decision: "DELIBERATELY DORMANT", reopened only by its trigger, and the telemetry comment against FIX-061's exit-retry subscription and the integrity audit's "un-enforced" | LF-17 |
| IC-05 | Live candle volume | "volume = 0 ... not tracked" (LF11) and "always 0" comments against "accumulated from ticks" (FIX-135) and M-D1 "HELD" (MD1) | CA-01, CA-14 |
| IC-06 | Live candle history size | `maxlen=390` (LF14) against the store's comment "500 accommodates multi-inning trail calculations" (names 390); not proposed for Rama | CA-05, CA-14 |
| IC-07 | The live candle writer | "persist candles to DB on each close" (`main.py` comment) against X8, EXPECTED, the 25-Jul decision C5 and DORMANCY: it "must stay dark even if the tick feed ever wires" (later, names the writer; the code was not changed) | CA-09, XC-05 |
| IC-08 | What "VWAP" is on the screening path | SS7: `vwap` "set to None (v2)" against `session_vwap`'s typical-price VWAP; the code passes the broker quote's `average_price` | CM-07 |
| IC-09 | The instrument cache's load contract | the module's IC1 to IC15 against MODULE 38's IC1 to IC15 (another constructor, exception classes and accessors, a blocking start failure) | IN-01, IN-11 |
| IC-10 | What `is_fno` marks; whether F&O rows belong in the file | "True if this instrument has F&O" (the cache's comment) against "True for F&O, False for equity" and "NSE + NFO" (MODULE 38) | IN-06, IR-01 |
| IC-11 | When a refreshed instrument file reaches the process | a startup `refresh_if_stale()` (SPEC) against an offline refresh, "NOT called from main.py at startup" (MODULE 38, TASK3), and FIX-092's in-process `reload` (later, unwired) | CA-12, IN-04, IN-05 |
| IC-12 | How the instrument file went stale on 21-Jun-2026 | the deploy hook's `git checkout -f` (commit `2b4d976`) against "a silent no-write" by the script (commit `c3c1242`); the event is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED | IN-05, IR-11 |
| IC-13 | What a candle's `ts` marks | the close time, "IST, tz-aware" (LF13, the store, the schema comment) against the bucket start (`resample` and its tests, the 24-Jul document's reading of stored labels) | CA-04, HA-03, DC-03 |
| IC-14 | How `instruments.csv` is built | the refresh docstring's RI1 to RI15 against TASK3's RI1 to RI15 and MODULE 38 (one commit; neither names the other) | IN-02, IR-01, IR-04, IR-06 |
| IC-15 | What text an alias value must be | the Zerodha name, a Kite "-BE" text (the alias file, commit `435b3a2`) against security-master texts matched exactly (the refresh) and texts of `instruments.csv` (the alias tool) | IR-10; match IN-23 |
| IC-16 | When the ban list is fetched | "Fetched at 08:30 IST" (the schema comment) against "08:35 IST" (the script's docstring, the system map; later; the declared run is 08:35) | FB-06 |
| IC-17 | The historical request rate | 2 per second, burst 2 (G7, RL2, the limits file, CTI) against "~3/s, never bursted" and "≤3 req/sec" through "the SAME broker RateLimiter" (two July documents; later; the code follows X) | HA-02, DC-10 |

Statements that only the code contradicts (CD-01 to CD-38; 36 counted, CD-07 and CD-34 withdrawn and cited as withdrawn in LF-06, LF-07 and FB-01). No other admitted source states a different intent for them; the legacy audits that record the same gap describe the code and state no other intent. By the records that cite them: the feed (CD-01 to CD-06, CD-08 to CD-10, CD-35; LF-01 to LF-05, LF-08, LF-10 to LF-13, LF-16, LF-18; CD-38, the feed's thread identity, is the subject of LF-14, which cites its source IS-138); the candle store (CD-12 to CD-14, CD-20; CA-02, CA-03, CA-05, CA-06, CA-12, CA-14); consumers documented as fed by the feed (CD-11; XC-06); `core/candle_math.py` (CD-15 to CD-18; CM-01, CM-02, CM-05, CM-09); historical access (CD-19, CD-21 to CD-24; HA-01, HA-06, HA-07, HA-09); the instrument cache (CD-20, CD-25; IN-01, IN-04, IN-11); the refresh and the alias tool (CD-26 to CD-30; IR-02 to IR-05, IR-11, IR-13); the daily script (CD-31 to CD-33; DC-01, DC-07); the existing records (CD-36, CD-37, with CD-12 and CD-32; XC-07, IR-01, IN-02, XC-05).

Statements attributed to Rama or the operator (RS-01 to RS-14). RS-01 to RS-07 and RS-14 are this project's own records of Rama's answers and Phase 2 inputs in the Open Issues Register (A01-01, A01-03, A01-05, A03-43, A04-28, A03-10, A03-41, A03-36); they are intent evidence as recorded, and the agreed treatments are Phase 2 inputs, not legacy intent. RS-08 is Rama's decisions A to G of 14-Nov-2026 on A05's scope and method; RS-09 his report that both legacy machines were terminated with no known backup (not independently verified). RS-10 to RS-13 come from legacy sources (TASK3's placement of the NSE files by Rama and his assigned refresh runs; MD1's and DORMANCY's references to Rama's attention; the daily script's comment on key rotation) and state roles or events, not an intent of Rama on A05 behaviour. They are cited in CA-04, HA-03 and DC-03 (RS-04), DC-10 (RS-05), XC-02 and FB-16 (RS-06), XC-03 (RS-07), HA-10, IR-14, DC-12 and FB-08 (RS-09), IR-09 (RS-10, RS-11) and IR-12 (RS-11), IR-24 (RS-02) and DC-13 (RS-13).

A point that bears on every comparison with production: every file, value and declaration in this AUDIT is the one at the baseline. Which commit and which files were in force on either machine (whether production ran any commit after `7d4970a`, so whether the `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` change of `b6df768` was ever in force), which configuration values and environment were in effect, what the broker's servers sent and whether any declared job ran, are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (Document 08, section 10; section 18).

### 5.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### LF-01 — Queue full: the newest tick is dropped with two CRITICAL lines per tick, against three intent sources that disagree with one another

- **Area and flow:** tick intake: `_on_ticks` putting each normalised tick on the bounded queue when the queue is full.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Failure handling (alerts that cannot fire).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) LF5, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2456 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "Queue full: drop oldest tick (get_nowait to evict), log WARNING, add new tick"; the module-instruction wording, `module_26_live_feed_candle_store.txt` L62 (17-Apr-2026): "Queue full: log WARNING, drop oldest tick." (2) The method docstring `data/live_feed.py` L265 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "Drop oldest if full." (3) FIX-029, `data/live_feed.py` L302 and L44 (14-May-2026, `5dc8563`): "queue.Full triggers soft_kill instead of dropping ticks"; "optional KillSwitch for soft_kill on queue full". The later source (3) replaces the eviction of (1) and (2) by a soft kill that keeps the ticks; (1) and (2) were not updated. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) and (2) IS-05, (3) IS-06, the conflict IC-01, the code-only contradiction CD-05; the tests IS-28.
- **Actual code path:** `data/live_feed.py` L302 to L322 (`_on_ticks`); constructor L44, L76; `main.py` L2927 to L2938 (no `kill_switch` passed).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: nothing is evicted. When the queue is full the feed logs CRITICAL "tick queue full ... triggering soft_kill" (L306 to L310); with no kill switch injected it logs a second CRITICAL "no kill_switch configured - cannot trigger soft_kill" (L313 to L316); it tries `put_nowait` once more and swallows `queue.Full` (L318 to L322), so the new tick is lost while the queue stays full. These steps repeat for every tick of the batch; there is no once-per-episode guard. FACT: `main.py` passes no kill switch, so in the wired system no soft kill and no notification come from this path (P01-S046). HISTORICAL: the comment L317 says the second put "may block briefly or raise"; INTERPRETATION (Python `queue` semantics): `put_nowait` never blocks. Committed capacity 10,000 (L36, LF5's value).
- **Evidence:** P01-S044, P01-S045, P01-S046, P01-S106 (the tests inject a mock kill switch that `main.py` does not wire), P01-S112; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The code follows none of the three sources: it drops the newest tick (against LF5 and the docstring), logs CRITICAL rather than WARNING (against LF5), and makes no soft kill (against FIX-029, because the kill switch is not injected: LF-02). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (a soft-kill mechanism exists for the case; no eviction mechanism exists); (b) fails: the soft-kill call works whenever a kill switch is injected, and its silence in the wired system follows from the wiring (LF-02), not from language or library semantics (the same reading as LF-02); (c) not established: a full queue needs a subscribed token and a stalled consumer; a subscription can come only from the exit-retry path, while the documented set-up declares the tick path dormant (`docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L82, 25-Jul-2026, IS-20; LF-17). No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; (d) holds (the outcome contradicts IS-05 and IS-06).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether the queue ever filled is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P01-S144).
- **Owner:** A05 (the feed); A01 (the wiring that omits the kill switch).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 1; P01 point 1; STUDY question 2; LF-02.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 2 bears on the intended policy only).

##### LF-02 — The feed's own kill-switch calls and notifier are never wired, although its comments say they are injected from `main.py`

- **Area and flow:** escalation: the feed's own `soft_kill` calls (queue full, reconnects exhausted, consumer restart failure) and its notifier (reconnect WARNING, off-hours WARNING, "WebSocket DEAD" alert).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Failure handling (alerts that cannot fire).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `data/live_feed.py` L44 and L76 (14-May-2026, `5dc8563`): FIX-029, "optional KillSwitch for soft_kill on queue full"; L628 to L631 (same date): "If restart fails, trigger soft_kill to halt new trading and alert operators". (2) L129 (31-May-2026, `03ac3b0`): "set via set_notifier() from main.py"; L251 (FIX-134): "Inject Telegram notifier for reconnect/noreconnect alerts"; L127, L395, L481 (same date): FIX-134 Item 37, "Telegram WARNING on every reconnect", "trigger SOFT_KILL when max attempts reached". (3) `config/system_config.yaml` L744 (31-May-2026, `03ac3b0`): "after this many consecutive failures, trigger SOFT_KILL". (4) The "ALERT DELIVERY CONTRACT" comment L485 to L493 (09-Aug-2026, `37336bc`): the feed-death notification follows the soft kill. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-06 and IS-14, (2) IS-16 and IS-15, (3) IS-118, (4) IS-15; CD-05 (and CD-35 for the configured key); the tests IS-28.
- **Actual code path:** `data/live_feed.py` L311 to L316, L396 to L408, L456 to L469, L481 to L483, L494 to L504, L660 to L667; `main.py` L2927 to L2938 (no `kill_switch`); no non-test `set_notifier` call on the feed (P01-S032); `alerts/delivery.py` L74 to L101.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `main.py` passes no kill switch and never calls `set_notifier` on the feed, so `_kill_switch` and `_notifier` stay `None`. INTERPRETATION: the queue-full and consumer-restart-failure paths reach only the log; the reconnect and off-hours notifications are never sent; the feed's "WebSocket DEAD" alert ends as an INFO record with outcome "suppressed" and reason "no notifier wired". MATCH part: the three paths that call `on_critical_failure` (a gap above 600 s, reconnects exhausted in market hours or when the market check raised, the tick-age watchdog) reach `kill_switch.soft_kill` and, when `main.py`'s notifier exists, the CRITICAL notification through `main.py`'s `_make_critical_failure_cb` (L657 to L685), so FIX-134's "SOFT_KILL when max attempts reached" is met in market hours by that route, and the alert-delivery contract's order (soft kill before notification) holds inside the callback (L669 to L684).
- **Evidence:** P01-S032, P01-S062, P01-S066 to P01-S068, P01-S069 to P01-S071, P01-S085, P01-S099, P01-S110, P01-S112, P01-S127; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The code's own comments (sources 1 and 2) state an injection that the wiring never makes; the queue-full and restart-failure escalations and every feed notification cannot fire. The tests that show these behaviours inject objects `main.py` does not wire (P01-S112). KNOWN DEFECT not considered: the outcome follows from wiring, not from a mechanism failing by language or library semantics (condition (b) is about the mechanism, which works when injected).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether any of these paths was reached is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P01-S144).
- **Owner:** A05 (the feed's arguments and texts); A01 (the wiring); A13 and A14 (what the kill switch and the notifier do).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 1; P01 point 2; STUDY question 1; A04 P07-S216 (the A05 side is here and in LF-19); LF-01, LF-03.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 1 bears on treatment).

##### LF-03 — The dead consumer is restarted at every interval without limit or notification, against "restart it once"; nothing supervises the watchdog itself

- **Area and flow:** health: `_check_consumer_health` on each watchdog interval; the watchdog thread's own lifetime.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Failure handling (errors only logged; no alert).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `data/live_feed.py` L628 to L631 (14-May-2026, `5dc8563`): "FIX-029: Periodic health-check for consumer thread. If the thread is detected as dead (not alive), attempt to restart it once. If restart fails, trigger soft_kill to halt new trading and alert operators." For the watchdog itself, no intent source states a supervisor; `docs/audit/system_security_audit_02jul2026.md` L160 (an older version of the file) raised the absence ("`live_feed` watchdog thread is itself unsupervised"). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): the docstring IS-14 (CD-09); the older audit's remark IS-25.
- **Actual code path:** `data/live_feed.py` L626 to L667, L725 to L729 (called every interval), L681 to L696, L698 to L790 (no outer `try` around the loop body).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: each interval (10 s, code default) the check returns when the consumer is alive; otherwise it logs CRITICAL and starts a new `live-feed-consumer-restarted` thread, with a WARNING on success; there is no counter, so a consumer that keeps dying is restarted at every interval. On a failed start it logs and calls `soft_kill` only if a kill switch was injected (not in the wired system: LF-02). It makes no notification and no critical-failure call. INTERPRETATION: an exception escaping `_watchdog_loop` ends the watchdog for the rest of the process, and with it the consumer check and the stale alarm; `_start_watchdog` is called only from `connect()`.
- **Evidence:** P01-S077, P01-S085, P01-S086, P01-S109, P01-S117; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. Against the docstring: unlimited restarts instead of one; no operator alert ("alert operators") on the failure branch in the wired system. The unsupervised watchdog is a weakness the code demonstrates (failure handling), with the older audit's statement as context; no intent source requires a supervisor. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism fails by semantics; the outcome is a design difference).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether the consumer ever died is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P01-S144). HISTORICAL: the B1 comment L708 to L717 (25-Jul-2026) says that before `b3c62a4` this check "NEVER RAN in production"; a comment, not runtime evidence.
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 1; P01 points 14 and 15; LF-02.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### LF-04 — The watchdog's forced reconnect calls `close()`, which in the published library stops retrying before it closes; the feed's comments expect the library to reconnect

- **Area and flow:** stale-feed recovery: watchdog sets `_force_reconnect`; the consumer calls `ticker.close()`; what follows.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (stated behaviour that the code may not produce); Failure handling (no safe recovery).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `data/live_feed.py` L108 to L111 (16-May-2026, `c3ce214`): "force a reconnect via ticker.close() (which triggers _on_close + the kiteconnect auto-reconnect path)"; L702 to L706 (25-Apr-2026, `13b3d09`): "force-close the ticker so kiteconnect's auto-reconnect machinery rebuilds the socket". (2) LF3, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2434 to L2437 (17-Apr-2026): "On network disconnect: KiteTicker auto-reconnects ... KiteTicker built-in reconnect handles backoff". (3) The published library's own text, `ticker.py` L355: "method `stop_retry` can be used to stop ongoing reconnect attempts"; L551 to L554: `close()` calls `stop_retry()` first; L562 to L565: `stop_retry()` calls `factory.stopTrying()` (library text, not a legacy source; IS-147). Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-11, (2) IS-03, the code-only contradiction CD-03; STUDY question 5 is answered by evidence at its date there (IS-03, IS-11).
- **Actual code path:** `data/live_feed.py` L783 to L786 (flag set), L548 to L562 (consumer calls `ticker.close()`); published `ticker.py` L546 to L565 (`close()` calls `stop_retry()`, which calls `factory.stopTrying()`, then `_close()`); the feed calls the library's `connect()` only at L151, from its own `connect()`, which `main.py` calls once (L3947).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (published 5.1.0 text): the library's `close()` asks the factory to stop retrying before it sends the close frame. INTERPRETATION: the library's `close()` is written as a final close; whether a reconnection follows is Twisted's behaviour after `stopTrying()`, UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, and which library version ran is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. If no reconnection follows, `_connected` stays False once `on_close` has run, the watchdog skips while disconnected (L742 to L743), the alert flag stays set, nothing in the feed connects again, and only `is_connected()` (False, reported without gating) shows the state. HISTORICAL: the tests named for the forced reconnect do not show a reconnection: `test_reconnection_after_flag_close` calls `_on_reconnect` itself, and the B.6 stale test's `close()` assertion is met by `disconnect()` (P01-S107, P01-S151).
- **Evidence:** P01-S083, P01-S084, P01-S107, P01-S151; P02-S024, P02-S025, P02-S086; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with an UNKNOWN part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. ISSUE part: the sources conflict — the feed's comments rely on the library reconnecting after `close()`, while the library's own text calls `stop_retry` the way to stop reconnect attempts and `close()` calls it; the code resolves the conflict by relying on an outcome the pinned library does not promise, and adds no reconnection of its own. UNKNOWN part: the runtime outcome (Twisted's `stopTrying()`; the installed package). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: condition (b) fails (the failure is not certain by the semantics read; Twisted was not read).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether the watchdog ever forced a close is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; the alarm arms only after a first tick (LF-09). No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 2; P01 points 3 and 26; P02 point 1; STUDY question 5; LF-05, LF-18, LF-20.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 5 bears on treatment).

##### LF-05 — After reconnects run out the feed stays down for the rest of the process, while the health readout is non-gating because "a feed disconnect auto-heals via reconnect"; the reconnect count meant "for metrics/alerting" has no reader

- **Area and flow:** recovery after `_on_noreconnect`; the `/health` readout of the feed; the feed's metrics.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (stated rationale the code does not support); Failure handling (no safe recovery within the process); Weakness (a value defined but never read).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) LF3, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2434 to L2435 (17-Apr-2026): "After max: fire on_critical_failure + log CRITICAL"; FIX-134 Item 37, `data/live_feed.py` L481 (31-May-2026): soft kill at the limit; FIX-189, commit `5603013` (19-Jun-2026) "suppress overnight false CRITICAL alerts (drift + WS reconnect)" and L451 to L453. (2) E-4, `main.py` L4056 to L4059 (08-Jul-2026, `dd4f3f4`): "live_feed connection is reported but non-gating (a feed disconnect auto-heals via reconnect and must not false-alarm monitors)". (3) `data/live_feed.py` L127 (31-May-2026, `03ac3b0`): "FIX-134 Item 37: reconnect count for metrics/alerting". No source says whether the feed is to be rebuilt within the process. HISTORICAL. ISSUE part (INTERPRETATION): after `_on_noreconnect` nothing in the feed or in `main.py` builds a new ticker or calls the library's `connect()` again; within kiteconnect only a new `connect()` starts a new cycle (P02-S037); so, unless Twisted retries by itself (UNKNOWN), the feed stays down until the process restarts — the "auto-heals via reconnect" rationale for non-gating does not hold in that state, and the readout shows only `connected: False`. FACT: `reconnect_count` and `tick_age_seconds()` have no non-test reader other than the watchdog's own use of the tick age (P01-S032, P01-S075, P01-S087).

INTERPRETATION (Python semantics): a raise from `candle_store.mark_reconnect` skips `smart_tgt.on_reconnect` (A01 C37 (g)) and the gap test for that attempt; a raise from the critical-failure closure in `_on_noreconnect` (for example from `soft_kill`) skips the feed's later steps; each such exception leaves the callback towards the library, whose handling is autobahn's and Twisted's, UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. The code's own stated rule is applied only in `_on_connect`; `_on_ticks`, `_on_reconnect` and `_on_noreconnect` call other code outside any `try`. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (BL-11's guard is a mechanism, applied in one callback); (b) fails (what the library does with the exception is not established, Twisted and autobahn not read, and no raise is shown to occur; the `_on_ticks` case cannot come from the published parser); (c) and (d) not reached.

##### LF-09 — The tick-age alarm checks only inside the entry window, while three sources say "market hours"; it is armed only after the first tick, as B2 intends

- **Area and flow:** stale-feed detection: `_watchdog_loop`'s arming and window tests.

(2) Market hours: L745 to L746 (25-Apr-2026, `13b3d09`): "Skip when outside market hours so off-session restarts do not keep firing alerts"; `main.py` L2935 to L2936 (25-Apr-2026): "tick-age watchdog gated on market hours so the alert does not flap pre-open / post-close"; `data/live_feed.py` L107 to L108 (lines 25-Apr-2026, `13b3d09`, and 16-May-2026, `c3ce214`): "if no tick arrived for tick_stale_threshold_sec during market hours". (3) Arming: B2, L731 to L734 (25-Jul-2026, `b3c62a4`): "deliberately NOT armed on subscription or on connect -- with nothing subscribed, "no tick for 30s" is not an anomaly". HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) and (2) IS-11, with the conflict IC-03 (the L745 to L746 comment IS-143); (3) IS-13; STUDY question 7 split there (window in conflict; arming answered by evidence at its date).
- **Actual code path:** `data/live_feed.py` L731 to L739 (arming), L741 to L756 (`is_entry_allowed`, fail-open), L445 (`_on_noreconnect` uses `is_market_open`); `core/market_windows.py` L75 to L106, L132 to L150.

INTERPRETATION: between the market open and the entry start, and between the entry end and the close, a silent feed raises no alarm, although the feed's only subscribing caller is the exit-retry path, which serves exits that can occur after the entry end. The library's own liveness check is pong-based, acts only after a first pong, and has no no-data timeout (P02-S038 to P02-S040), so the watchdog is the only no-data check.

##### LF-10 — `_on_connect` re-sends every tracked token in one unsliced message, bypassing FIX-059's slicing; on a failed send it logs "feed is live" while the library has begun to close

- **Area and flow:** subscriptions: the re-subscription in `_on_connect`; the library's own resubscription.

(2) BL-11, `data/live_feed.py` L327 to L335 (19-Apr-2026, `de22a04`): on a failure, log CRITICAL "and let the next _on_connect cycle retry"; "Every subsequent _on_connect re-pushes the full set." Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-04, (2) IS-17, (3) IS-18; the code-only contradictions CD-02 and CD-04.
- **Actual code path:** `data/live_feed.py` L173 to L197 (`subscribe`, slices of 50), L324 to L364 (`_on_connect`: one `ws.subscribe(tokens)` and one `ws.set_mode(MODE_LTP, tokens)`); published `ticker.py` L567 to L628 (a failed send calls `_close` and re-raises), L630 to L647, L681 to L690 (the library's own `resubscribe` on every open after the first).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): `_on_connect` re-sends the whole tracked set, as LF4 and BL-11 state. ISSUE part (FACT): the re-send is not sliced, so FIX-059's purpose is not applied on that route (it matters only above 50 tracked tokens). INTERPRETATION (published text): on a reconnect with tracked tokens the library also resubscribes its own record, so subscribe and mode messages are sent twice, both for LTP (no defect shown). INTERPRETATION: a send that raises inside `_on_connect` makes the library call `_close` first; the feed then logs "feed is live but no ticks will flow (will retry on next _on_connect)" although the library has begun to close that connection and the factory's retry is left armed; the retry is Twisted's, UNKNOWN.
- **Evidence:** P01-S029, P01-S033, P01-S034, P01-S103, P01-S129; P02-S028, P02-S030, P02-S033, P02-S089.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no failure certain by semantics; the 50-token case is not shown to arise).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established. No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.

##### LF-12 — LTP mode only: in the published parser an LTP tick has no exchange time, volume or depth, so the LF6 fallback key matches nothing, the dispatcher substitutes the dispatch time, and the bid and ask meant for the shadow tracker stay 0.0

- **Area and flow:** tick intake: the requested mode, the normalised tick, and what `_tick_dispatcher` passes on.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Logic and calculation (time source); Consistency (sources that conflict).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) LF4 L2445 (17-Apr-2026): "Default tick mode: MODE_LTP". (2) LF6, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2465 to L2470 (17-Apr-2026): the tick normalised to `instrument_token`, `last_price`, `timestamp: datetime|None`, `volume: int|None`; "exchange_timestamp preferred over timestamp field"; the day's OHLC excluded. (3) Audit #20, `data/live_feed.py` L279 to L282 (22-Apr-2026, `7ed3a3b`): "extract top-of-book bid/ask when depth is present (MODE_FULL). shadow_tracker uses these for SL simulation to avoid LTP-at-ask optimism. Missing depth falls back to 0.0 and shadow_tracker reverts to LTP-based checks." (4) `docs/audit/tick_candle_dormancy_25jul2026.md` L249 to L260 (25-Jul-2026): MODE_LTP is sufficient and MODE_FULL would activate a volume defect. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-04, (2) IS-07, (3) IS-24, (4) IS-21; the H-9 statement that exchange time is "only reliably populated in KiteTicker `full` mode" IS-41; the code-only contradiction CD-06.
- **Actual code path:** `data/live_feed.py` L191, L353 (only `MODE_LTP` is requested), L283 to L300 (normalisation); `main.py` L2966 to L2968 (`ts = t.get("timestamp") or now_ist()`, `vol = int(t.get("volume") or 0)`); published `ticker.py` L739 to L746, L769 to L776, L806 to L822.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): the code requests only LTP (LF4; source 4) and builds LF6's keys, with `exchange_timestamp` preferred and the day's OHLC excluded; it adds `bid` and `ask` (source 3). ISSUE part (FACT, published text): an 8-byte LTP packet yields only `tradable`, `mode`, `instrument_token` and `last_price`; no tick of any mode has a `timestamp` key. INTERPRETATION: for every LTP tick the normalised `timestamp` and `volume` are None, so the dispatcher gives the candle store `now_ist()` read on the consumer thread at dispatch (neither the exchange time nor the receipt time the feed stamps at L270) and a volume of 0; LF6's `timestamp` fallback key can never match; and `bid` and `ask` are always 0.0, so the Audit #20 purpose (bid and ask for the shadow tracker's SL simulation) cannot be met in the only mode the code requests, which source 3 accepts as a fallback and source 4 chooses. If a full-mode packet were ever parsed, its `exchange_timestamp` would be a naive host-local value, while the substituted time is aware IST, so the candle store could receive both forms (it does not read the argument: part CA). The legacy machine's local time zone and the packets the server sent are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P01-S041, P01-S042, P01-S055, P01-S149; P02-S046, P02-S049 to P02-S051, P02-S090, P02-S098.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The sources conflict (Audit #20's depth-based bid and ask against the LTP-only choice of LF4 and the dormancy document) and the code resolves the conflict for LTP; LF6 names a fallback field that the pinned parser never produces. KNOWN DEFECT not considered.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established. No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05 (the feed); A01 (the dispatcher in `main.py`); part CA (what the candle store does with time and volume); A11 (the shadow tracker's use of `bid` and `ask`).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 5 (and point 14 for the meaning of candle time, part CA); P01 point 16; P02 points 7 and 8; STUDY question 4.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 4 bears on treatment).

##### LF-13 — The callback registry's identity tests cannot match a bound method, so LF5's "register is no-op if already registered" fails for bound methods and a bound method cannot be unregistered

- **Area and flow:** dispatch: `register_callback` and `unregister_callback`.

Callbacks are idempotent: register is no-op if already registered." - **Actual behaviour and effective value:** INTERPRETATION (Python semantics; synthetic test T1): each access to a bound method creates a new object and a `WeakMethod` returns a new one when called, so both `is` tests are False for a bound method: a second registration of the same bound method adds a second entry, and a bound method is never removed. For plain functions both tests work (T1). FACT: the only non-test bound-method registration is the placer's, which guards itself with a flag (`orders/order_placer.py` L3407 to L3409), and there is no non-test `unregister_callback` caller.
- **Evidence:** P01-S052, P01-S053, P01-S032; synthetic test T1.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent). INTERPRETATION; FACT; HISTORICAL. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (a duplicate check and a removal exist); (b) holds (by Python semantics the `is` test fails whenever a bound method is passed again or passed for removal); (c) fails (the only bound-method registrant guards itself and nothing unregisters, so the case cannot arise in non-test code); (d) holds.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none (the case does not arise in non-test code).
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 6; P01 point 11.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### LF-14 — `_ticker_thread_id` holds the consumer thread's identity, not the library thread's; FIX-088's guard that reads it is never given the feed; FIX-064's "own thread context" is not the library's thread, and the library hands nothing to its reactor thread

(2) FIX-064, `data/live_feed.py` L113 to L115 and L537 to L539 (16-May-2026, `c3ce214`): the consumer calls `.close()` "from its own thread context to avoid cross-thread call hazards". HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (2) IS-12 (L113 to L115; the docstring L537 to L539 states the same); (1) FIX-088: the feed lines IS-138, the `core/state_store.py` lines IS-144 (the file's point-6 row records no source on thread hand-off to the reactor).
- **Actual code path:** `data/live_feed.py` L543 to L545 (`threading.get_ident()` inside `_consume_ticks`), L548 to L562 (the consumer's `close()`), L153 to L161 (`disconnect()` from the shutdown caller), L190 to L191 (`subscribe` from the caller's thread); `core/state_store.py` L285 to L310; `orders/eod_squareoff.py` L501 (the only non-test `checkpoint()` call, with no feed); published `ticker.py` (no `callFromThread`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the stored identity is the consumer thread's (overwritten by a restarted consumer); the library's callbacks run on its reactor thread (INTERPRETATION, P02-S034); the state-store guard is never used because its only caller passes no feed. FACT (published text): `subscribe`, `set_mode`, `close` and `stop_retry` call autobahn and Twisted directly in the calling thread. INTERPRETATION: FIX-064 moved `close()` from the watchdog thread to the consumer thread, which is also not the reactor thread, so the cross-thread call it aimed to avoid still happens (as do `subscribe` from the exit-retry caller and `close()` from the shutdown caller). Whether autobahn and Twisted tolerate these calls is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; on which thread the library ran the callbacks at runtime is UNKNOWN.
- **Evidence:** P01-S047, P01-S097, P01-S146; P02-S034, P02-S087, P02-S088.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with an UNKNOWN part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Both stated purposes rest on a thread identity the code does not obtain. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: for FIX-088, (a) holds (a thread-identity guard exists), (b) holds (the stored identity is always a consumer thread's, so the guard could never recognise the library's thread), (c) fails (the guard's only caller passes no feed, so the case cannot arise under the documented set-up); for FIX-064, (a) holds (the hand-over to the consumer thread), (b) fails (the hazard's outcome is Twisted's and autobahn's, not established).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established.
- **Owner:** A05; A03 (the checkpoint guard); A01 W-24 (the threads; classification unchanged).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 6; P01 point 12; P02 point 6; LF-20.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### LF-15 — Five shared feed flags are written from several threads without a lock; a connect between `subscribe`'s lock and its connected test can send a new token twice

- **Area and flow:** threading: `_connected`, `_disconnect_time`, `_reconnect_notified`, `_reconnect_count`, `_watchdog_alert_fired`; `subscribe` against `_on_connect`.
- **Issue category:** Flow and state (the same item processed concurrently); Weakness.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** not established (no source states the threading discipline for these flags; LF5 and LF1 address the queue and the single consumer only: IS-01, IS-05; `A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`'s point-6 row records no source on lock discipline for the flags).
- **Actual code path:** `data/live_feed.py` L161, L337 to L338, L366 to L372, L378 to L380, L410 to L412, L440, L767 (written without a lock), L269 to L274 (`_watchdog_alert_fired` cleared under `_last_tick_lock`), L181 to L191 (`subscribe` reads `_connected` after releasing `_lock`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** INTERPRETATION (code reading; Python semantics): the flags are written from the library's callbacks, the watchdog and the shutdown caller without a common lock; `_watchdog_alert_fired` is set without the lock under which `_on_ticks` clears it; a connect between L183 and L185 can make `_on_connect` and `subscribe` both send a new token (whether a repeated subscribe is harmless on the broker's side is not established; the library's own record absorbs it, P02-S028).
- **Evidence:** P01-S105; P02-S028.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor; latent). INTERPRETATION. With intent not established, the race is a weakness the code itself demonstrates (concurrent processing of one item, Document 04, section 5.3); no harmful outcome is shown. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (timing-dependent; condition (b) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established.
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 6; P01 point 24.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### LF-16 — The feed's operating values are code literals with no key; the three `live_feed` keys reach no feed code (A02 K-30's A05 side); `reconnect_delay_sec` is the library's ceiling and also its minimum

- **Area and flow:** configuration of the feed: reconnect limits, watchdog, gap, queue, slice, consumer wait.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (hard-coded values); Intended against actual (a configured value that the code ignores).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md` 1.4 and 1.9 (L31 to L38, L66 to L69; lines of 17-Apr-2026): "No hardcoded ... settings"; "System behavior must be fully controlled via configuration." (2) LF2, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2421 to L2424 (17-Apr-2026): constructor defaults `max_reconnect_attempts=10`, `reconnect_delay_sec=5`, "no config_loader dependencies"; LF3 L2436 to L2437: "reconnect_max_tries and reconnect_max_delay passed to KiteTicker constructor ... we configure the limits"; LF5 L2454: `maxsize=10000`; LF7 L2482: "gap > 10 min". (3) `config/system_config.yaml` L743 to L746 (31-May-2026, `03ac3b0`): `max_reconnect_attempts: 10`, `reconnect_backoff_base_seconds: 1`, `reconnect_backoff_max_seconds: 30` ("FIX-134 Item 37: WebSocket reconnect hardening"). The sources conflict: LF2 keeps the feed free of configuration, the later FIX-134 section configures it. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (2) IS-02, IS-03, IS-05, IS-08; (3) IS-118, with A02 K-30 as IS-132 and the code-only contradiction CD-35; (1) the foundation rules IS-207.
- **Actual code path:** `data/live_feed.py` L36, L45 to L53, L165, L417, L512 to L517, L564; `main.py` L2927 to L2938 (none of the five tuning arguments passed); published `ticker.py` L423 to L441, L490 to L491; `ops_dashboard/backend/services/capacity.py` L294 to L295 (A15).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: in force are the code values: 10 attempts, 5 s maximum delay, 30 s stale threshold, 10 s check interval, slice 50, and the literals queue 10,000, gap 600 s, consumer wait 0.1 s, join margin 1 s. MATCH part: 10 and 5 (LF2), 10,000 (LF5) and 600 s (LF7) equal their own sources, and the attempts value equals the file value 10. FACT (A02 K-30, unchanged): the three keys reach no feed code; the file's 30 s maximum back-off does not reach the library (which receives 5 s) and its 1 s base has no counterpart. INTERPRETATION (published text): the value named `reconnect_delay_sec` becomes the factory's `maxDelay`, the ceiling of a growing delay, not a fixed delay; 5 s is also the library's minimum ceiling, so the back-off is capped at the lowest value the library accepts; the actual delays and the library's initial delay are Twisted's, UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. FACT: the dashboard shows the configured `max_reconnect_attempts` as the "Live-feed reconnect cap", equal to the code value by value only. The stale threshold, check interval, slice size, consumer wait and join margin have no source giving their value other than the code.
- **Evidence:** P01-S089 to P01-S092, P01-S103, P01-S126; P02-S015, P02-S017, P02-S035, P02-S085; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor; weakness: hard-coded values), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The unread keys are A02 K-30's ISSUE, counted in A02 and not again here; this record counts the A05 side (the literals and the reading of `reconnect_delay_sec`). KNOWN DEFECT not considered.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; effective values UNKNOWN where overlays matter (the feed reads none).
- **Owner:** A05 (the feed's literals); A02 (K-30, keys); A01 (wiring); A15 (the dashboard row).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 7; P01 point 13; P02 point 2; related A02 K-30 (Batch T5 group G1, AGREED, 09-Nov-2026, unchanged); part XC (configuration read by A05).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### LF-17 — The "deliberately dormant" tick-feed decision against a live code path that can subscribe a token; the tripwire that "ENFORCES" the decision only counts calls and reports them at shutdown

- **Area and flow:** subscriptions: no subscription at boot; the exit-retry subscription (A10's caller); the effect counter.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (sources that conflict, resolved one way); Failure handling (a guard that detects only after the fact).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L82 (25-Jul-2026, `e754c7e`): the live tick-to-candle feed is "DELIBERATELY DORMANT", "a DECISION, NOT A BACKLOG ITEM", with a reopen trigger. (2) FIX-061, `orders/order_placer.py` L3370 to L3374 (15-May-2026, `91ab56a`; A10's): "Add exit order to retry queue and subscribe to LiveFeed. Called when SL/SL-M placement fails with LTP validation error." (3) IA-P1-06, `docs/audit/integrity_audit_2026.md` L256 to L272 (01-Aug-2026): the dormancy is "un-enforced" because the exit-retry caller "can silently end it". (4) `data/live_feed.py` L59 to L61 and L178 to L179 (01-Aug-2026, `132e571`): the counter is a "dormant tripwire" that "ENFORCES IA-P1-06 ... acted>0 = the decision silently ended"; "ANY call is the dormancy tripwire". HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-20, (2) IS-23, (3) IS-22, (4) IS-19; the conflict IC-04.
- **Actual code path:** `data/live_feed.py` L82, L173 to L197, L340 to L343; `main.py` (no `subscribe` on the feed; P01-S035); `orders/order_placer.py` L2911 to L2912, L3337 to L3359, L3398 to L3409; `core/effect_telemetry.py` L84 to L87, L102 to L114; `main.py` L1643 to L1675 (the census at shutdown); `config/expected_managers.yaml` L212 to L218.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): `main.py` never subscribes a token and `_subscribed` starts empty, as decision (1) states. ISSUE part (FACT): the exit-retry path subscribes the failing exit's token when a stop-loss placement fails with what the code treats as an LTP validation error, so the decision can end without any change of configuration or code, as source (3) records; the code resolves the conflict between (1) and (2) by keeping (2)'s path live. FACT: the "tripwire" increments a counter on every `subscribe` call (repeated tokens included) and the subscription proceeds; the count reaches the operator only as an INFO "MISMATCH(ii) ... expected-dormant" census line when `_shutdown` runs ("A crash day produces no census", `core/effect_telemetry.py` L41, HISTORICAL). INTERPRETATION: the counter detects after the fact; it does not enforce. No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P01-S012, P01-S031, P01-S035 to P01-S039, P01-S098, P01-S114 to P01-S116, P01-S118, P01-S148.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism fails by semantics; the counter does what its code says).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether the exit-retry path ran, and so whether the decision ended in production, is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05 (the feed); A10 (the subscribing caller); A15 (the census).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 4 (dormancy wording); P01 point 25; STUDY question 3; parts CA (the candle side of the decision) and XC.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 3 bears on which source governs).

### 6.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### CA-01 — Live candle volume: the store sums each tick's `volume` argument (FIX-135), against LF11's "not tracked" and comments that still say "always 0"; in LTP mode every real candle has volume 0, and for quote or full ticks the sum of a day figure would be neither the minute's volume nor the day's

- **Area and flow:** candle construction: the `volume` the dispatcher passes, its accumulation, and what reaches `CandleData.volume` and the `candles` row.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (sources that conflict, resolved one way); Logic and calculation (a sum of a running total).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) LF11, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2523 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "volume = 0 (unreliable from ticks; not tracked)". (2) FIX-135 Item 46, `data/candle_store.py` L33, L48 (31-May-2026, `25c4a77`): "accumulated from ticks (was always 0)"; L174 (06-Jul-2026, `beb8278`): "volume accumulated from ticks"; commit `25c4a77` "volume accumulation". (3) `core/config_loader.py` L1681 to L1684 and `orders/smart_tgt_manager.py` L108 to L109 (14-May-2026, `5dc8563`; A02's and A11's): "Currently CandleData.volume is always 0 (LF11), so this flag is preventive". (4) M-D1, `docs/audit/ma2_ms3_md1_25jul2026.md` L10, L245 to L283 (25-Jul-2026): "HELD, no code"; "Summing a cumulative series per tick yields neither cumulative nor delta"; under MODE_LTP "the accumulator always adds 0". (5) The PB-01 decision, `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L286 (13-Jul-2026, `5a71fb6`; A18's): the entry stage polls the broker's history, "NOT LiveFeed+CandleStore", because `CandleStore.on_tick` "sums cumulative `volume_traded` across ticks" (the entry stage's own docstring, `v3_chain/pb01_entry.py` L9 to L11, same date, names the fetcher as its candle source and does not state this reason). FIX-135 is later than LF11 and names the "was always 0" it replaces; the 14-May comments were not updated; M-D1 records the problem as held. No source states a per-minute quantity as the intent. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-31, (2) IS-32, (3) IS-33, (4) IS-34, (5) IS-46; the tests IS-48; the conflict IC-05; the switch IS-119; STUDY question 9 OPEN QUESTION (conflict) narrowed there.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L2967 to L2968 (`vol = int(t.get("volume") or 0)`); `data/live_feed.py` L299 (`"volume": raw.get("volume_traded")`); `data/candle_store.py` L63 (`self.volume += volume`), L191 to L195, L324, L342; `main.py` L3939; `orders/smart_tgt_manager.py` L397 to L402 (read only behind `volume_dependent_trails`, committed `false`, `config/system_config.yaml` L645).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the store adds whatever `volume` it is given and computes no difference between successive values; synthetic candles carry 0. INTERPRETATION (published 5.1.0 parser; LF-12): an LTP tick has no `volume_traded`, so the dispatcher passes 0 and every real candle built from LTP ticks has volume 0, which agrees in value with LF11 and the 14-May comments (MATCH part, by outcome only). INTERPRETATION: if quote or full packets were parsed, the dispatcher would pass the library's `volume_traded` (the current vendor page calls it "Volume traded for the day", current external context, read 14-Nov-2026), and the store would add that running figure once per tick, which gives neither the minute's volume nor the day's, as M-D1 itself states. FACT: no in-memory consumer uses the volume with the committed switch (`false`; effective value UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED); the live writer stores it; a stored 0 does not show whether anything traded (P03-S080).
- **Evidence:** P03-S011, P03-S014, P03-S015, P03-S064 to P03-S066, P03-S072, P03-S080, P03-S088, P03-S112, P03-S114, P03-S116; P01-S042; P02-S050; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The sources conflict and the code follows the later one (FIX-135), while the earlier comments, which describe the LTP outcome, were left in place; the summation itself, for any tick that carries a day figure, is the defect M-D1 records and holds. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (FIX-135's accumulation is a mechanism for the case); (b) holds only on the field's meaning (a per-tick sum of a running day total is never the interval's quantity), which for the legacy period is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (the vendor wording is current external context); (c) fails: the feed requests only MODE_LTP and its `set_mode` has no non-test caller (LF-11), so a tick with a volume cannot arise under the documented set-up; (d) holds (no source intends a sum of day figures). No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; which packets the server sent is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S121).
- **Owner:** A05 (the store and the dispatcher's value); A01 (the dispatcher in `main.py`); A11 (the volume-dependent trail, switched off); A18 (PB-01's choice).
