##### CA-02 — A tick joins the minute that is open when it is dispatched, not the minute in which it was received; the `ts` argument, `window_start` and `tick_count` are carried but never used

- **Area and flow:** tick intake: `on_tick`; which candle a tick belongs to.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a stated windowing basis that the code does not use); Weakness (fields written and never read).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) LF12, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2531 to L2535 (17-Apr-2026): the timer closes "ALL symbols with any accumulated LTP in window"; "candle close must NOT depend on next tick arrival". (2) FIX-080, `data/candle_store.py` L237 to L238 (16-May-2026, `39110d8`): "candle close timing must match market time". (3) H-9, `on_tick` docstring L182 to L185 and `update` docstring L53 to L55 (06-Jul-2026, `beb8278`): "Candle windows are clock-driven (the timer thread closes on `now_ist()` boundaries), so `ts` (the received/exchange tick time) is retained for the caller interface but is INFORMATIONAL ONLY"; "window assignment is clock-driven upstream"; commit `beb8278`: "Receive-time (clock-driven) windowing has been correct since go-live" (its "correct since go-live" is a runtime claim, UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED). (4) FIX-049, L47 (15-May-2026, `cff70b0`): `window_start` is the "minute boundary for this accumulator". HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) and (2) IS-35, (3) IS-41, the code-only contradiction CD-13; (4) the FIX-049 field comment IS-148.
- **Actual code path:** `data/candle_store.py` L187 to L195 (`on_tick` under the lock; `window_start = now_ist()...` stored, never compared), L62, L269 to L271 (the close's snapshot); `main.py` L2964 to L2968 (the dispatcher on the feed's consumer thread); `data/live_feed.py` L270 (the per-batch `_last_tick_at` stamp the watchdog reads; the feed adds no receipt time to the tick itself, P01-S042).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `on_tick` ignores `ts`; it writes `window_start` and increments `tick_count`, and nothing reads either, and neither reaches `CandleData`. INTERPRETATION (code reading): a tick belongs to the first close whose snapshot takes the lock after the tick's `on_tick` call; so membership follows the dispatch (processing) time on the consumer thread, not the exchange time or the time the feed received the tick; a tick received before a boundary but still in the feed's queue joins the next candle. MATCH part: membership is clock-driven and not tied to the next tick's arrival, as LF12 and H-9 state, and no exchange time is used, as H-9 decided.
- **Evidence:** P03-S016, P03-S029 to P03-S032; P01-S042, P01-S047; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. H-9's record names the basis "receive-time"; the code uses dispatch time, which differs whenever a tick waits in the feed's queue across a boundary; the dead `window_start` and `tick_count` fields are a weakness the code shows (values kept that nothing reads). KNOWN DEFECT not considered (the difference arises only with a queue backlog at a boundary; no mechanism fails by semantics).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established. No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 10; P03 point 3; STUDY question 4 (the feed's question on placing a tick in a minute; LF-12); CA-03.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CA-03 — The close does not check that the boundary was reached or which boundary it last closed: a long close can merge two minutes under one stamp, and an early wake can split one

- **Area and flow:** the clock: `_timer_loop` and the start of `_close_candles`.
- **Issue category:** Logic and calculation (interval boundaries); Flow and state (an interval processed twice or skipped).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** LF12, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2531 to L2532 (17-Apr-2026): the timer "fires at candle_interval_sec wall-clock boundaries (clock-aligned via time.time() // interval arithmetic)"; FIX-080, `data/candle_store.py` L234 to L240 (16-May-2026, `39110d8`): "Clock-aligned candle close. Fires at candle_interval_sec boundaries"; "candle close timing must match market time"; the H-9 comment L263 to L265 (06-Jul-2026): "the timer fires AT the boundary (e.g. 10:01:00) to close the window that just ENDED (10:00)". HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): IS-35 (LF12, FIX-080), IS-41 (H-9); CD-13.
- **Actual code path:** `data/candle_store.py` L241 to L251, L262 to L278.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT and INTERPRETATION, arithmetic): the next boundary is recomputed from `time.time()` before every wait, the `max(0.0, ...)` guard prevents a negative wait (FIX-080), drift does not accumulate, and with the 60 s interval the boundaries fall on IST minute boundaries. ISSUE part (FACT): when the wait ends the close runs without testing that the boundary has been reached, and `_last_closed_window` is derived from the close's own clock reading and compared with nothing. INTERPRETATION: a close (with its callbacks, among them one insert per candle) that takes longer than the interval skips the next boundary, so the following candle covers more than one minute under one stamp; a wake before the boundary (for example a wall-clock step back during a monotonic wait) runs one close early and another at the boundary, splitting a minute. Which clock `Event.wait` uses depends on the Python build; the production version and clock behaviour are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (A01 B-41).
- **Evidence:** P03-S035 to P03-S038, P03-S049, P03-S122; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. FIX-080's stated aim ("must match market time") is not secured against a late, skipped or early close; nothing detects it. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the clock-aligned wait is the mechanism); (b) fails (the outcome needs an overrun or a clock step, timing-dependent, not certain by semantics); (c) and (d) not reached.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether any such close occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S122).
- **Owner:** A05; A17 (the host clock and the Python build).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 10; P03 point 4; CA-04, CA-07, CA-08.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CA-04 — The live `ts` is a close-time stamp, as LF13, the store's field comment and the schema comment intend, but it is the moment the close ran (seconds and microseconds kept), and it shares one field and one key with candles whose `ts` other sources read as the minute's start (A03 Y-06's A05 side)

- **Area and flow:** the meaning of a live candle's time: `CandleData.ts`, the `ts` the live writer hands to `insert_candle`, and readers of stored rows.
- **Issue category:** Consistency (one field, different meanings across sources and writers); Logic and calculation (time stamp).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) LF13, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2544, L2548 (17-Apr-2026): "ts: datetime (IST, tz-aware)", "ts is IST tz-aware for consistency"; `data/candle_store.py` L34 (17-Apr-2026): "IST candle close time (from clock thread)". (2) `core/analytics_schema.sql` L28 (14-Jun-2026, `f172171`; A03's): "ISO-8601 IST candle close". (3) H-9, L263 to L265 (06-Jul-2026): the timer "fires AT the boundary (e.g. 10:01:00) to close the window that just ENDED (10:00)". (4) Against these, for other candle kinds: `resample` (`core/candle_math.py` L341 to L366, 12-Jul-2026): "the bar's `ts` = the bucket START"; the candle-math tests stamp candles at their start; the 24-Jul document reads stored one-minute labels as minute starts; the `Candle` type allows aware or naive IST (`sr_detector/models.py` L25, 27-Jun-2026; A08's). Neither side names the other. (5) A03 Y-06 and OIR A03-43: the stored form is A03's; Rama's Phase 2 input: one canonical, time-zone-defined stored form, chosen in Phase 2, "the meaning of the time stays A05's". HISTORICAL (1) to (4); project records (5). Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-36, (2) IS-106, (3) IS-41, (4) IS-54, IS-59, IS-60, IS-74; the conflict IC-13; (5) IS-135, RS-04; STUDY question 8 OPEN QUESTION (conflict) narrowed there.
- **Actual code path:** `data/candle_store.py` L262, L325, L343 (`close_ts = now_ist()` once per close, given to every candle); `core/time_authority.py` L58 to L59, L94 to L99; `main.py` L3933 (`candle.ts.isoformat()`); `core/state_store.py` L2030 to L2054 (A03's); readers `reports/daily_report.py` L165 to L175 and `reports/daily_trade_review.py` L2569 to L2574 (A15's; `ts[11:16]` keys).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT and INTERPRETATION): the live `ts` is aware IST at a fixed +05:30 from the host clock (LF13), set by the clock thread at the close (the store's comment), and marks the end of the interval covered, which agrees in meaning with the schema's "candle close"; the host's time-zone setting does not affect it. ISSUE part (INTERPRETATION): it is the host time read when the close starts, a little after the boundary, not the boundary itself that H-9's example names, and it keeps seconds and microseconds; written by `isoformat()` it is `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.ffffff+05:30`, the fraction omitted when it is exactly zero. The same field and the same text key hold the daily script's broker labels, which the current vendor examples and A18's arithmetic read as the minute's start (current external context, read 14-Nov-2026; the legacy-period convention UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; parts HA and DC). On that reading the two writers' rows for one market minute would differ by about one minute plus the close's delay, besides the text-form difference A03 Y-06 records, and two A15 reports that key rows by `ts[11:16]` would find a live-written row under the minute after the one it covers. No source states one meaning for live, broker and stored candles, or who handles a difference.
- **Evidence:** P03-S038, P03-S039, P03-S045, P03-S077, P03-S079, P03-S082, P03-S098, P03-S128; P08-S042; P10-S086, P10-S087; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The live stamp agrees with its own sources in meaning; the sources conflict across candle kinds (IC-13) and the code resolves the conflict differently per writer under one field and one key. This record is A05's side of A03 Y-06 (the meaning); Y-06's classification, owners and treatment, and A03-43's status, are unchanged, and no stored format is chosen here. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism fails by semantics; the outcome depends on a convention that is not established for the legacy period).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether any live-written row existed is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S120). No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05 (the meaning of the live `ts`); A03 (the stored form and key: Y-06, A03-43, unchanged); A15 (the reports' `ts[11:16]` keys); parts HA and DC (the broker labels).

##### CA-05 — Synthetic flat candles are made as FIX-020 intends, but they continue without limit and with no session check, stay in the 500-candle history until they can push out every real candle, and reach every consumer and the table unmarked except by `is_synthetic`, which no in-memory consumer reads

- **Area and flow:** the close: synthetic carry-forward candles; the history; the consumers.
- **Issue category:** Flow and state (stale data treated as current); Weakness (no bound on a carry-forward).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) FIX-020, `data/candle_store.py` L256 to L257, L280, L331 and L36 (13-May-2026, `b991054`): "If a token had no ticks this minute BUT has previous history, emit a synthetic flat candle: O=H=L=C=prev_close, V=0, is_synthetic=True"; commit `b991054` "flat candle carry-forward for illiquid symbols"; "Ensures SmartTgtManager SL trailing continues during silent periods". (2) LF12 L2533 to L2534 (17-Apr-2026): close "ALL symbols with any accumulated LTP in window"; "1 tick -> valid candle" (no synthetic candle described; FIX-020 is later and adds them). (3) LF14 L2555 and the store's comment L75 to L76 (both 17-Apr-2026): 390 against 500, "generous buffer beyond 1 full trading day". (4) The legacy record of the hazard, `docs/audit/tick_candle_dormancy_25jul2026.md` L265 to L272 (25-Jul-2026): synthetic candles are "sticky", one per token "every 60 s for the rest of the process", each fanning out to every callback, "including a `_persist_candle` DB insert", recorded as a hazard "a naive fix would hit". No source states a time or session limit. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-40 (CD-14), (2) IS-35, (3) IS-37 (IC-06), (4) IS-45; STUDY question 10 split there (limits OPEN QUESTION narrowed; the smart target's treatment answered by evidence at its date).
- **Actual code path:** `data/candle_store.py` L279 to L287, L331 to L347, L353 to L359 (history `deque(maxlen=500)`), L362 to L368; `main.py` L3927 to L3944 (`is_synthetic` stored); `orders/smart_tgt_manager.py` L804 to L823 (`get_candles(token, n=390)`; max `high` or min `low`); `orders/structure_exit_manager.py` (A11's; reads no `is_synthetic`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): a synthetic candle is made only for a mapped token with history and no tick in the interval, flat at the previous close with volume 0 and `is_synthetic` True, and it reaches the smart target's trail, as FIX-020 states. ISSUE part (FACT and INTERPRETATION): each synthetic candle is appended to the history, so the next one repeats the same price, every minute while the process runs, with no session or market-hours check in the store; the history keeps the last 500 candles of either kind, so after 500 silent minutes no real candle is left in memory; the smart target's reconnect recompute then takes its best price from flat candles only (A11's use); no in-memory consumer reads `is_synthetic` and the live writer stores each synthetic candle as a row. The code itself thus shows a carry-forward that, with no bound, replaces the real history it was meant to bridge.
- **Evidence:** P03-S040, P03-S041, P03-S047, P03-S089, P03-S094, P03-S103, P03-S107, P03-S115; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Intent on the limits is not established; the ISSUE part rests on the weakness the code demonstrates (stale data passed on as current and pushing out real history) and on the legacy record that names it a hazard. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism for a limit exists; condition (a) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; how long the process ran each day, and so whether after-hours or 500-minute synthetic runs arose, is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S124). No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05 (the store); A11 (the consumers' treatment of synthetic candles); A01 (the process's daily run length).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 11; P03 point 5; STUDY question 10; CA-06, CA-11, CA-14 (390 against 500).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 10 bears on the intended limits).

##### CA-06 — The synthetic-only guard meant to make "a starved/misconfigured feed" loud stays silent for a feed that never delivers a tick, and for a silent token while any other token ticks

- **Area and flow:** the close: the H-9 synthetic-only counter and its WARNING.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (an alarm that cannot fire for part of its stated case).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** H-9, `data/candle_store.py` L98 to L100 and L289 to L293 (06-Jul-2026, `beb8278`): "surfaces a starved feed instead of letting the store silently drift 100% synthetic"; "the feed may be starved/misconfigured. Make it LOUD instead of letting the store drift 100% synthetic unnoticed (the state the late-tick landmine would have caused)"; commit `beb8278`: "so a starved/misconfigured feed is LOUD instead of silently synthetic". The DORMANCY record (L153 to L157, 25-Jul-2026) calls the guard "structurally blind to *never-started*", and expects (L273 to L274) "a burst of "ZERO real ticks" warnings for every subscribed-but-quiet illiquid token". The 25-Jul decision makes a feed with nothing subscribed the intended state (`docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L82). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): H-9 IS-41 (CD-14); DORMANCY IS-45; the decision IS-20.
- **Actual code path:** `data/candle_store.py` L281 to L308.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the counter rises only at a close with no real candle and at least one synthetic candidate, resets at any close with a real candle, and logs one WARNING for the whole store at the third such close and every multiple of 30. INTERPRETATION: with no tick since the start no token has history, so there is no candidate and no warning; a token that falls silent while others tick never raises the counter; the warning is one line per qualifying close, not one per token (so the DORMANCY expectation of a per-token burst does not match the code). The landmine state the comment names (every tick discarded) would, from a process start, also leave no history and so no warning. MATCH part: once some candles were real and then every token falls silent, the guard warns as stated.
- **Evidence:** P03-S042, P03-S043, P03-S103, P03-S126; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the counter is the mechanism); (b) holds for a never-started feed (no history, so the branch cannot be reached, by code logic); (c) holds in part (a never-started feed is the boot state under the documented set-up, and "starved/misconfigured" can cover it); (d) fails: under the 25-Jul decision (IS-20) a feed with nothing subscribed is the intended state, so silence in that state does not contradict every intent source.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether the WARNING was ever logged is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S123). No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05; A15 (telemetry and reports that might watch it).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 11; P03 point 6; LF-09 (the feed's tick-age alarm, armed only after a first tick); CA-05.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CA-07 — The candle timer thread has no exception guard, health check or restart; if it ended, ticks would keep accumulating with no close and no signal

- **Area and flow:** the timer thread's lifetime: `_timer_loop`, `_close_candles`, callbacks on the same thread.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (no safe recovery; no alert).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** LF10 and LF12, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2510, L2531 (17-Apr-2026): "start() begins clock timer thread"; "Internal timer thread fires at candle_interval_sec wall-clock boundaries"; LF17 (L2583 to L2587): callbacks outside the lock. No source states a guard, health check or restart for this thread (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`'s point-12 row). For comparison only, the feed's consumer is declared "IMMORTAL" and has a health check (FIX-029, `data/live_feed.py` L532 to L535, L628 to L631; lines of 14-May-2026, `5dc8563`), a practice of the same codebase stated for another thread. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): IS-30, IS-35, IS-39; the FIX-029 lines are IS-139 (the `_consume_ticks` docstring, L527 to L535) and IS-14 (the `_check_consumer_health` docstring, L627 to L634).
- **Actual code path:** `data/candle_store.py` L110 to L118, L233 to L251 (no `try`), L300 to L308 and L368 (logger calls outside any `try`), L362 to L368 (`except Exception` per callback); no code checks or restarts `_timer_thread` (P03-S023).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the loop has no `try` and no supervisor. INTERPRETATION (Python semantics): an exception raised in a close outside the per-callback `try` (in practice only from the two logger calls, or on resource exhaustion), or a `BaseException` from a callback, ends the thread for the rest of the process; `on_tick` keeps adding ticks to accumulators that are never closed, and no candle, synthetic or real, is emitted or stored; nothing reports it. A slow callback delays every later callback, candle and close (CA-03).
- **Evidence:** P03-S021, P03-S023, P03-S049, P03-S050, P03-S061, P03-S104; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Intent is not established; the weakness is one the code demonstrates (failure handling: a failure that is neither recovered nor reported), with only narrow raising paths. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no recovery mechanism exists; condition (a) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether the timer thread ever died is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S123).
- **Owner:** A05; A15 (health signals).

##### CA-08 — Failures of the close callbacks and of the live writer are nearly invisible: callback errors without a traceback, insert errors at DEBUG, and rows skipped by `OR IGNORE` not logged at all (A03 Q-16's A05 side)

- **Area and flow:** close callbacks on the timer thread; the live writer `_persist_candle` and `insert_candle`.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (errors only logged, at a level below notice; failures not reported).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** no source states the logging level or reporting of a failing close callback or candle insert; LF13 and LF17 (17-Apr-2026) require only that callbacks run outside the lock; the structure-exit callback's own docstring (`orders/structure_exit_manager.py` L176; A11's) says "NEVER raises (would break sibling cbs)". A03 Q-16 (ISSUE; TG4, AGREED 11-Nov-2026) records that `INSERT OR IGNORE` reports a NOT NULL or CHECK violation exactly like a duplicate. Intent: not established. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): IS-39 (LF17); Q-16 IS-135; the A11 docstring IS-152.
- **Actual code path:** `data/candle_store.py` L362 to L368 (`self._log.error("CandleStore: callback raised: %s" % exc)`, no `exc_info`); `main.py` L3929 to L3943 (`_log.debug("candle persist failed: %s", exc)`); `core/state_store.py` L2030 to L2054 (`INSERT OR IGNORE`, outcome not checked; A03's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: a raising callback is logged at ERROR with the exception text only and skipped; a raising insert is logged at DEBUG only; a row skipped by `OR IGNORE` raises nothing and is logged nowhere. INTERPRETATION: for store-built candles every NOT NULL column receives a value, so the `OR IGNORE` masking acts on this writer only for a repeated `ts` text (two closes reading the same clock value) or a None symbol from the cache; at the usual INFO level a failing writer leaves no trace. The insert runs synchronously on the timer thread, one per candle per close.
- **Evidence:** P03-S048, P03-S069, P03-S076, P03-S078, P03-S083, P03-S100; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION. With intent not established, the weakness is one the code demonstrates (failures swallowed at DEBUG or unlogged, Document 04, section 5.3); the `OR IGNORE` part is A03 Q-16's, counted there and not again here; this record counts the A05 side (the DEBUG level and the callback logging). KNOWN DEFECT not considered.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether any callback or insert failed is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S123).
- **Owner:** A05 (the store's callback loop); A01 (the `_persist_candle` closure in `main.py`); A03 (`insert_candle`, Q-16 unchanged); A15 (log levels and reports).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 12; P03 point 8; related A03 Q-16; CA-09.
- **Rama:** not needed.

INTERPRETATION: the writer is "dark" only while the store emits nothing; once any token ticks (only the exit-retry path can subscribe one, LF-17), it writes that token's real candles and then a synthetic row every minute for the rest of the process (CA-05), into the same table as the daily script. ##### CA-10 — `stop()` says "Stop timer thread cleanly" but only sets an event: it neither joins the thread nor closes the open candles, and the shutdown then closes only the main thread's store connection (A01 C14's store side)

- **Area and flow:** shutdown: `candle_store.stop()` in `_shutdown`, the timer thread and the live writer.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a stated clean stop that the code does not ensure); Flow and state (work in progress during shutdown).

No source states whether the candles being built are to be emitted at a stop (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`'s point-12 row). A01 C14 (ISSUE; Group B, AGREED 06-Nov-2026) records the late signal handlers and the threads not joined. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): LF10 IS-30; the `stop()` docstring IS-149; C14 IS-137.
- **Actual code path:** `data/candle_store.py` L120 to L122, L241, L249 to L251; `main.py` L1739, L1753 (A11 managers stopped early), L1788 (feed), L1791 to L1794 (`candle_store.stop()`), L1827 to L1830 (`store.close()`); `core/state_store.py` per-thread connections (A03's; interface only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `stop()` sets `_stop_event`; a waiting loop then ends without a close, so the candles being built are dropped (apart from one narrow case, P03-S022); there is no `join`. INTERPRETATION: a close already running when `stop()` is called continues, with its callbacks and inserts, while `_shutdown` goes on; `store.close()` closes only the calling thread's connection, so an insert on the timer thread is not stopped by it; the daemon thread may still be inside a close or an insert when the interpreter exits. MATCH part: in the usual case (the loop waiting) the thread ends at once and no further close runs.
- **Evidence:** P03-S022, P03-S073, P03-S074, P03-S096, P03-S108; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. "Cleanly" is not ensured for a close in progress; the dropped open candles have no stated intent either way and are recorded, not classified. C14's classification, owners and treatment are A01's and unchanged; this record counts only the A05 side (the store's `stop()`). KNOWN DEFECT not considered (timing-dependent).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; at the usual shutdown after the session the open candles would be synthetic or empty (INTERPRETATION); runtime UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05 (`stop()`); A01 (the shutdown order, C14 unchanged); A03 (the connection model).

##### CA-11 — Reconnect: the open candles are discarded at the first attempt, as LF7, LF16 and G6 intend; the gap minutes become synthetic candles and nothing is backfilled, against G6's "Backfill the cache via REST historical API for missed minutes"; the next real candle may be partial and is not marked

- **Area and flow:** the reconnect chain on the store's side: `mark_reconnect`, the following closes, the smart target's recompute.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (sources that conflict, resolved one way); Flow and state (a partial interval passed on unmarked).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) G6, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L126 (17-Apr-2026): "On gap detected: discard partially-built candles. Backfill the cache via REST historical API for missed minutes (don't re-fire smart_tgt callbacks for those bars). Recompute trail SL once on reconnect from current candle history."; the rationale: "the cache needs the data so future calculations have correct context". (2) LF7 L2477 to L2485 (same date): notify the store "On _on_reconnect (first attempt per disconnect)", "Called ONCE per disconnect event". (3) LF16 L2571 to L2576: `mark_reconnect` "Discards ALL partially-built (unclosed) candles", "Closed candles in _history are preserved", "Logs WARNING with count"; "G6: partial candles during gap are unreliable". (4) ST13, `orders/smart_tgt_manager.py` L35 to L37 (A11's): `on_reconnect()` "must be called AFTER candle_store.mark_reconnect()". All of one commit; LF7 calls itself the "G6 reconnect protocol"; no source names synthetic gap candles. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-09 and IS-47, (2) IS-08, (3) IS-38; the conflict IC-02; (4) ST13 IS-153; STUDY question 11 split there (discard and timing answered by evidence at their date; gap filling in conflict).
- **Actual code path:** `data/candle_store.py` L219 to L227, L187 to L195, L281 to L287; `main.py` L2991 to L2994; `data/live_feed.py` L378 to L414 (part LF); `orders/smart_tgt_manager.py` L334 to L352, L789 to L828 (A11's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): `mark_reconnect` clears the open accumulators under the lock, keeps history, map and callbacks, and logs one WARNING with the count (LF16); the feed calls it once per disconnect at the first attempt (LF7; LF-25) and `main.py` calls `smart_tgt.on_reconnect` after it (ST13); the smart target recomputes once from history without re-firing callbacks (G6's last two parts, A11's). ISSUE part (FACT and INTERPRETATION): the timer keeps closing while the feed is down, so each gap minute gives every mapped token with history a synthetic candle; no code fetches the missed minutes; after the call a token's next candle covers only the time after the call, with nothing marking it partial; the smart target's recompute reads up to 390 candles that include those synthetic ones. On G6's own rationale the cache then lacks the correct context.
- **Evidence:** P03-S055 to P03-S057, P03-S067, P03-S089, P03-S090, P03-S105, P03-S108; P01-S063; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The sources of one commit differ on the gap minutes (IC-02) and the code follows LF7 and LF16, with FIX-020's synthetic candles (later) filling the gap in place of G6's backfill. The missing `try` around the reconnect callback is LF-08's (related A01 C37 (g)); `mark_reconnect` itself has no raising path apart from its logger call, so C37 (g) does not arise from the store. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no backfill mechanism exists; condition (a) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether `mark_reconnect` was ever called is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S123). No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05 (the store); A11 (the smart target's recompute); A01 (the reconnect lambda).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 13; P03 point 11; STUDY question 11; LF-25, LF-08; CA-05.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 11 bears on the intended gap filling).

##### CA-12 — The store's token map is the boot copy for the whole process; `gc_sweep`, "Called on InstrumentsRefreshed event", has no caller, and the event carries no set of tokens for it (A01 C29's store side)

- **Area and flow:** the token-to-symbol map and the FIX-092 clean-up chain on the store's side.

Removes accumulators and history for tokens not in valid_tokens set. Called on InstrumentsRefreshed event to clean up delisted symbols."; `core/instrument_cache.py` `reload` and `core/events.py` L223 to L232 (FIX-092; part IN's). A01 C29 (ISSUE; Group I, AGREED 06-Nov-2026; part (f) counted in A01 M-07) records the unwired chain. HISTORICAL. - **Actual code path:** `data/candle_store.py` L128 to L131, L133 to L168, L279 to L287; `main.py` L3945 to L3946 (the only `set_token_map` call); `core/instrument_cache.py` L188 to L215, L287 to L293; `core/events.py` L223 to L234.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `gc_sweep` has no non-test caller; nothing subscribes to `InstrumentsRefreshed`; the event's own field is `token_count`, while `gc_sweep` needs the set of valid tokens; `gc_sweep` would not change the token map; the map is set once at boot from every cached instrument. INTERPRETATION: during a process the map never changes and the set of tokens with history only grows; a token added by a later refresh would get `str(token)` as its symbol, and one removed keeps getting synthetic candles (if it has history). As written, the docstring's trigger could not drive `gc_sweep` even if subscribed, without another source for the tokens. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (`gc_sweep` is a mechanism for the case); (b) holds only as far as the event carries no tokens; (c) fails (`reload` has no caller, so an in-process refresh cannot arise under the documented set-up); (d) not reached.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none (no caller); the order of the day's refresh and the process's start is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (part IN).
- **Owner:** A05 (`gc_sweep`, the store's map); A01 (C29, unchanged); part IN (`reload`, the event's publisher).

- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CM-01 — The module header and two legacy documents describe an earlier, unwired two-helper module; the header's "single candle type" statement does not hold

- **Area and flow:** the module's own description (header L1 to L31) and the legacy documents that describe it, against the module as it stands and its callers.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented or stated behaviour that the code does not implement; implemented behaviour that nothing describes).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) the header, `core/candle_math.py` L3 to L7 (purpose: "Two genuinely-new helpers ... (1) True Range series + ATR ... (2) Timeframe RESAMPLE"), L17 to L20 (inputs and outputs of `atr`, `true_range_series` and `resample` only), L28 to L31 ("Wiring : NONE yet (V3 Step 1 is build + unit-test only). Nothing in the live trading / scoring / sizing / S&R path consumes these functions"), and L9 to L11 ("the SHARED OHLCV `Candle` value object (sr_detector.models.Candle — the single candle type in the codebase; NO parallel type is defined here)"); lines of 2026-07-12 (`536172c`), unchanged since. (2) `PATHS.md` L211 (entry dated 11-Jul-2026): "BUILT (unwired, built-but-unpushed)", "NOT wired into any trading path", "Tests `tests/unit/test_candle_math.py` (26 pass)"; `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L219 to L224: "**Wiring: NONE yet.**", "31 = 26 + 5 `session_vwap`" (lines 2026-07-12). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-49, (2) IS-56; the code-only contradiction CD-15.
- **Actual code path:** `core/candle_math.py` L54 to L456 (eight public functions); the six importing lines `core/daily_stats.py` L21, `regime/engine.py` L26 to L28, `sr_detector/zone_builder.py` L16, `v3_chain/pb01_entry.py` L39, `v3_chain/pb01_runner.py` L31, `v3_chain/runner.py` L27; `data/candle_store.py` L24 to L36 (`CandleData`, line L25 of 2026-04-17, `bcf03b5`); `tests/unit/test_candle_math.py` (46 test functions).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the file defines eight public functions (`true_range_series`, `atr`, `session_vwap`, `ema_series`, `ema`, `adx`, `rsi`, `resample`); the header's purpose, inputs and outputs lines name three of them. FACT: six non-test modules import the module and call it at fourteen sites (P04-S068). Two of the imports (`regime/engine.py`, `sr_detector/zone_builder.py`) came in the same commit that created the header (`536172c`, 2026-07-12); the others followed on 12 and 13 Jul 2026 (`474e10b`, `5a71fb6`, `6bca493`); no later commit changed the header (P04-S010). So "Wiring : NONE yet" did not describe the code even on the day it was written. FACT: the test file has 46 test functions covering all eight functions, not 26 or 31 (P04-S081, P04-S084). FACT: `data/candle_store.py` defines a second candle record, `CandleData`, with the same six OHLCV and `ts` attribute names plus four more (P04-S015), dated before the module; "the single candle type in the codebase" does not hold, although no non-test caller passes `CandleData` to this module (P04-S066; CM-21). The rest of the header (no parallel type defined in the module; purity; injected session bounds) matches (CM-12, CM-13).
- **Evidence:** P04-S007 to P04-S010, P04-S015, P04-S066, P04-S068, P04-S081, P04-S084; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL. The header and both documents state a wiring state, a function list, a test count and a type uniqueness that the code at the baseline does not have. The difference is in the description only; no behaviour depends on it. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism).
- **Severity or impact where established:** documentation only.
- **Owner:** A05 (the header); the legacy documents' A05 side here, the documents as a whole with part XC.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 36; P04 points 1 and 18; CD-15; CM-22 (the documents that do agree with the code).

##### CM-02 — `adx`'s docstring and the system map say it reuses `true_range_series`; it computes the same true range inline

- **Area and flow:** `adx`, the true-range step.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Consistency across components (duplicated logic).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `core/candle_math.py` L194 to L195 (`adx` docstring, 2026-07-12, `536172c`): "Uses true_range_series for TR (reuse, no duplication)"; `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L244 (2026-07-12): "`adx` (Wilder, reuses `true_range_series`; ...)". HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): IS-51; CD-17.
- **Actual code path:** `core/candle_math.py` L207 to L217 (inline true range), L76 to L77 (`true_range_series`), L117 (the only call of `true_range_series` in the module, inside `atr`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `adx` does not call `true_range_series`; it computes `max(high - low, |high - prev close|, |low - prev close|)` inline for each candle after the first. INTERPRETATION (comparison of L77 with L213 to L217): the inline expression is the same formula, so `adx`'s true ranges equal `true_range_series(candles)[1:]`; there is no difference in the values, only in the reuse claim. The true range is thus written twice in the module, and nowhere else in non-test code (P04-S089, P04-S090). The existing technical record already notes the inline form (P04-S096).
- **Evidence:** P04-S034, P04-S035, P04-S085, P04-S090; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. A documented "reuse, no duplication" that the code does not implement; no effect on the computed values. KNOWN DEFECT not considered.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none on values; a maintenance point (two copies of one formula).
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 36; P04 point 2; CD-17.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CM-03 — `DAILY_TF` is defined and never used; `resample` tests a literal tuple instead

- **Area and flow:** `resample`, the daily-timeframe test.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (code defined but never used); Consistency across components (the same value held as a constant and as a literal).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `core/candle_math.py` L297 (comment, 2026-07-12, `536172c`): "Accepted string value of `target_tf` for a one-bar-per-session-day resample", above `DAILY_TF = "day"` (L298); the `resample` docstring L344: "``"day"``/``"daily"`` -> one bar per session date" (IS-54). HISTORICAL. The L297 comment is IS-158.
- **Actual code path:** `core/candle_math.py` L298, L378.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `DAILY_TF` is not used anywhere after its definition, tests included (whole-word search, P04-S016); `resample` tests `target_tf.strip().lower() in ("day", "daily")` (L378). The accepted values match the docstring ("day" and "daily", in any case and with surrounding spaces). Noted, not a separate basis: INTERPRETATION (arithmetic, P04-S038): the check `len(dxs) < period` in `adx` (L238 to L239) cannot be true when it is reached, because L201 already requires `2 × period + 1` candles; it is harmless.
- **Evidence:** P04-S016, P04-S038, P04-S044; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The behaviour matches the docstring; the basis is the weakness the code itself shows (a constant meant to define the accepted value, unused, with the value repeated as a literal). KNOWN DEFECT not considered.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none on behaviour.
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** P04 point 3.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CM-04 — Period defaults: `atr` and the EMA have none "baked in", while `adx` and `rsi` default to 14 in the module, and the periods of calls 1 and 2 are code literals with no setting

- **Area and flow:** where each indicator's period comes from: the module's signatures and the callers' wiring.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (hard-coded values); Intended against actual (a stated convention the code does not follow throughout).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `atr` docstring, `core/candle_math.py` L92 to L93 (2026-07-12, `536172c`): "`period` is a caller-supplied PARAMETER — no default period is baked in here; any default belongs in config, at the wiring site"; `ema_series` docstring L169: "`period` is a PARAMETER (no baked default)". (2) The header L30 to L31 (IS-49): later modules "wire them in and own the config defaults". (3) `docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md` 1.4 ("No hardcoded ... settings") and 1.9 ("System behavior must be fully controlled via configuration"), lines of 2026-04-17 (`bcf03b5`). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-155 (`atr`) and IS-156 (`ema_series`); (2) IS-49; (3) IS-207.
- **Actual code path:** `core/candle_math.py` L83 to L87 (`atr`, no default), L165 and L184 (EMA, no default), L191 (`adx`, `period: int = 14`), L250 (`rsi`, `period: int = 14`, line of 2026-07-13, `3c85512`); `core/daily_stats.py` L38 to L39 (`atr_period: int = 14`, `rsi_period: int = 14`, 2026-07-13, `6bca493`), L72 to L73; `scripts/forward_shadow_record.py` L213 (`compute_daily_stats(dc, date_iso)`, no periods passed).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `adx` and `rsi` carry a default period of 14 in the module, against the convention stated for `atr` and the EMA. FACT: no non-test caller relies on those module defaults: the regime engine passes `self._adx_period` (calls 5 and 9) and `core/daily_stats.py` passes `rsi_period` (call 2) (P04-S068). FACT: calls 1 and 2 take their periods from the keyword defaults of `compute_daily_stats` (14 and 14), and their only non-test caller passes none, so these two periods are code literals with no configuration key (P04-S068, P04-S069). MATCH part: the regime and V3 chain calls take their periods from their settings (CM-23).
- **Evidence:** P04-S016, P04-S068, P04-S069, P04-S072, P04-S075; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor; weakness: hard-coded values), with a MATCH part (CM-23). FACT; HISTORICAL. The module's `adx` and `rsi` defaults are latent (unused by non-test code); the literal periods of calls 1 and 2 are the committed code values. KNOWN DEFECT not considered.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether the forward-shadow script ran is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (CM-11).
- **Owner:** A05 (the module defaults); A15 (`core/daily_stats.py`'s literals); A18 (the script's call).
- **Cross-reference:** P04 point 4; CM-23.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CM-05 — UNKNOWN: whether the candles reached the functions oldest first, without duplicates; the module relies on its callers for order, as it states, and no step in the fetch chain sorts the rows

- **Area and flow:** the input contract of every function: order, duplicates and gaps, from the broker's rows through `Candle.from_kite` and the callers to the module.
- **Issue category:** Logic and calculation (steps in the wrong order, if the input is out of order); Weakness (missing validation).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** the header L17 (IS-49): "Inputs : Sequences of Candle (chronologically ordered)"; `true_range_series` docstring L66 to L67: "processed in the given order and never re-sorted (caller supplies chronological order)"; `resample` docstring L368 to L370: "assumed chronologically ordered ... order is preserved, not re-sorted" (IS-50; lines 2026-07-12). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): IS-49, IS-50; CD-16. No source states whether the fetch layer or the broker's row order was meant to provide the order, or how duplicates and gaps were to be handled by these functions (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`, question 17 row).
- **Actual code path:** `core/candle_math.py` L71 to L79, L114 to L129, L207 to L217, L271 to L285, L173 to L180, L393 to L428 (no sort, no order or duplicate check); `sr_detector/fetch.py` L122 to L126 and `scripts/forward_shadow_record.py` L212 (no sort; A08's and A18's, boundary); `regime/engine.py` L117 to L118, L251 (call 9).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT, module side (MATCH part): no function sorts its input; each processes it in the given order, as the three sources state; `resample` sorts only its output bars (L428) (P04-S059). INTERPRETATION: every result depends on that order (the previous close, the Wilder and EMA recursions, `resample`'s open and close), so an out-of-order input would give different values without an error; a duplicated candle enters every recursion twice and `resample`'s volume twice; a missing bar, session break or day break is not detected, because only `resample` reads time (P04-S060). FACT, the chain: neither the published library, nor the fetcher, nor the forward-shadow script sorts the rows or removes duplicates (P04-S067; P02-S065). FACT, current external context (read 14-Nov-2026, P04-S067): the vendor's historical-data example lists records in ascending time and states no ordering rule. FACT (call 9): the regime engine passes its whole fetched intraday window to `adx`, with no date or session filter; with `intraday_lookback_days` 1 (committed value; effective value UNKNOWN) the window is the last 24 hours and can span a session break, which `adx` treats as consecutive bars (P04-S071, P04-S103); what that means for the day-type axis is A18's.
- **Evidence:** P04-S059, P04-S060, P04-S067, P04-S071, P04-S098, P04-S103; P02-S065; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, with a MATCH part (the module does what IS-49 and IS-50 say). FACT; INTERPRETATION; UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. Whether any input reached the module out of order or with duplicates depends on what the broker's server sent in the legacy period, which is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P04-S098). The code demonstrates no failure for an ordered, unique input, which is the input its sources assume. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no failing mechanism is shown).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established.
- **Owner:** A05 (the module's contract); A08 (the fetcher, which passes the rows as received); A18 (the regime's intraday window).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 15; P04 points 6 and 12; STUDY 18.2 item 5; STUDY question 17; CD-16.
- **Rama:** not needed for the A05 module side; STUDY question 17 stays OPEN FOR RAMA for the fetch side (A08's).

##### CM-06 — The same indicator and period can take different values on different paths, because the Wilder recursion depends on where each caller's window starts

- **Area and flow:** the Wilder ATR (also ADX and RSI) and the EMA, as called by the PB-01 entry stage and the V3 chain runner on the same instrument and interval.
- **Issue category:** Logic and calculation (the same quantity calculated differently in different modules); Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** the header L13 (IS-49): "deterministic and identical in paper and live by construction"; `atr` docstring L105 (2026-07-12): "Pure and deterministic: same candles + period + method -> same output" (IS-155); L96 to L98: the Wilder seed is "mean of the first ``period`` prev-close true ranges". HISTORICAL. No source states whether one indicator and period must agree across paths that fetch different history (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`, question 15 row: "OPEN QUESTION (no source found)").
- **Actual code path:** `core/candle_math.py` L125 to L129 (Wilder ATR), L176 to L180 (EMA), L240 to L242 (ADX), L280 to L285 (RSI); `v3_chain/pb01_entry.py` L279 to L283 (call 11: `atr(c30, atr30_period)` over 10 days of 30-minute candles, truncated to the session open); `v3_chain/runner.py` L199 (call 13: `atr(c30, atr30_period)` over the fetcher's 180-day window, truncated to the signal's `as_of`); `main.py` L3680, L3730 (the 180-day lookbacks, committed default).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the module is deterministic for a given input, as stated (P04-S025). FACT: calls 11 and 13 compute the same quantity, the Wilder ATR with `atr30_period` (14, committed value; effective value UNKNOWN) of one symbol's 30-minute candles, over windows of 10 and 180 days (P04-S103). INTERPRETATION (the recursion, P04-S025): for a period of 2 or more the result depends on the first candles of the window through the seed, so the two calls give different values for the same bar; and because each window ends at the call time, its start moves from call to call. INTERPRETATION (arithmetic, T1): with a period of 14, the seed keeps a weight of (13/14)^m after m later true ranges, for example 0.00938 after 63 and 0.00137 after 89, so for windows of these lengths the difference is small but not zero. The EMA calls 12 and 14 use the same 180-day default window on both paths; the other ATR, ADX and RSI calls are on different instruments or intervals and are not the same quantity.
- **Evidence:** P04-S025, P04-S032, P04-S039, P04-S043, P04-S068, P04-S075, P04-S076, P04-S103; T1; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Intent is not established; the basis is the weakness the code itself shows, a kind Document 04, section 5.3 lists: one quantity computed in two modules takes two values. The docstrings document the seed but not the dependence on the window's start. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism fails; the values follow from the inputs chosen).
- **Severity or impact where established:** small in size for the committed windows (T1); both values feed gates recorded as "LOG-ONLY — recorded, never enforced" in the V3 runner (`v3_chain/runner.py` L207, HISTORICAL), and call 11's value is also used inside the PB-01 entry stage (P04-S086; A18's). Whether either path ran is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (CM-11).
- **Owner:** A05 (the module's window dependence); A18 (the windows and the use).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 15; P04 point 7; STUDY question 15.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 15 bears on treatment.

##### CM-07 — UNKNOWN: two different values are called VWAP, and no source says which one the system meant

- **Area and flow:** the volume-weighted average price: `session_vwap` (used only by the S&R anchors, call 10) and the broker quote's `average_price` (passed by the secondary screener as `"vwap"`).
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (two values under one name); Intended against actual (a conflict between sources).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) SS7, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L3006 to L3014, and SE8 L2925 to L2933 (2026-04-17, A07's): the screening `market_data` keys include `vwap`, set "to None (v2)", with "v2.1 will add data providers for these fields". (2) The `session_vwap` docstring, `core/candle_math.py` L136 to L146 (2026-07-12): a typical-price VWAP over candles the caller scopes, None when there is no volume. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-53, (2) IS-52; the conflict IC-08 (neither names the other).
- **Actual code path:** `core/candle_math.py` L136 to L158; `sr_detector/zone_builder.py` L160 to L164 (call 10; A08's); `screening/secondary_screener.py` L408 and `broker/zerodha_adapter.py` L1792 (A07's and A04's, boundary only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `session_vwap` returns Σ(typical price × volume) / Σ(volume) over candles with positive volume (P04-S027); it is called only by the S&R anchors, whose switch `sr_detector.intraday_anchors_enabled` is `false` in the tracked file (committed value; effective value UNKNOWN), and on the export script's route, which stops at L139 at the baseline (P04-S073, P04-S102). FACT: the secondary screener copies the quote record's `vwap`, taken from the quote's `average_price`, into its market data; no screening module imports this module (P04-S091). FACT, current external context (read 14-Nov-2026, P04-S091): the vendor's quotes page calls `average_price` "The volume weighted average price of a stock at a given time during the day" and does not say how it is computed. INTERPRETATION: a bar-based typical-price VWAP and the server's value need not agree. Nothing in the code compares the two.
- **Evidence:** P04-S027 to P04-S029, P04-S073, P04-S074, P04-S091, P04-S102; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL; OPEN QUESTION. The screener's use follows neither source (SS7 expects None until providers are added; `session_vwap` is not used there); whether that is the "v2.1" provider SS7 foresaw, and whether one VWAP was meant, is not established, and the two values feed different consumers, so the code shows no defect of its own. The basis used in CM-06 (one quantity computed in two modules takes two values) does not apply: only `session_vwap` is computed in the code; `average_price` is a value the broker's server supplies, and the two are taken over different scopes (the anchors' candidate date against the quote's current day), so whether they are meant to be one quantity is itself the open intent point. Rama's answer to STUDY question 16 decides between MATCH (two distinct values intended) and ISSUE (one VWAP intended); no source or code fact settles it without him. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: (a) fails (no mechanism reconciles or compares the two values).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether either path ran is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (CM-11).
- **Owner:** A05 (`session_vwap`); A07 (the screener's `vwap`); A08 (the anchors); A04 (the quote field).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 16; P04 point 16; STUDY question 16; IC-08.
- **Rama:** needed to classify (STUDY question 16): OPEN FOR RAMA.

##### CM-08 — `resample` reports slots not yet reached as gaps, does not check that it is given a lower timeframe, and does not check the one-zone convention it assumes (latent: no non-test caller)

- **Area and flow:** `resample`: the empty-slot report, the target timeframe against the base, and the time-zone convention of its inputs.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented meaning not kept in one case); Weakness (missing validation).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** the `ResampleResult` docstring, `core/candle_math.py` L320 to L321 (2026-07-12, `536172c`): "`empty_bucket_starts` — in-session grid slots that had NO base data (gaps). Reported, not fabricated." (IS-159); the `resample` docstring L340 ("Resample base-timeframe candles into a higher timeframe", inside IS-50, L335 to L371), L360 to L362 ("an in-session grid slot with no base candles is NOT emitted as a bar; its start is reported in ``empty_bucket_starts``", IS-54) and L368 to L370 ("assumed ... to share one tzinfo convention (aware IST, or naive IST — matching the Candle contract)", IS-50). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `core/candle_math.py` L378 to L383, L393 to L410, L428, L431 to L450.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: for each date with at least one kept candle, every slot of the whole session without a bucket is recorded in `empty_bucket_starts` (L446 to L450). INTERPRETATION (P04-S054): for a call made during a session, every slot after the last base candle is reported as a gap, although it has not yet been reached. FACT: there is no base-timeframe parameter, and the integer `target_tf` is checked only for being positive (L379 to L383). INTERPRETATION (P04-S046): a target smaller than the base spacing is accepted, and the slots between base bars are then reported as gaps; a target that is not a multiple of the base spacing gives buckets holding different numbers of base bars, unflagged. FACT and INTERPRETATION (P04-S056): no zone is converted; aware timestamps in another zone are bucketed on that zone's wall clock; kept candles that mix naive and aware timestamps make the sort at L428 raise `TypeError`; aware times from `session_bounds` make L400 raise; when the flag zone (taken at L395 to L396 from the first candle that has one, even an excluded one) differs from the kept candles' convention, every slot is reported empty while the bars are still emitted. MATCH part: the gap report for real gaps, the session-tail flag and the never-padded bars behave as documented (CM-19).
- **Evidence:** P04-S046, P04-S048, P04-S052, P04-S054, P04-S056, P04-S058; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent), with a MATCH part (CM-19). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The "(gaps)" meaning is not kept for a call made before the session's close, and the "higher timeframe" and one-zone assumptions are stated but not checked. Latent: `resample` has no non-test caller (P04-S058); under Rama's decision B that stays a code fact, and nothing is inferred from it about intent or production use. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (a gap report exists); (b) holds for the not-yet-reached slots (by the code, they are reported whenever the input of a date ends before its close); (c) not shown: no source says `resample` handles an input that ends during the session, and the case arises in no non-test code path at the baseline (a code fact under decision B, from which no intent is inferred); (d) fails: the outcome agrees with the literal definitions at L320 to L321 and L360 to L362 (slots "that had NO base data"; "an in-session grid slot with no base candles"), and only the word "(gaps)" differs, so it does not contradict every intent source found.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none (no non-test caller).
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 points 14 (`resample` side) and 17; P04 points 8, 9 and 10; CM-19.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CM-09 — `watchlist.entry_tf` and its schema comment say the retest timeframe is "built via candle_math.resample"; the code has no such build, and `resample` would reject the configured value "5minute" (A05 side of A02 K-26)

- **Area and flow:** the PB-01 retest timeframe: the configuration key, its two comments and `resample`'s accepted forms.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Consistency across components (a configured value form the module's contract does not accept).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `config/system_config.yaml` L628 (`entry_tf: "5minute"`, comment "retest timeframe (built via candle_math.resample)") and `core/config_loader.py` L1544 (`entry_tf: str = "5minute"`, comment "the retest timeframe (built via candle_math.resample)"); lines of 2026-07-13 (`5a71fb6`). HISTORICAL; committed value; effective value UNKNOWN. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): IS-55; CD-18; A02 K-26 IS-133; Rama's decision B IS-131.
- **Actual code path:** `core/candle_math.py` L378 to L383 (accepted `target_tf` forms); `v3_chain/pb01_entry.py` L44, L306 to L311 (the 5-minute candles fetched from the broker history, A18's; boundary).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: no non-test code reads `entry_tf` (A02 K-26's subject; P04-S080); the PB-01 entry stage fetches `"5minute"` candles directly with a literal; `resample` has no non-test caller (P04-S058). FACT: `resample` accepts as `target_tf` only a positive integer of minutes or the text "day" or "daily" (L378 to L383). INTERPRETATION (P04-S046): the configured value `"5minute"`, a broker interval name, would raise `ValueError` if it were passed to `resample` unchanged. Under Rama's decision B nothing is inferred from `resample` having no caller; the record rests on the two comments as the intent source.
- **Evidence:** P04-S046, P04-S058, P04-S079, P04-S080, P04-S095; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Related: A02 K-26 (ISSUE; owners A02 and A18; AGREED 09-Nov-2026, batch T5 group G1; "Rama: not needed"), whose ID, owners, classification, treatment and status are unchanged; this record is A05's side only: the comments name a module build that does not exist, in a value form the module's contract rejects. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: (a) fails (the code implements no build of the retest timeframe through `resample`, and nothing reads `entry_tf`).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none (no reader).
- **Owner:** A05 (`resample`'s accepted forms); A02 and A18 (K-26, unchanged).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 17; P04 point 11; CD-18; A02 K-26.
- **Rama:** not needed (A02 K-26 records "Rama: not needed"; the STUDY does not raise P04's draft question 2).

##### CM-10 — Input values are not validated: NaN, infinity, a high below the low and a negative volume pass unchecked and are treated differently by different functions (latent)

- **Area and flow:** every function's handling of bad numeric input; the rounding and tick side of the same outputs.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (missing validation); Logic and calculation (one input handled differently by different functions).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** not established for input validation, rounding or NaN (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`, question 15 row: "no source on consistency across paths, period checks, rounding or NaN"). Related statements: the `atr` docstring L91 to L92 ("Fewer candles -> **None** (never fabricate a value)"), the `session_vwap` docstring L144 to L145 ("never fabricates a price", IS-52), which concern insufficient data, not bad values; the `Candle` contract (IS-59): `from_kite` converts prices with `float` and volume with `int(... or 0)`.
- **Actual code path:** `core/candle_math.py` L72 to L79, L150 to L158, L209 to L217, L230 to L236, L271 to L277, L412 to L424; `sr_detector/models.py` L36 to L43 (boundary).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: no function checks for NaN or infinity, that `low <= high`, or that the close lies between them (P04-S020, P04-S061). INTERPRETATION (Python float semantics, P04-S061): a NaN spreads through the ATR, the EMA and `session_vwap`; in the true range it is returned only as the first term; in `adx` a NaN movement or smoothed true range gives 0.0 (L211, L212, L230, L236); in `rsi` a NaN delta gives a gain and a loss of 0.0; in `resample` a NaN high or low after a bucket's first candle is ignored. INTERPRETATION (P04-S020, P04-S029): a first candle with `high < low` gives a negative first true range; `session_vwap` skips a negative volume silently; a `None` volume or price raises `TypeError`. FACT: no function rounds or snaps a value to a tick, and the module reads no tick size (P04-S061, P04-S062); no source asks it to, so the unrounded outputs are recorded as context, not as an issue.
- **Evidence:** P04-S013, P04-S020, P04-S029, P04-S061, P04-S062, P04-S098; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION. Intent is not established; the basis is the weakness the code itself shows (missing validation, so one bad value gives NaN in one function and a silent 0.0 or an ignored value in another). Latent: whether any non-finite or inconsistent value reached the module depends on the server's rows, which are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P04-S098). KNOWN DEFECT not considered: (a) fails (the module implements no mechanism for non-finite or inconsistent values; there is no check to fail), and (c) fails (no source says the module handles such values; the "never fabricate" statements concern insufficient data).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established.
- **Owner:** A05; A08 (the row-to-`Candle` conversion).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 15; P04 point 17; A04 CG-27 (noted only: it does not reach this module, section 7.2.4).
- **Rama:** not needed.

### 8.1 Scope and method of this part

### 8.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### HA-01 — One "no candles" outcome for every failure, as SR-F4 intends; but a pacing timeout or a shutdown abort, in which no broker call is made, is logged as a WARNING that "historical_data failed", the abort against FIX-060's "INFO ... not a failure", and the closure adds no 429 path (A04 RL-05's A05 side)

- **Area and flow:** what a consumer receives, and what is logged, when one historical fetch through the closure and `OhlcFetcher._fetch_one` does not return candles: no handle, a limiter wait, a limiter timeout, a shutdown abort, a broker or transport exception (a 429 included), an empty answer, a malformed row.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (errors only logged; a log text that names the wrong cause; an expected shutdown logged as a failure); Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) SR-F4, `sr_detector/fetch.py` L20 to L21, and the module docstring L7 to L8 (27-Jun-2026, `dea336a`; A08's): "Every failure path (no token, fetch raises, empty result) returns None for that TF; nothing propagates into the pipeline"; "FAIL SAFE (never raise; an error yields no candles so the detector logs a fetch_failed row". (2) The closure's docstring, `main.py` L422 to L426 (27-Jun-2026, `dea336a`): "fails safe if no kite handle (the OhlcFetcher records a fetch_failed row, never raises)". (3) G7, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L137 to L138 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): the buckets wrap "every broker call", "4-step backoff on 429". (4) The exception classes, `core/exceptions.py`: `BrokerRateLimitError` L244 to L249 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`), "Client-side token bucket was exhausted and max_wait_sec elapsed", "WARN (not ERROR): rate limiting is an expected operational condition"; `BrokerRateLimit429Error` L265 to L266 (19-Apr-2026, `dfc4779`): `BrokerRateLimitError` "signals client-side bucket exhaustion (pre-emptive; never reached the broker)"; FIX-060 `RateLimitAbortedError` L286 to L292 (15-May-2026, `07979fa`): "aborted because the shutdown event was set", "This allows graceful shutdown", "INFO (not WARN/ERROR): shutdown is an expected operation, not a failure". (5) The tests (IS-75): the fetcher fails safe on an error, a token-lookup error and an empty answer. (6) RL9, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L1131 to L1142 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): `BrokerRateLimitError` with `SEVERITY = "WARN"`, "WARN (not ERROR) because rate limiting is an expected operational condition". HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-64, (2) IS-62, (3) IS-61 with CD-19, (5) IS-75; (4) IS-162, (6) IS-161 (IS-61 lists G7 and RL2 only); the 24-Jul record of the same outcome, IS-68. STUDY question 18 is ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE at its date there: one "no candles" outcome was intended.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L428 to L437 (no `try`; `rate_limiter.acquire("historical")` then `market_kite.historical_data(...)`); `broker/rate_limiter.py` L201 to L244 (A04's: the timeout and the abort); `sr_detector/fetch.py` L121 to L136 (`except Exception`: WARNING "sr_detector fetch: historical_data failed for %s/%s: %s", None), L59 to L68, L80 to L83, L98 to L101 (A08's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT and INTERPRETATION, P06-S025, P06-S026): every case ends as None for the interval (or a missing key from `fetch_timeframes`), nothing propagates, and the V1 detector turns an empty result into a `fetch_failed` analysis, as SR-F4 and the tests intend; an empty answer is treated like a failure, as SR-F4 lists it. ISSUE part (FACT): the closure lets `BrokerRateLimitError` and `RateLimitAbortedError` out unchanged, and the fetcher logs both, like any broker exception, as one WARNING whose text says "historical_data failed", although in neither case was `historical_data` called (P06-S025, table rows 3 and 4); the abort that FIX-060 calls an expected shutdown at INFO is logged at WARNING; the exception's own text is appended, so the cause can be read only from that tail. An empty answer and a missing handle log nothing. FACT (A04 RL-05, unchanged): the closure has no 429 freeze path and no translation; a broker 429 freezes nothing, so G7's backoff does not reach this path. The pacing-timeout WARNING agrees with RL9's "WARN" severity (P06-S018).
- **Evidence:** P06-S016, P06-S018, P06-S020, P06-S021, P06-S024 to P06-S027, P06-S069, P06-S081; P02-S059, P02-S061; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The single outcome matches its sources; the log text names a broker call that was not made for the two limiter outcomes, and the abort's severity contradicts FIX-060. The missing 429 path is A04 RL-05's ISSUE, unchanged, and is not counted here. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the fail-safe and its log line are the mechanism); (b) holds (the text is the same whenever a limiter exception occurs); (c) holds (several consumer threads share the bucket, so a wait and a timeout can arise; a stop can set the event during a wait; SR-F4 counts any raise as a failure path it handles); (d) fails: the outcome, None and no propagation, agrees with SR-F4 and the closure's docstring, and the WARNING level for a pacing timeout agrees with RL9 and the `BrokerRateLimitError` docstring; only the wording (against `BrokerRateLimit429Error`'s "never reached the broker") and the abort's level (against FIX-060's INFO) are contradicted, so not every intent source is.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; the closure feeds research and shadow features (A08, A18) and places no order (A04 RL-05's severity line). Whether any timeout, abort or broker error occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P06-S025; A04 evidence P05-S171).
- **Owner:** A05 (the closure lets the limiter's exceptions through); A08 (the fetcher's log text and level); A04 (RL-05, the 429 path, unchanged).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 18; P06 points 1 and 2; P02 points 10 and 14 (closure side); STUDY question 18; related A04 RL-05 (and A04 RL-17 for the abort itself, MATCH, unchanged); HA-12.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### HA-02 — Texts that state the historical request rate as "~3/s, never bursted" or "≤3 req/sec" through "the SAME" limiter, against G7's 2 per second with a burst of 2 and against the recorder's own limiter (IC-17)

- **Area and flow:** the documented pacing of historical fetches, as read by maintainers and operators.
- **Issue category:** Documentation against code; Intended against actual (sources that conflict, resolved one way).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) G7, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L137 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "historical: 2/2"; `config/broker_limits.yaml` L17 to L19 (17-Apr-2026): `burst: 2`, `rate_per_sec: 2`; `docs/crash_test_info_04jun2026.md` L364 to L369 (lines 11-Jun-2026): "2/s burst". (2) Against these: `docs/audit/eod_capture_safety_2026-07-24.md` L25 (file 25-Jul-2026, `308e9ce`): every capture fetch is "paced (≤ the historical bucket, ~3/s), never bursted", with line references "main.py:423-432" and "main.py:426"; `docs/audit/forward_shadow_deployment_evidence_13jul2026.md` L29 to L33 (14-Jul-2026): the shadow recorder's fetches go "through the SAME broker `RateLimiter` the live `OhlcFetcher` uses (`rate_limiter.acquire("historical")`, ≤3 req/sec — no second throttle)". The later texts do not name G7. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-61, IS-67; (2) IS-68, IS-69; the conflict IC-17 ("Code follows X").
- **Actual code path:** `config/broker_limits.yaml` L17 to L19; `broker/rate_limiter.py` L148 to L162 (A04's); `main.py` L2348, L431; `scripts/forward_shadow_record.py` L116 to L120 (A18's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the committed `historical` bucket holds 2 tokens and refills at 2 per second (committed value; effective value UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED), so two fetches can go back to back and the sustained rate is about 2 per second, not 3, and the bucket does burst (P06-S017 to P06-S019, P06-S077). FACT: the shadow recorder builds its own limiter in its own process (P06-S057); "SAME" holds for the class and the file, not the instance (A04 RL-06). - **Evidence:** P06-S017 to P06-S019, P06-S057, P06-S072, P06-S076 to P06-S078; code lines above.

The shadow recorder's "≤3 req/sec" and "SAME" texts are A04 RL-07's and RL-06's subjects, unchanged; this record's own part is the 24-Jul capture text, which no earlier record holds. ##### HA-04 — UNKNOWN: whether the broker's answers included a still-forming last candle; the fetch layer neither marks nor drops it, and only the A18 consumers state and apply a rule of their own

- **Area and flow:** the end of every historical window (the moment of the call), the last candle of the answer, and who decides whether an incomplete candle is used.

(3) The PB-01 capture, `v3_chain/watchlist_capture.py` L11 to L12 (13-Jul-2026, `5a71fb6`; A18's): "SETTLED-ONLY — a breakout on a FORMING daily candle ... is SKIPPED". (4) For the fetch layer, the V1 detector and the regime engine: no source states a rule (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`, question 19 row). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-73; (2) IS-166, (3) IS-167; STUDY question 19 OPEN QUESTION narrowed there.
- **Actual code path:** `sr_detector/fetch.py` L122 to L129 (window `now - lookback` to `now`; rows converted in order; nothing dropped); `main.py` L428 to L437; consumers at the boundary: `v3_chain/runner.py` L165 to L169, `v3_chain/pb01_entry.py` L176 to L177, L306 to L308 (A18's); the V1 detector, the regime engine and the retest monitor (no truncation found by the search of section 1).

MATCH part (FACT, boundary): the V3 chain and the PB-01 entry stage truncate to an as-of time before use, as their own sources state, so the fetch layer's passing the whole answer on is consistent with those consumers' design. FACT: the search found no truncation in the V1 detector, the regime engine or the retest monitor (A08's and A18's); with the V1 detector's 1800 s TTL, a list whose last candle was incomplete would be served again for up to 30 minutes (P06-S028, P06-S034). No source states a rule for the fetch layer, and whether the case arose depends on the server's answers of the legacy period; the lack of a stated rule alone is not an ISSUE. Whether the V1 detector and the regime engine were meant to use an incomplete last candle is their owners' (A08, A18). KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism for the case exists in the fetch layer; condition (a) fails).
