##### HA-06 — The fetchers' cache (A08's, boundary): a `(symbol, interval)` cache with a TTL, as SR-F3 states; but its age is taken from the wall clock while its comment says "monotonic", its key leaves out the lookback window, and the regime fetcher takes its TTL from the `sr_detector` section

- **Area and flow:** the in-memory cache of each `OhlcFetcher` built over the closure in `main.py`: what is cached, for how long, under which key, and which setting gives each fetcher its TTL.
- **Issue category:** Documentation against code; Weakness (a cache key that does not cover the request); Intended against actual (a setting read from another section).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) SR-F3, `sr_detector/fetch.py` L18 to L19 (27-Jun-2026, `dea336a`; A08's): "Intra-session cache keyed by (symbol, interval) with a TTL, so a re-signalling symbol is not re-fetched"; the field comment L49 (same date): "(symbol, interval) -> (fetched_at_monotonic_dt, candles)". (2) `config/system_config.yaml` L512 (27-Jun-2026, `dea336a`): `cache_ttl_sec: 1800` "intra-session (symbol, interval) re-fetch suppression"; L511: one lookback that "fits one request per interval". (3) `config/system_config.yaml` L615 (12-Jul-2026, `474e10b`): the V3 chain's `fetch_cache_ttl_sec: 1800.0` "own OhlcFetcher cache TTL (distinct from sr_detector.cache_ttl_sec)". (4) `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L251 (12-Jul-2026, `536172c`): the regime engine has its "own OhlcFetcher". No source states which TTL the regime fetcher was meant to use, and none states a cache shared between fetchers. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-64 with CD-22 (the "monotonic" comment) and CD-23 (the key); (2) IS-65; (3) IS-164; (4) IS-66.
- **Actual code path:** `sr_detector/fetch.py` L49 to L51, L100, L118 to L120, L127 to L128, L138 to L151; `main.py` L3519 to L3523 (regime: `cache_ttl_sec=float(getattr(_sr_cfg, "cache_ttl_sec", 1800.0))`), L3559 to L3562, L3678 to L3682, L3728 to L3732, L3774 to L3777; `sr_detector/__init__.py` L53 to L60 and `sr_detector/detector.py` L84 to L86, L168, L217 to L223 (the V1 fetcher and its optional second request; A08's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): each fetcher caches a non-empty result under `(symbol, interval)` (or `(cache_key, interval)` for `fetch_by_token`) and serves it while its age is at most the TTL; a failed or empty result is not cached, so a later request fetches again (P06-S027, P06-S028); at the committed values the V1 detector and the V3 chain keep lists for up to 1800 s and the watchlist fetcher has TTL 0 (committed values; effective values UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED). ISSUE part (FACT): the age is `now_fn()` minus the stored `now_fn()`, with `now_fn` = `time_authority.now_ist`, the host's wall clock, not a monotonic clock as L49 says; INTERPRETATION: a backward clock step makes the age negative, so an entry is served longer (and a TTL-0 entry is served at all), and a forward step expires entries early (P06-S028, P06-S038). FACT: the key leaves out the lookback, so within one fetcher a request for the same symbol and interval with another window inside the TTL would receive the earlier window's list; INTERPRETATION: at the committed constructions no fetcher with a non-zero TTL asks for one interval with two windows, so this does not arise there (P06-S028). FACT (check addition): the configuration documents an option under which it does arise: the V1 detector's fetcher (TTL 1800) makes a second request, `fetch_interval(symbol, anchor_intraday_interval, anchor_lookback_days)`, when `intraday_anchors_enabled` is true (`sr_detector/detector.py` L84 to L86, L190 to L191, L217 to L223; committed `false`), and the key's comment lists `30minute` among the allowed fine intervals ("5minute/15minute/30minute", `config/system_config.yaml` L542 to L544, 12-Jul-2026, `536172c`), one of the committed structure intervals at 180 days (L510 to L511); INTERPRETATION: with that setting the 1-day request for `30minute` is answered from the 180-day list, and, if the structural `30minute` fetch gave nothing and the 1-day fetch succeeded, a later structural request inside the TTL receives the 1-day list (what `intraday_anchors` and the detector make of either is A08's). The watchlist fetcher, which asks `day` and `30minute` with two windows each, has TTL 0, so a collision there needs a wall clock stepped back (P06-S038). FACT: the regime fetcher, not built at the committed values, takes its TTL from `sr_detector.cache_ttl_sec`, while the V3 chain's key states a TTL "distinct from sr_detector.cache_ttl_sec" for its own fetcher; the `regime` section has no TTL key (P06-S035). FACT: the six fetchers share no cache (P12-S064).
- **Evidence:** P06-S027, P06-S028, P06-S034 to P06-S039; P04-S077; P12-S064; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The cache does what SR-F3 states; its comment misnames the clock (CD-22), the key does not cover every request parameter (CD-23; latent: no committed construction exercises it), and the regime fetcher's TTL comes from another feature's section while the parallel V3 setting is stated as distinct (no source for the regime; the basis is the cross-section read the code shows). KNOWN DEFECT considered for the key and not given: (a) holds (the cache is the mechanism); (b) holds (a second window inside the TTL is always answered from the first); (c) holds only under a documented but not committed setting (anchors on with `anchor_intraday_interval: 30minute`, L542 to L543; or a backward clock step for the TTL-0 watchlist fetcher); no committed construction asks one fetcher with a non-zero TTL for one interval with two windows; (d) fails: SR-F3 states the very key, "(symbol, interval)", and the `fetch_interval` docstring (L73 to L79, 12-Jul-2026, `536172c`) says it "Reuses the same token/cache/fetch_fn machinery", so the outcome does not contradict every intent source.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether a clock step occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (A17).
- **Owner:** A08 (`sr_detector/fetch.py`, the cache); A18 (the regime fetcher's construction in `main.py`); A01 (`main.py` as a file); A02 (the keys); A05 (the boundary only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 20; P06 points 9, 10 and 17; P04 point 13 (passed by part CM); part XC (the cross-section reads of point 33, P09); HA-04.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### HA-08 — `Candle.from_kite` turns a missing, `None` or zero volume into 0 and truncates a fractional one, so a consumer cannot tell a missing volume from no trade; a `Candle` carries no instrument, interval or source (latent)

- **Area and flow:** the row-to-`Candle` conversion at the A08 boundary: the volume field and the candle's identity, as handed to the consumers that test volume (the V1 detector's breakout volume, PB-01's confirmation volume).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a silent default); Weakness (an object that does not identify its own data).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `sr_detector/models.py` L25, L35 (27-Jun-2026, `dea336a`; A08's): "One OHLCV bar"; "Build from a kite.historical_data() dict row". The documents that describe the broker's volume as "true per-interval (delta) volume" (MD1 L258 to L266, 25-Jul-2026) and "minute volume" (25-Apr-2026) are statements about the broker's data, not about missing values. The consumers that use the volume state their purpose: `config/system_config.yaml` L518 (`volume_surge_mult` "breakout volume must exceed this × avg for confirmation", 27-Jun-2026) and the PB-01 decision's "G-CONFIRM volume test" (`docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L286, 13-Jul-2026). No source states how a missing volume was to be treated. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): IS-59 (the type and the conversion), IS-72 (the volume documents); the L518 comment IS-165; SMAP L286 is IS-73.
- **Actual code path:** `sr_detector/models.py` L36 to L43 (`volume=int(row.get("volume") or 0)`); the published `kiteconnect` 5.1.0 `connect.py` L655 to L670 (part P02: every row carries `volume` from its sixth element).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT and INTERPRETATION (P06-S044, P06-S045): a missing key, `None` or `0` all become 0, and a fractional value is truncated toward zero; INTERPRETATION: with the pinned library the key is always present, so a 0 for a missing value arises only if the server sent `null` (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED for the legacy period, P06-S079). FACT: a `Candle` holds `ts` and the five price and volume fields only; the consumer knows the instrument, the interval and the source only from its own request (P06-S045 (g)). Value validation in general (NaN, `high < low`, a negative volume) is CM-10's and not repeated here.
- **Evidence:** P06-S043 to P06-S045, P06-S051, P06-S079; P02-S065; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Intent for missing values is not established; the basis is the silent default the code shows (Document 04, section 5.3: "a silent default or fallback"), for a field that two consumers use in confirmation tests. Latent: the case needs a `null` from the server, which is not established. The missing identity fields are recorded as context; no consumer on the `main.py` path is shown to mix candles of different requests. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) and (b) hold (the `or 0` default is the mechanism and always gives 0 for a missing or `None` volume); (c) not shown (the case needs a `null` from the server, not established for the legacy period); (d) fails (no intent source on a missing volume is found, so none is contradicted).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established.
- **Owner:** A08 (`Candle` and `from_kite`); A05 (the meaning of the broker's fields at the boundary); A18 (PB-01's volume test).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 21; P06 point 14; CM-10; CA-01 (the live volume, for contrast); HA-15.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### HA-09 — Texts at the `main.py` call site that do not describe the code: "Both default OFF → nothing constructed" over a six-flag condition with `sr_detector.enabled` committed `true`; "the OhlcFetcher records a fetch_failed row"; "None if no token" for a handle that is never None in Live; and a "spec C" that is not in the repository

- **Area and flow:** the comments, docstrings and log text of the historical call site, as read by maintainers.
- **Issue category:** Documentation against code.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `main.py` L3417 to L3420 (27-Jun-2026, `9f56b07`): the closure is "built ONCE if EITHER the V1 observer or the V2 WAIT_FOR_RETEST feature is enabled. Both default OFF → nothing constructed → zero pipeline change (dormant on deploy)". (2) `_make_sr_fetch_fn` docstring L424 to L426 (27-Jun-2026, `dea336a`): "Routes every call through rate_limiter.acquire("historical") (spec C pacing); fails safe if no kite handle (the OhlcFetcher records a fetch_failed row, never raises)". (3) `_build_market_data_kite` docstring L401 to L402 (same date): "None if no token (the fetch closure then fails safe → fetch_failed rows)"; the boot WARNING L3483 to L3486: "sr_detector/retest enabled but no market-data kite handle (no token?) — fetches will fail safe (fetch_failed rows)". (4) "spec C" in the closure docstring L425, `sr_detector/fetch.py` L7 to L8 and `scripts/sr_corp_action_spotcheck.py` L4. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-63 with CD-21; (2) and (3) IS-62; (4) section 7 of that file ("spec C" not in the repository).
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L3421 to L3487 (L3480, `5a71fb6`, 13-Jul-2026: six flags); `config/system_config.yaml` L509 (`sr_detector.enabled: true`, "committed true 28-Jun"); `main.py` L404 to L405, L2352 to L2377; `sr_detector/fetch.py` L121 to L136; `sr_detector/detector.py` L168 to L174 (A08's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: (1) the condition has six flags, and at the committed values three are on (`sr_detector.enabled` `true`, `v3_chain_mode` `"shadow"`, `watchlist.enabled` `true`), so the closure is built (committed values; effective values UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) (P06-S030, P06-S032, P06-S033); the comments added for the later flags (L3466 to L3478) each say they share the closure, and agree with the code (HA-16). (2) The fetcher records no row; it returns None and logs a WARNING for an exception; the `fetch_failed` analysis is made by the V1 detector only, and the other consumers do other things with None (P06-S024, P06-S026). (3) In Live the handle is the adapter's client and is never None, so the WARNING cannot be logged in Live, and it names two of the six features (P06-S014). (4) No specification named "spec C" is in the repository (P06-S073).
- **Evidence:** P06-S014, P06-S024, P06-S026, P06-S030, P06-S032, P06-S033, P06-S073; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL. Each text was true of an earlier state or of the paper branch, or names a component that does not do what it says; none changes behaviour. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism is involved).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none on behaviour.
- **Owner:** A05 (the market-data call-site texts); A01 (`main.py` as a file); A08 (`sr_detector/fetch.py` and the check script for "spec C").
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 36 (historical-access part); P06 points 11 and 15; part XC (point 36 as a whole); HA-01, HA-11, HA-16.
- **Rama:** not needed.

### 9.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### IN-01 — `load` raises classes other than the two its contract names: a short row, a reader error and an `exists()` failure leave unwrapped, and an undecodable file is reported as "missing" (A05 side of A01 C9)

- **Area and flow:** the load: `InstrumentCache.load(path)` and the exception it raises for each file problem; the pre-load in `main.py` that catches it.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Failure handling (errors reported under the wrong class).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) IC12, `core/instrument_cache.py` L25 to L26 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "load(path) classmethod; raises ConfigMissingError if file absent, ConfigSchemaError if any row is malformed". (2) The `load` docstring L106 to L109 (same date): "ConfigMissingError: file does not exist. ConfigSchemaError: a required column is absent or a row has a type-conversion error." (3) The `reload` docstring L203 to L204 (17-May-2026, `f08b485`): "Raises: ConfigMissingError, ConfigSchemaError: same as load()". (4) `docs/audit/integrity_audit_2026.md` L1616 to L1622 (01-Aug-2026): "`InstrumentCache.load` is fail-fast (ConfigMissingError / ConfigSchemaError ...)". (5) A01 C9's boundary sentence (A01 AUDIT L748), a project record, not a legacy intent source: `ConfigMissingError` "for an absent or unreadable file" and `ConfigSchemaError` "for a present but invalid one". MODULE 38's IC4 (L80 to L82, 17-Apr-2026) puts `InstrumentNotFoundError` under `ConfigError`, a different contract under the same labels (IC-09). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) to (3) IS-76, IS-82; (4) IS-83; the code-only contradiction CD-25; the conflict IC-09; the tests IS-88.
- **Actual code path:** `core/instrument_cache.py` L111 to L115 (the `exists()` test outside the `try`), L124 to L186 (the `try`, the row build at L142 to L150, the `except (KeyError, ValueError)` at L151, the `except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)` at L180); `main.py` L2440 to L2449 (the pre-load's `except Exception`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT and INTERPRETATION (the evidence table P05-S034): an absent file gives `ConfigMissingError` ("not found"); a missing header, a missing column and a value that does not convert, and the three refused values, give `ConfigSchemaError` with a line number, as intended. Beside these: a row with fewer fields than the header gives an unwrapped `TypeError` or `AttributeError` (the missing cells are `None`, which neither `except` clause catches); a `csv` reader error gives an unwrapped `csv.Error`; an `exists()` failure other than "not found" leaves as that `OSError`, unwrapped, because L111 precedes the `try` (on the standard-library version read for the STUDY; the production Python version is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, P05-S020, P05-S113); a present file whose bytes are not UTF-8, a directory and an unreadable file give `ConfigMissingError` ("Could not read"), the class the docstring reserves for a file that "does not exist". INTERPRETATION (`main.py` L2445): the pre-load catches every class alike, so in the trading process the class makes no difference to the outcome, which is A01 C9's (P05-S053). The `reload` docstring also omits `EventDispatchError`, which `publish` raises when a synchronous subscriber fails (P05-S036).
- **Evidence:** P05-S019, P05-S020, P05-S026, P05-S027, P05-S030, P05-S031, P05-S034, P05-S036, P05-S053, P05-S081, P05-S097 (no test uses these cases), P05-S105; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The code raises classes that IC12 and both docstrings do not name, and reports an undecodable present file as missing. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the `except` at L151 is the mechanism that turns a malformed row into `ConfigSchemaError`); (b) holds for a short row (by Python and `csv` semantics the `TypeError` or `AttributeError` escapes L151 whenever a row is short); (c) not established: IC12 says the load handles "any row" that is malformed, but under the documented set-up the file is written whole by `csv.DictWriter` with every column (P05-S076; part IR for the write discipline), so a short row needs a damaged or hand-edited file; (d) fails for the start-level outcome: `main.py` treats every class alike, so the start ends as it would for a `ConfigSchemaError` (A01 C9's path); only the class and its message differ from the sources. Severity minor: the class reaches the ERROR log line only; the outcome is A01 C9's, unchanged.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none beyond the log text; whether any load ever failed is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P05-S112).
- **Owner:** A05 (the load's exceptions); A01 (C9: the `main.py` handling, unchanged).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 22; P05 point 1; related: A01 C9 (ISSUE; KNOWN DEFECT; FACT; A01's, unchanged); IN-11 (the documents).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### IN-02 — Token-0 placeholder rows load as ordinary instruments: they share one token entry, and three consumers treat token 0 in three different ways, one of them without a log line

- **Area and flow:** the load and the token lookups: a row whose `instrument_token` is 0, which the writer produces for a security-master symbol it cannot match in the broker's dump.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (missing validation); Consistency across components (one value handled differently by different consumers); Failure handling (silent fallback).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The refresh docstring RI12, `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L25 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "Symbols not matched in Kite: token=0, lot_size=1, tick_size=0.05" (IS-89): the writer is meant to write such rows. (2) FIX-134 (commit `03ac3b0`, 31-May-2026): post-refresh validation of "the zero-token %" (IS-92; the figure is a code value, part IR). (3) IC12 (L25 to L26, 17-Apr-2026): `ConfigSchemaError` "if any row is malformed" (IS-76); no source says whether a token-0 row is malformed. (4) TASK3 (17-Apr-2026) builds the universe from the dump, where the case would not arise (IS-90; the conflict IC-14, part IR's). (5) Record 02 R02-45 (L1197 to L1200, 18-Sep-2026): the fetcher "warns" on a zero token (IS-127; CD-37). No source states how the cache or its consumers were to treat a token-0 row (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`, section 6.2, question 25). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `core/instrument_cache.py` L144, L158 to L177 (no check on the token's value), L93 to L94 (the indexes), L293 (`token_map`); consumers at their call lines: `sr_detector/fetch.py` L105 to L112 (A08); `orders/order_placer.py` L2214 to L2221, L3401 to L3404 (A10); `main.py` L3946 (the candle store's copy, part CA).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `load` accepts token 0 (and negative tokens, IN-03); no row is marked as a placeholder. INTERPRETATION (dictionary semantics): all token-0 rows share one `_by_token` entry and one `token_map()` entry, each holding the last such row's symbol, so `get_by_token(0)` returns one arbitrary symbol; `count()` includes them, so they count towards the 1000-row check. At the consumers: the S&R fetcher's `token or None` (L109) turns 0 into "no token" without a log line (the WARNING at L111 is only for an exception), against Record 02's statement; the smart-target registration passes 0 on (L2214 to L2221: "smart_tgt tolerates 0"); the exit-retry path would call `subscribe([0])` (L3403 to L3404). No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. HISTORICAL: the last tracked file (2228 rows, `9f58848`, 21-Apr-2026) had 144 token-0 rows, the only repeated token; it is not the deployed file (P05-S079). The alias tool skips token "0" as "invalid" (P05-S073, P05-S080).
- **Evidence:** P05-S017, P05-S028, P05-S046, P05-S064 (rows 5, 6, 13), P05-S066, P05-S079, P05-S080; P07-S024, P07-S035; P11-S071, P11-S217; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Intent for the cache's treatment is not established, but the code demonstrates a weakness of a kind Document 04, section 5.3 lists (missing validation; one value treated three ways): a value that the writer's own docstring defines as "not matched in Kite" reaches the cache as a valid key, collapses in the token index, and is used, silently in two of three consumers, as if it were a token. Record 02's "warns" does not describe the fetcher (CD-37; HISTORICAL, the record is part XC's). KNOWN DEFECT not considered: no mechanism of the cache is meant to refuse token 0.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; the deployed file's token-0 rows are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P05-S111); whether the exit-retry path ever subscribed is as stated above.
- **Owner:** A05 (the cache and the file's contents); part IR (the writer's RI12 rows and the 20-percent rule); A08, A10 (their consumers' handling).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 22; P05 points 4 and 12; STUDY question 25; CD-37; part IR (STUDY 18.1 point 27).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 25 bears on treatment).

##### IN-03 — Other values `load` lets through without notice: negative tokens, any `exchange` text, unrecognised `is_fno` text read as False, `nan` and `inf` ticks, and repeated symbols or tokens ("later row wins")

- **Area and flow:** the load's row checks and the two indexes; the tick guards of the consumers that use `tick_size`.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (missing validation); Failure handling (silent fallback); Consistency across components (guard forms that differ for one value).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) IC12 (L25 to L26, 17-Apr-2026): `ConfigSchemaError` "if any row is malformed". (2) The field comments L64 (`"NSE" | "BSE"`), L67 ("True if this instrument has F&O"), L66 ("Minimum price movement e.g. 0.05") (17-Apr-2026). (3) The class docstring L59 to L60: "All numeric types are pre-converted at load time so callers never need to cast" (17-Apr-2026; IS-169). (4) A04 CG-27 (MATCH; A04's): "the instrument cache's refusal of a non-positive tick, on which the helpers' safety rests" (IS-136). No source addresses repeated symbols or tokens (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`, section 6.1, point 22). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): IS-76, IS-77, IS-88; IS-136.
- **Actual code path:** `core/instrument_cache.py` L140 to L178, L93 to L94; the guard forms at `broker/zerodha_adapter.py` L1397 and `orders/smart_tgt_manager.py` L740 (`<= 0`) against `orders/order_placer.py` L4032 and `orders/structure_exit_manager.py` L654 (`tick and tick > 0`) (consumers' files; boundary only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: only an empty symbol, a lot size below 1 and a tick of 0 or below are refused; a negative token, any `exchange` text (the empty text included) and any `sector` load. INTERPRETATION (conversion and comparison semantics, P05-S025, P05-S029): `is_fno` is True only for "true", "1" or "yes", and any other text (a misspelling, "t", "y", an empty cell) becomes False without error; `float()` accepts `nan` and `inf`, and `tick_size <= 0` is False for both, so such rows load. The guard forms then differ: the `tick and tick > 0` forms fall back to 0.05 for `nan`; the `<= 0` forms (the adapter's `_resolve_tick`, the smart-target manager) pass `nan` on; `inf` passes both (P05-S085). INTERPRETATION (dictionary semantics, P05-S017): a repeated symbol or token is resolved silently by the later row; `all_rows()`, `count()` and `token_map()` still include every row, so the size check counts duplicates while the lookups see one row, and two rows of one symbol with different tokens put both tokens in `token_map()`. A BSE and an NSE row of one symbol would collide the same way (P05-S087). FACT: no non-test code reads `exchange`, and only the paper-only slippage engine reads `is_fno` (P05-S071), so those two values have no Live effect. HISTORICAL: the past tracked file had no repeated symbol, every row `NSE`, ticks among six positive values (P05-S079).
- **Evidence:** P05-S017, P05-S024, P05-S025, P05-S028, P05-S029, P05-S071, P05-S079, P05-S085, P05-S087, P05-S097 (no test of these cases); code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION. A weakness the code demonstrates (missing validation; silent fallback; consumers disagreeing on one value) against the docstring's statement that a malformed row fails the load. A04 CG-27's MATCH for the refusal of a non-positive tick is unchanged (IN-20); this record is the A05 side of the `nan` and `inf` case only. KNOWN DEFECT considered for the `nan` tick and not given: (a) holds (L171 is the mechanism that refuses an invalid tick); (b) holds (by floating-point semantics `nan <= 0` is False whenever the value is `nan`); (c) not established: the writer takes ticks from the broker's dump or the 0.05 default (part IR, P07-S024, P07-S082), and whether a `nan` or `inf` could arise there is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; (d) holds (the outcome contradicts IC12 and CG-27's stated basis).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; the deployed file's content is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P05-S111).
- **Owner:** A05 (the load); A04 and A11 (their guard forms); part IR (what the writer can produce).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 points 22 and 25; P05 points 4 and 10; related: A04 CG-27 (MATCH, unchanged), A04 CG-25 (MATCH, unchanged; IN-21).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### IN-04 — `reload` returns a new cache that no holder would see and publishes a count-only event that nothing receives; FIX-092's refresh cannot reach the consumers as built (A05 side of A01 C29)

- **Area and flow:** refresh lifecycle: `InstrumentCache.reload(path, event_bus)` and the `InstrumentsRefreshed` event it publishes.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour not implemented); Weakness (code kept that nothing calls).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) FIX-092, the `reload` docstring `core/instrument_cache.py` L190 to L194 (17-May-2026, `f08b485`): "Reload instruments from CSV and publish InstrumentsRefreshed event ... This allows downstream consumers (like CandleStore) to garbage-collect dead tokens." (2) `core/events.py` L225 to L229 (FIX-092 lines of 17-May-2026): the event "Signals that the instrument universe has changed (symbols added/removed, tokens updated). Consumers like CandleStore use this to garbage-collect dead tokens". (3) The module docstring L7 (17-Apr-2026): "Never mutates after load()". HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): IS-82, IS-76; CD-20; the conflict IC-11 (IN-05).
- **Actual code path:** `core/instrument_cache.py` L188 to L215; `core/events.py` L222 to L234, L300 to L345; `main.py` L2441 (the only `load`), L3847, L3858 (`main.py`'s own `subscribe` calls; no non-test code subscribes to `InstrumentsRefreshed`, P05-S038).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `reload` builds a new cache with `load`, publishes `InstrumentsRefreshed` carrying only `token_count` (the row count, duplicates and token-0 rows included) and returns the new object; it assigns nothing to `self`. It has no non-test caller; nothing subscribes to the event (the only caller is a test). INTERPRETATION: every consumer received the boot-time object at boot (the adapter, the sizer, the risk engine's lambda, the shadow tracker, the managers, the order placer, the signal processor, the S&R objects), so even a caller of `reload` would change none of them unless it rebound every reference; the event names no token or symbol. If a synchronous subscriber raised, `publish` would raise `EventDispatchError` after the new cache was built, and the caller would not receive it (P05-S036).
- **Evidence:** P05-S035 to P05-S038, P05-S056 to P05-S063, P05-S082, P05-S100; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The unwired chain (no caller, no subscriber) is A01 C29's ISSUE, counted in A01 and not again here; this record counts the A05 side: the method's own model (a new object, not a refresh of the one held) cannot deliver FIX-092's stated purpose to the existing holders, and its event carries nothing a consumer could act on. The event's content against `gc_sweep`'s argument is part CA's (CA-12), not repeated. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: the method has no caller, so condition (c) fails (the case does not arise).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none (no caller). Whether `reload` was ever run by other means is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P05-S116).
- **Owner:** A05 (`reload`, the event's publisher); A01 (C29, unchanged); part CA (the store side, CA-12).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 24; P05 point 6; STUDY question 13; related: A01 C29; CA-12; IN-05.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 13 bears on treatment).

INTERPRETATION (Python semantics): at L3402 the `else` branch cannot run with this cache; a symbol missing from the cache leaves through the `except Exception` at L3424 and is logged as `exit_retry_subscribe_failed`, and the retry stays queued (P05-S066). In the tick callback, L3451 calls `get_by_symbol` for every pending retry with no `try` between L3438 and L3453, so while one queued retry's symbol is missing every call of the callback raises before any tick is examined, and no pending retry of any symbol is processed by that path; the feed catches and logs each such exception (P05-S067; part LF, LF-24); how long such an entry stays queued is A10's. - **Model parts.** Parts LF, CA, CM, HA and IN own the points they record; this part cites them and does not repeat them. In particular: part IN owns the loader side of the file (token-0 rows in the cache, IN-02; values the loader lets through, IN-03; one cache per process and the 21-Jun-2026 staleness on the cache side, IN-05; the meaning of `is_fno`, IN-06; the shared path, IN-10; the column contract, IN-18; the keys, IN-23); part LF disposes of the feed side of STUDY 18.1 point 8 and hands the refresh's `ImportError` text to this part (LF section 5); part HA disposes of the closure side of the REST error mapping (P02 point 14) and hands the refresh's call to this part.
- **Labels and results** as the A04 AUDIT, section 1.6 (the A01 AUDIT's KNOWN DEFECT rule unchanged). For an alert that cannot fire because the caller passes no notifier, this part reads condition (b) as part LF does (LF-01, LF-02): silence that follows from the wiring at the call site, not from language or library semantics. Every legacy source is HISTORICAL; a declaration is not proof that a path ran. Wherever subscriptions or ticks arise: No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. No credential value, access-token value, account identifier, personal name, address or absolute path is written; credential variables are named only as configuration; no security finding is made (D-07).

### 10.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### IR-01 — Two designs for `instruments.csv` under the same labels RI1 to RI15: the code follows the refresh docstring's design and differs from TASK3 on the universe, the symbol text, the lot-size and F&O authorities, the sector labels and fallback, Kite-only symbols, the report, the log file and the default account, and from MODULE 38 on "NSE + NFO" (related A04 CG-04)

- **Area and flow:** the refresh as a whole: which source defines each column of `config/instruments.csv` and which symbols are written.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Consistency across components (competing sources of truth for one file's design).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The refresh docstring, `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L1 to L28 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "Build instruments.csv from three authoritative sources": the security master ("symbol universe"), the sector files ("sector classification"), the Kite API ("instrument tokens, lot sizes, tick sizes"); RI1 "Security master defines the symbol universe"; RI3 "is_fno from fno_symbols.csv; security master fno_stock column ignored"; RI4 Kite provides token, lot size and tick size; RI8 "Fallback: env vars" (IS-89). (2) TASK3, `docs/web_claude/04_post_build_ops/task3_nse_reference_data.txt` (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "For each symbol in Kite instruments dump (exchange=NSE, instrument_type=EQ)" with `symbol` "from Kite (tradingsymbol)" (L87 to L90); `lot_size` "from NSE security master (market_lot)", then Kite, then 1 (L93 to L95), and "USE Kite's lot_size for F&O" (L145 to L151); `is_fno` "from NSE security master (fno_stock=yes)", `fno_symbols.csv` as a cross-check, the security master the authority on a mismatch (L96 to L97, L137 to L143); sector labels such as BANKING, FINANCIAL_SERVICES, PRIVATE_BANKING, PSU_BANKING (L60 to L61 and the list above it), "If symbol in NO sector index -> sector = "OTHER"" and conflicts "Log INFO" (L129 to L131); "Symbols NOT in security_master but IN Kite: include with sector="OTHER"" (L133 to L135); a final report of counts (L191 to L200); a log file `logs/refresh_instruments.log` (L233 to L238); "Default: use primary account from accounts.csv" (L189); "Validate: row count > 1000" (L180) (IS-90). (3) MODULE 38, `modules_38_39_40_final.txt` IC2 L47 to L53 and IC12 L158 to L162 (17-Apr-2026): the script "fetches kite.instruments()", "Operator runs this daily (new F&O contracts appear each expiry)", "Filters to NSE + NFO + needed fields" (IS-91). The three are of one commit and one date, and none names another: the conflict IC-14 (with IC-10 for F&O rows). A04 CG-04 (ISSUE (minor; latent); A04's) records that the instrument record has no instrument-type field (IS-136). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L92 to L109 (`_load_security_master`), L112 to L145 (sector map, F&O set), L148 to L156 (`_build_kite_lookup`), L159 to L204 (`_build_rows`), L294 to L306 (one `instruments("NSE")` call), L309 to L348 (credentials), L380 to L426 (`main`); all `bcf03b5` (17-Apr-2026) except the validation lines (`03ac3b0`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the code follows source (1): the universe and the `symbol` text are the security master's (L101, L175, L195); the dump gives only the token, the lot size and the tick, found by exact `tradingsymbol` (L176, L183 to L185); `is_fno` is membership in `fno_symbols.csv` and `fno_stock` is not read (L12, L187); the security master's `market_lot` is not read, so every lot size is the dump's equity-row value or 1 (L180, L184; P07-S029); the labels are BANK, FIN_SERVICES, METAL, PRIVATE_BANK, PSU_BANK and the others of L72 to L85, with a blank, not "OTHER", for a symbol in no file (L188); no conflict is logged; a symbol in the dump but not in the security master is never written; the only report is the printed counts and the unmatched-symbol WARNING (L385, L390, L396, L400 to L403, L414, L425); no log file is written (output goes to stdout and stderr, which the declared line appends to `logs/cron-refresh-instruments.log`, P07-S041, P07-S059); without `--account` the generic environment names are the fallback, not a primary account (L340 to L347); exactly 1000 rows pass (L258) where TASK3 asks for more than 1000. Against MODULE 38: one request for the NSE dump only (L294, L394), segments "NSE" and "NSE-EQ" only (L154), no NFO row, no expiry, strike or instrument-type column (L55 to L58) (P07-S031, P07-S090). INTERPRETATION: the tick taken from the dump with a 0.05 default agrees with TASK3 L98 to L99 and the atomic write with TASK3's RI7 (IR-16, IR-22); everything else listed differs. HISTORICAL: the last tracked file (`9f58848`, 21-Apr-2026) has the code's labels, lot size 1 in every row and 206 `is_fno` "true" rows, consistent with source (1); it is not the deployed file (P07-S025, P05-S079).
- **Evidence:** P07-S007, P07-S012, P07-S022, P07-S023, P07-S029, P07-S031, P07-S041, P07-S042, P07-S076, P07-S088 to P07-S090; P05-S079; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. A conflict between intent sources of one date that the code resolves one way: it implements the docstring's design and none of TASK3's differing points, and it writes no NFO data although MODULE 38 asks for "NSE + NFO" (the writer side of A04 CG-04, which is kept). KNOWN DEFECT not considered: the code implements the design it chose; no mechanism fails.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none on the run itself. The consequences of the choice are recorded where they arise: unmatched security-master symbols written as token-0 rows rather than Kite-only symbols added (IR-07, IR-19; IN-02 for the cache); equity lot sizes for F&O-eligible symbols (IN-06, CG-04).
- **Owner:** A05 (the refresh and the file's design); A04 (CG-04, unchanged); A10 (whether any F&O instrument was traded, CG-04).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 28; P07 points 10 and 11; STUDY questions 22, 28 and 29; IC-14, IC-10; related: A04 CG-04 (ISSUE (minor; latent); A04's, unchanged); IN-06 (the cache side); CD-36 (Record 01's description of the file, part XC's).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY questions 28 and 29 bear on treatment).

##### IR-02 — The refresh docstring's own counts and the alert's docstring do not describe the code: "13 sector files" against 12, four series against five plus every row with an empty series, and a "Telegram CRITICAL" alert sent as "ERROR" (related A02 F-35)

- **Area and flow:** the refresh's documents: the docstring's RI2 and RI11, and the docstring of `_send_refresh_alert`.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement; implemented behaviour that nothing describes).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) RI2, `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L14 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "Sector from config/reference_data/index_members/ (13 sector files)"; TASK3 L105 (17-Apr-2026) also says 13 and lists 12 (P07-S076). (2) RI11, L24 (17-Apr-2026): "Only EQ-series rows from security master (series='eq'|'be'|'n'|'bt')"; the constant's comment L67: "RI11: accepted equity series (lowercase)". (3) RI1, L13: "~2800 EQ-series symbols". (4) `_send_refresh_alert` docstring L275 (31-May-2026, `03ac3b0`): "Send Telegram CRITICAL alert on validation failure". Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): IS-89, IS-92; the code-only contradiction CD-26. A02 F-35 (MATCH for the file set; A02's) lists points 3 and 5 of this record in its handoff (IS-134). Record 02 R02-68 also omits the empty series (IS-129); part XC (XC-07) counts R02-68 among the PARTLY AGREES disposed of by this record, as a precision omission with no separate finding. HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L68 (`_EQ_SERIES`), L72 to L85 (`_SECTOR_INDEX_PRIORITY`), L103 to L107 (the series filter), L274 to L287 (`_send_refresh_alert`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `_SECTOR_INDEX_PRIORITY` lists 12 files; `_EQ_SERIES` holds five texts, `eq`, `be`, `n`, `bt` and `il`; the filter at L106 skips a row only when its series is non-empty and not in the set, so every row with an empty series is also kept (P07-S011, P07-S012, P07-S013 (a)); the function would pass `severity="ERROR"` (L280). INTERPRETATION: how many security-master rows pass the filter, and what `n`, `bt` and `il` denote, are not stated in the code; the tracked security master has 2802 lines with the header (P07-S014), and its series values were not read, so RI1's "~2800 EQ-series symbols" can be neither confirmed nor refuted here (P07-S032).

A02 F-35's MATCH for the file set is kept; this record is A05's side of its points 3 and 5. No KNOWN DEFECT (documents only).

##### IR-03 — The refresh's own failure alert cannot fire: `main` calls `_send_refresh_alert(errors)` without a notifier, so the "[SYSTEM] INSTRUMENT REFRESH FAILED" message is never sent and the cron wrapper's per-job alert is the only alert path

- **Area and flow:** failure reporting after a failed validation (step 9 of `main`).
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (alerts that cannot fire).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The function's docstring, `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L275 (31-May-2026, `03ac3b0`): "FIX-134 Item 40: Send Telegram CRITICAL alert on validation failure", with the title "[SYSTEM] INSTRUMENT REFRESH FAILED" and the body ending "Old instrument data retained." (L279 to L285). (2) Commit `03ac3b0` (31-May-2026), FIX-134 item 40, which adds the validation and this alert (IS-92). (3) Commit `75d5e99` (18-Jun-2026), TASK #3: "HeartbeatTimer(alert=True) sends a per-job alert on exit (FAILED always; SUCCESS only for critical jobs)" (IS-93, IS-122); it does not mention the refresh's own alert. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): IS-92, IS-93; the code-only contradiction CD-28; the tests IS-98. HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L274 to L277 (return when `notifier is None`), L405 to L412 (`main` calls `_send_refresh_alert(errors)` at L410, `03ac3b0`); L429 to L442 (`_cron_main`, `75d5e99`); `utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L156 to L171 (A16's; the per-job alert call).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the only non-test caller passes no notifier, so the function returns at L277 on every validation failure; nothing in the script builds or imports a notifier; only `tests/unit/test_fix134_instrument_validation.py` (L111 to L127) calls it with a test double (P07-S036, P07-S063). The validation messages go to stderr (L408 to L409), so under the declared line they reach the cron log only. INTERPRETATION: every command-line start runs through `_cron_main` (L445 to L446), so a failed validation records a FAILED heartbeat with the message "exit code 1" and calls `alert_job_result` (A14's), whose content was not read (P07-S051, P07-S052); the operator's alert, if any, carries the job's status, not the validation messages.
- **Evidence:** P07-S034, P07-S036, P07-S049 to P07-S052, P07-S063, P07-S091; P11-S197; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The code does not implement what the function's docstring and FIX-134 state. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (`_send_refresh_alert` is a mechanism for the case); (b) fails, read as part LF reads an uninjected kill switch or notifier (LF-01, LF-02): the function sends whenever it is given a notifier, and its silence follows from the call at L410, not from language or library semantics; (c) holds (a validation failure can arise under the documented set-up, for example an empty dump, P07-S047); (d) not established: the later TASK #3 source (IS-93) sets up the per-job alert as the scheduled jobs' alert path and says nothing of the refresh's own alert, so whether the outcome contradicts every source is STUDY question 31.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: a failed refresh still produces a FAILED heartbeat and a per-job alert call (where the database and alert path work), but no message names the validation errors. Whether any validation failed in the legacy period, and what the per-job alert sent, are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P07-S052; IR-14).
- **Owner:** A05 (the refresh); A14 (the per-job alert's content); A16 (the cron wrapper).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 26; P07 point 1; STUDY question 31; CD-28; IR-26 (match: the cron wrapper).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 31 bears on treatment).

##### IR-04 — Exit codes follow neither RI14 nor TASK3: broker and token rejections end with 1, a missing or empty token file with 2, a malformed token file with 1, and 2 also means a missing library or an option error, so the status cannot tell an API or authentication failure from a file or configuration failure (related A04 CL-15)

- **Area and flow:** the refresh's failure paths and their exit statuses (G3), as recorded by the heartbeat and read by the pre-flight's fix.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (errors reported under the wrong code); Consistency across components (two intent sources and the code give three mappings).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) RI14, `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L27 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "Exit codes: 0=success, 1=file/config error, 2=API/auth error"; RI7, L19: "abort (exit 1) if output row count < 1000" (IS-89). (2) TASK3 RI12, L223 to L231 (17-Apr-2026): exit 2 for a missing security master or `index_members/`; "Missing Kite token: exit 6"; "Kite API error: exit 1 with error details"; "Empty Kite response: exit 1"; its opening section L66: "If files not present: print clear error and exit 1" (IS-90; TASK3 is inconsistent within itself on the missing files). The conflict IC-14; the code-only contradiction CD-27. A04 CL-15 (ISSUE; A04's) records the malformed token file's exit 1 against RI14's 2 (IS-136). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L94 to L96, L298 to L303, L304 to L306 (no `try`), L317 to L348, L333 (no guard), L355 to L377 (argparse), L405 to L412, L421 to L424, L429 to L442; `core/account_registry.py` L104 to L108, L121 to L202 (A01's registry; raise lines only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT and INTERPRETATION (the table P07-S050): 0 for success, `--dry-run`, `--help` and a non-trading day; 1 for a missing security master, an account not among the enabled rows, an empty API-key variable, unset generic names, the validation and the post-write check, and, with a traceback, every uncaught exception: the account file's own load errors, a malformed token file (CL-15), a malformed reference row or input that is not UTF-8, a write error, and every broker-side failure (a `requests` exception such as the library's 7-second default timeout, a kiteconnect exception chosen by the server's `error_type` such as `TokenException`, a `TypeError` for an error body without `error_type`, a parse error of the dump) (P02-S059 to P02-S061, P02-S069, P07-S047); 2 for an absent token file, an empty `access_token` field, a missing `kiteconnect` (or Twisted or autobahn) and an argparse option error. INTERPRETATION: against RI14, every API and authentication failure from the broker exits 1 while 2 covers token-file and environment problems; against TASK3, the API error's 1 agrees, the missing token's 2 differs from its 6, and the missing security master's 1 agrees with L66 but not with RI12's 2. The heartbeat records "exit code 1", "SystemExit: <n>" or "<class>: <message>" (P07-S050), and the pre-flight's fix tests only for 0 (P07-S061), so only the message text tells the failure kinds apart.
- **Evidence:** P07-S007, P07-S043, P07-S044, P07-S046 to P07-S050, P07-S061, P07-S078, P07-S088; P02-S059 to P02-S061, P02-S069, P02-S094; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The code's mapping differs from both sources (Document 04, section 5.3: errors reported under the wrong code; documented behaviour not implemented). A04 CL-15 is A04's and kept; this record is A05's side for the other paths. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: no mechanism maps broker failures to 2 ((a) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: no reader acts on 1 as against 2 (the cron wrapper records FAILED for any non-zero status and the pre-flight's fix tests for 0); the distinction is lost for diagnosis only. Whether any of these failures occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (IR-14).
- **Owner:** A05 (the refresh's exit codes); A04 (CL-15, unchanged); A01 (the account registry's exceptions); A16 (the heartbeat record).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 26; P07 point 2; P02 points 11 and 14 (refresh side); IC-14, CD-27; related: A04 CL-15 (ISSUE; A04's, unchanged), A04 CL-12 (the uncaught account-file errors; kept).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### IR-05 — Run signals that do not describe the run: `--help` and option errors record a FAILED heartbeat and an alert call, `--dry-run` records SUCCESS under the job's name although nothing is written, and a missing Twisted or autobahn is reported as "kiteconnect package not installed"

- **Area and flow:** the completion signals of the refresh under the cron entry (heartbeat row, per-job alert call) and its import failure message.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (alerts that fire wrongly); Flow and state (a status updated inconsistently with the run's effect).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) Commit `75d5e99` (18-Jun-2026; IS-93, IS-122): the scheduled scripts record heartbeats and alert by a per-job policy, so that the operator sees which jobs were done, failed or missed. (2) Commit `c3c1242` (21-Jun-2026; IS-94): a refresh that does not write must exit non-zero "so a silent no-write is caught by the cron heartbeat/marker". (3) RI9, `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L22 (17-Apr-2026): "--dry-run: display stats without writing" (IS-89). (4) The import message L299 to L301 (17-Apr-2026): "kiteconnect package not installed" (a code text, not an intent source; `A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`, section 6.1, point 8). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L296 to L303, L355 to L377, L416 to L418, L429 to L442; `utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L140 to L173 (A16's); `kiteconnect/__init__.py` L111 to L113 and `ticker.py` L18 to L22 (published 5.1.0 source, data only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT and INTERPRETATION (P07-S048, P07-S050): `--help` raises `SystemExit(0)` inside the timer, which records FAILED "SystemExit: 0" and calls the per-job alert; an unknown option records FAILED "SystemExit: 2"; `--dry-run` resolves the credentials, calls the API, writes nothing and returns 0, so the timer records SUCCESS for the job `refresh_instruments` and calls the alert with `critical` left `None` (`utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L121, L132, A16's; `_cron_main` L435 passes no `critical`) (P07-S049, P07-S052). HISTORICAL: by commit `75d5e99` and the timer's docstring (L110 to L111) a SUCCESS is alerted "only for jobs marked critical in the registry", and the registry marks this job `critical: true` (`config/cron_registry.yaml` L310); whether `alert_job_result` reads the registry when `critical` is `None`, and so what it sends for a dry run, is A14's and was not read (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED). The `except ImportError` at L298 also catches a missing Twisted or autobahn, because importing the package imports the ticker module (P02-S012, P02-S093); the message names only kiteconnect. The declared line passes neither `--help` nor `--dry-run` (P07-S059), so these paths arise only from a manual or other start.
- **Evidence:** P07-S048 to P07-S052, P07-S059; P02-S012, P02-S093; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. A dry run is recorded under the job's name exactly as a refresh that wrote the file, which is the kind of no-write that source (2) meant the heartbeat to catch (here an explicit choice, not a silent one); a help request is recorded as a failed job; the import message misnames the missing package. No KNOWN DEFECT: each path does what its code says; the signals misdescribe the run.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor; manual starts only. Whether any such start occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (IR-14).
- **Owner:** A05 (the refresh); A16 (the wrapper's treatment of `SystemExit`); A14 (the alert's content).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 points 8 (refresh part) and 26; P07 points 13 and 14; P02 point 13; CD-27; IR-26 and IR-27 (match: the cron entry; RI9); FB-12 and DC-15 (the same A16 helper fact for the ban-list and daily scripts, recorded there within MATCH rows).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### IR-06 — Missing sector files, a missing F&O list or a missing `index_members/` directory are skipped with no message, and the run can succeed with every sector blank and every `is_fno` "false", while TASK3 asks for a stop (related A02 F-35)

(the reference files, sector index members included); TASK3 RI12 L226: "Missing index_members/: exit 2 with message" (IS-90). (2) The refresh docstring RI2 and RI3, L14 to L15 (17-Apr-2026): the sector from the sector files and `is_fno` from `fno_symbols.csv`; it states no rule for a missing file (IS-89). (3) TASK3 L220 to L221 (17-Apr-2026): with real sector data the risk engine's "sector concentration check will now function" (the use is A09's; IS-177, in IS-90's file). The conflict IC-14. A02 F-35 (MATCH for the file set; A02's) lists the silent skips as its handoff point 2 (IS-134). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L112 to L130 (L121 to L123: an absent file is skipped), L133 to L145 (L136 to L137: an absent list gives an empty set), L387 to L390 (the printed counts), L248 to L271 (the validation reads neither); all `bcf03b5` except the validation (`03ac3b0`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: an absent sector file is skipped with no message; an absent `fno_symbols.csv` gives an empty set with no message; an absent `index_members/` directory makes the 12 sector files and the F&O list all absent; only the counts printed at L390 (0) show it (P07-S015, P07-S018, P07-S076). The validation tests neither the number of sectors nor the number of F&O flags (P07-S035), so such a run writes the file and records SUCCESS. INTERPRETATION (P07-S033, P07-S093): every blank sector becomes "UNKNOWN" in the cache's `sector` accessor; `is_fno` has no Live reader (IN-06). HISTORICAL: the last tracked file has 142 rows with a sector and 206 with `is_fno` "true" (P07-S025), so the files were present for that file's run.
- **Evidence:** P07-S015, P07-S018, P07-S021, P07-S025, P07-S033, P07-S035, P07-S076, P07-S093; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. A source states a stop for missing inputs and the code skips them without notice; the run's success signal does not reflect the loss. A02 F-35's MATCH for the file set is kept; this is A05's side of its point 2. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: the code has no mechanism for stopping on a missing input ((a) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent; whether the inputs were ever missing on the machine is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (IR-09, IR-14); the effect on sector-based consumers is theirs (A09, A18).
- **Owner:** A05 (the refresh); A02 (the files; F-35 unchanged); A09, A18 (consumers of the sector).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 26; P07 point 3; STUDY question 32; IC-14; related: A02 F-35 (MATCH for the file set; A02's, unchanged); IN-15 (the "UNKNOWN" sector).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 32 bears on treatment).

##### IR-07 — The output checks let through what they are meant to guard against: a required symbol written with token 0 counts as present, a smaller dump passes while at most 20 percent of symbols are missing from it, and repeated symbols, repeated dump rows and shared tokens are neither detected nor reported

- **Area and flow:** `validate_instrument_rows` and the join's handling of repeats, before the write.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (missing validation); Failure handling (silent fallback).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) FIX-134 item 40, commit `03ac3b0` (31-May-2026): "post-refresh validation (count + required symbols + zero-token %)"; the function's docstring L254: "Validate instrument data after refresh"; the ten required symbols L60 to L63 (IS-92). (2) RI12, L25 (17-Apr-2026): "Symbols not matched in Kite: token=0, lot_size=1, tick_size=0.05" (IS-89). (3) TASK3 RI12 L229 to L231 (17-Apr-2026): "Empty Kite response: exit 1 (token may be stale)"; "Partial data: NEVER write incomplete instruments.csv (atomic rename only after full success)" (IS-90). (4) The alias tool's comment `scripts/find_symbol_aliases.py` L73 (07-May-2026, `4fcf984`): token "0" is skipped as one of the "invalid tokens" (IS-178). No source states the 20 percent figure or a rule for repeats (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`, section 6.2, question 25; section 6.1, point 27). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L92 to L109 (no de-duplication), L148 to L156 (the dump lookup, "later wins"), L175 to L204 (one row per listed symbol), L258 to L269 (the three rules), L400 to L403 (the WARNING).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the required-symbol rule (L261 to L264) tests only that the ten texts appear among the rows' symbols, so a required symbol written with token 0 passes it (P07-S035); the token-0 rule fails only above 20 percent of rows (L268); an empty dump fails it at 100 percent (P07-S047; IR-21, match). INTERPRETATION (P07-S047): a dump that answers successfully but lacks some symbols passes when the missing share is at most 20 percent, and the missing symbols are written as RI12 rows; nothing tells such rows from symbols the broker never listed. INTERPRETATION (list and dictionary semantics, P07-S013 (b), P07-S020, P07-S027; P02-S068, P02-S093): a symbol listed twice in the security master gives two identical rows; two dump rows of one `tradingsymbol` in the accepted segments leave the later one with no notice; every unmatched symbol shares token 0; no rule compares the new rows with the previous file. HISTORICAL: the last tracked file had no repeated symbol and no repeated non-zero token, and 144 token-0 rows of 2228 (6.5 percent) (P07-S025, P07-S027).
- **Evidence:** P07-S013, P07-S020, P07-S024, P07-S025, P07-S027, P07-S034, P07-S035, P07-S047; P02-S068, P02-S069, P02-S093; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The token-0 rows themselves are RI12's (IR-19, match); the ISSUE is that the checks FIX-134 added cannot see a required symbol that is unusable or a dump that is incomplete, and that repeats pass silently into a file whose loader also keeps "the later row" (IN-03): a demonstrated weakness of a kind Document 04, section 5.3 lists (missing validation). KNOWN DEFECT not considered: no mechanism is meant to test a required symbol's token or the dump's completeness.
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent; the dump of the legacy period and the deployed file's content are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (IR-14). The cache's treatment of token-0 rows is IN-02's.
- **Owner:** A05 (the refresh's checks).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 27; P07 points 4 and 17; P02 point 12 (refresh side); STUDY question 25; related: IN-02, IN-03 (the cache side).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 25 bears on treatment).

##### IR-08 — The writer and the loader disagree on what a valid file is: the refresh checks no tick or lot size, so a dump value of 0 or below for the tick, or below 1 for the lot, passes its validation and replaces the file, which the loader then refuses whole at the next start; `nan` and `inf` ticks pass both (related A04 CG-27)

- **Area and flow:** the tick and lot values from the dump to `config/instruments.csv`, and the file's next load by the trading process.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (two modules disagreeing on a value's validity); Weakness (missing validation).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) FIX-134 item 40, the validation's docstring L254 (31-May-2026, `03ac3b0`): "Validate instrument data after refresh" (IS-92). (2) The cache's IC12, `core/instrument_cache.py` L25 to L26 (17-Apr-2026): `ConfigSchemaError` "if any row is malformed" (IS-76), applied to `lot_size < 1` and `tick_size <= 0` (P05-S028). (3) A04 CG-27 (MATCH; A04's): "the instrument cache's refusal of a non-positive tick, on which the helpers' safety rests" (IS-136). (4) TASK3 RI5 L145 to L149 (17-Apr-2026): the Kite dump "has the REAL lot size" (IS-90). (5) Commit `c3c1242` (21-Jun-2026): a refresh must not end as a success unless the write is good (IS-94). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L181, L184 to L185 (the values), L248 to L271 (no tick or lot rule), L420 (the write); `core/instrument_cache.py` L164 to L177 (part IN's); `main.py` L2440 to L2449, L2506 (A01 C9's path).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the writer takes the tick and the lot size from the dump (`float`, `int`) or the literals 0.05 and 1 for an unmatched symbol, and tests neither anywhere (P07-S028, P07-S029). INTERPRETATION (P05-S028, P05-S029, P05-S053): a dump row with a tick of 0 or below, or a lot size below 1, is written and the run records SUCCESS; the previous file has already been replaced (L215); at the next start the loader raises `ConfigSchemaError` for the whole file, the cache is `None`, and the start ends through A01 C9's path (exit 2); a `nan` or positive `inf` tick (the library parses the dump's `tick_size` with `float`, `connect.py` L829, published 5.1.0 source) is written as the text `nan` or `inf` and accepted by both the writer and the loader, because `tick_size <= 0` is False for both (L171; P05-S029; IN-03); a `-inf` tick is below 0 and refused like any non-positive value. The cache's refusal on which A04 CG-27 rests is therefore real for a file this writer produces, but it acts one load later and on the whole file, after the good file is gone.
- **Evidence:** P07-S028, P07-S029, P07-S034, P07-S035, P07-S082; P05-S028, P05-S029, P05-S053, P05-S085; P02-S068; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The writer's validation accepts a file that the loader's stated contract refuses (Document 04, section 5.3: two modules disagreeing on a value; missing validation). A04 CG-27's MATCH is kept: the loader does refuse a non-positive tick; this record is A05's writer side. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: the writer has no tick or lot mechanism ((a) fails); whether the dump ever held such a value is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent; the dump's values in the legacy period are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (IR-14); the boot outcome is A01 C9's (KNOWN DEFECT; A01's, unchanged).
- **Owner:** A05 (the writer's checks and the loader's rule); A04 (CG-27, unchanged); A01 (C9's path).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 27; P07 point 5; related: A04 CG-27 (MATCH; A04's, unchanged); IN-03, IN-20 (the loader side); A01 C9 (unchanged).
- **Rama:** not needed.
