- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L51 to L53 (the defaults), L92 to L145 (the loaders: no age, date or row-count test), L207 to L218 (the write: seven columns, no as-of field).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: all 18 tracked files under `config/reference_data/` were added at `bcf03b5` (17-Apr-2026) and none has changed since; no code in the repository downloads or rewrites them; no file name or header carries an as-of date (P07-S054 to P07-S056). The output has no timestamp, as-of date, source identity, version or row count; its only date is the file's modification time, which the pre-flight grades (P07-S041; IS-97). INTERPRETATION (P07-S057): with the tracked copies, a symbol listed after the security master was taken is absent from the output, one renamed or delisted is written with token 0 if the dump no longer lists it, and changes of index membership or F&O eligibility are not reflected, while the tokens, lots and ticks are those of the day's dump. Whether the machine's copies were the tracked ones, and how current they were at any refresh, is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P07-S056).
- **Evidence:** P07-S041, P07-S054 to P07-S057, P07-S086; IS-96, RS-10, RS-11.
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. Source (1) ties the refresh to NSE's additions and removals, which a run can reflect only if the security master on the machine is renewed, but no source says whether, by whom or how often the files were to be renewed; without that intent, and without the machine's copies, no defect is demonstrated. The missing as-of date in the output is recorded here as part of the same question (no source asks for one).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established.
- **Owner:** A05 (the reference data's use and the output's content); A02 (the files).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 27; P07 points 6 and 18; STUDY question 30; RS-10, RS-11; IR-12 (how often the refresh runs).
- **Rama:** needed: STUDY question 30 (OPEN FOR RAMA): were the files under `config/reference_data/` meant to be renewed after 17-Apr-2026, and if so by whom and how often?

##### IR-10 — An alias may point at a text the writer cannot produce: the refresh matches the security master's text exactly to the dump's `tradingsymbol`, with no series or suffix mapping, while the alias file maps a Chartink name to a Kite "-BE" text (IC-15)

- **Area and flow:** symbol identity on the writer side (I1), and the alias file's targets against the file the writer produces (A06 owns the consumer).
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (two sources disagree on what a symbol text is); Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `config/symbol_aliases.yaml` L1 to L7 (07-May-2026): Chartink sends names "that don't match Zerodha's official trading symbols"; "Format: chartink_name: zerodha_name"; commit `435b3a2` (07-May-2026): "add SIGMAADV alias (Chartink sends base, Kite has -BE series)" (IS-100). (2) The refresh docstring RI1 and RI4 (17-Apr-2026): the security master defines the universe and Kite provides the token "per symbol" (IS-89). (3) TASK3 L90 (17-Apr-2026): `symbol` "from Kite (tradingsymbol)" (IS-90). (4) The alias tool L83 to L84 (07-May-2026): candidates are texts of `instruments.csv`, to "Review and add to config/symbol_aliases.yaml" (IS-99). The conflict IC-15 (neither names the other). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L101 (the stripped security-master text), L152 to L155 (the dump keyed by `tradingsymbol` as given), L176, L195 (the written `symbol`); `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L276 to L281, L701, L792 (A06's; reader named only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the written `symbol` is always the security master's text; a token is found only when the dump has exactly the same `tradingsymbol` in segment "NSE" or "NSE-EQ"; the security master's series is used only to filter, never to build the broker's text (P07-S026, P07-S084). INTERPRETATION (conditional on the dump's form, which is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED): if the dump lists a BE-series stock as "<base>-BE", the security master's plain text gets token 0, and an alias value "<base>-BE" is a symbol of the written file only if the security master lists that exact text. HISTORICAL (own count-only comparison, P07-S073): the second alias value ends in "-BE" and is not a symbol of the last tracked file (which predates the alias file); whether the security master lists it was not checked (its data rows were not read). What the consumer and the cache then do with an alias target is A06's and part IN's (IN-23).
- **Evidence:** P07-S026, P07-S071 to P07-S073, P07-S084, P07-S095; P05-S047, P05-S089; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. A conflict between sources that the code resolves one way: the writer admits only security-master texts matched exactly, while the alias file's own record expects the broker's series text as a target. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: whether the case arose depends on the dump and the security master's rows, neither established.
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent; the deployed alias file and instrument file are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (IR-14).
- **Owner:** A05 (symbol identity in the file); A06 (the alias consumer); A02 (the alias file).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 27; P07 points 7 and 19; STUDY question 27; IC-15; IN-23 (the cache side, A04 RD-33 kept).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 27 bears on treatment).

##### IR-11 — The post-write check runs after the rename: it cannot catch the failure mode it was added for, it cannot fail in a normal run, and when it does fail the new file stays in place while the job is recorded FAILED (IC-12)

- **Area and flow:** the write and the post-write check (steps 11 and 12 of `main`), and the 21-Jun-2026 staleness record.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (a check that cannot fire for its stated case); Flow and state (a status inconsistent with the file's state).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) Commit `c3c1242` (21-Jun-2026): a post-write assertion that makes the script "exit non-zero on a missing/stale/truncated/malformed write", "so a silent no-write is caught by the cron heartbeat/marker (the failure mode that froze instruments 7 weeks)" (IS-94); the docstring L223 to L228 (same commit): it "Confirms the file was ACTUALLY written fresh" (IS-84). (2) Commit `2b4d976` (21-Jun-2026) and `.gitignore` L35 to L40: the deploy hook's `git checkout -f` "silently reverts the VM's fresh copy", so the file is untracked (IS-84, IS-95). The two give different mechanisms for one event (IC-12); the code-only contradiction CD-29. HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L207 to L218 (`_write_csv`, `bcf03b5`), L221 to L245 and L421 to L424 (`_check_fresh_write` and its call, `c3c1242`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the check runs only after `_write_csv` returned, that is after `tmp_path.replace(csv_path)` succeeded; it tests existence, an age of at most 600 s, at least 1000 bytes, UTF-8 and "symbol" in the first line; on failure `main` prints the reason and returns 1 with the new file in place (P07-S037, P07-S039). INTERPRETATION (P07-S040; POSIX rename semantics): in this code a write either lands or raises, and a raise already ends the run with exit 1, so a "silent no-write" by the script cannot reach the check; a validated file of at least 1000 rows passes every test; the check fails only on a forward clock jump of more than 600 s, a file-system time far from the process clock, or another process changing the file in the instant between; it cannot see a revert after the script exits, which is source (2)'s mechanism. MATCH part: the five tests are implemented as the docstring lists them. What happened on the machine in June 2026 is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P07-S092).
- **Evidence:** P07-S037 to P07-S040, P07-S092; P05-S106; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The check does what its tests say (match part) but cannot detect the failure it was added for, and a failure leaves the job FAILED with the new file already live. The untracking (source (2)) addresses the other mechanism and is A17's deploy side. No KNOWN DEFECT: no case the check is said to handle is shown to arise in which it fails by semantics.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor; the event of June 2026 is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05 (the refresh); A17 (the deploy hook and the untracking); A20 (the pre-flight's date grading, the only later look at the file's age).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 26; P07 points 9 and 20; IC-12, CD-29; IN-05 (the cache side of the 21-Jun-2026 staleness).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### IR-12 — The documents disagree on how often the refresh runs: "on first setup and whenever NSE adds/removes symbols", "weekly", "daily" and "09:00 Mon-Fri"; the later declaration is daily at 09:00, while the docstring and TASK3 were not updated

- **Area and flow:** the refresh's intended frequency and time (the declaration itself is A16's).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code and its declaration do not follow; documents not updated).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The refresh docstring L10 (17-Apr-2026): "Run on first setup and whenever NSE adds/removes symbols" (IS-89). (2) TASK3 RI14 L240 to L244 (17-Apr-2026): "Weekly minimum. Daily for F&O trading days"; L297 to L302 and L330 to L331: a manual first run, "Recurring weekly" (IS-90, RS-11). (3) MODULE 38 IC2 L47 to L53 (17-Apr-2026): "Operator runs this daily" (IS-91). (4) The archived crontab, `docs/archive/CRON_FIX_2026_05_18.md` L72 to L73 (lines 19-May-2026, `4ac1238`): "18:00 IST Sunday | weekly instrument master refresh" (P07-S064; IS-176). (5) The registry, `config/cron_registry.yaml` L306 to L320 (lines 18-Jun-2026 to 23-Jun-2026; identifier-bearing lines cited by number only): `0 9 * * 1-5`, `market_day_only: true`, `critical: true` (IS-95); the `.gitignore` comment L35 (21-Jun-2026): "regenerated on the VM daily" (IS-95); `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L624: 09:00 Monday to Friday (P07-S064). (6) Current external context (read 14-Nov-2026 and 15-Nov-2026; not a legacy-period source): the vendor advises fetching the dump once a day, "ideally at around 08:30 AM" (P07-S020). The later sources (5) agree with one another; none names the earlier ones. HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L429 to L446 (the cron entry; no frequency in the script); the declared line `deploy/cron/trading-system.cron` L72 to L73 (A16's; cited by number only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the script has no schedule of its own; its entry skips non-trading days (L433 to L434), so a Sunday run such as source (4)'s would record SKIPPED and do nothing under the code at the baseline (the guard was added on 18-Jun-2026, after that crontab) (P07-S051, P07-S052). The declarations follow source (5) (P07-S059). INTERPRETATION: on a declared day the refresh runs after the pre-flight's phase A (08:30), which grades the file as "today's 09:00 refresh pending" (P07-S060), and after the 08:15 token refresh it depends on. Whether, when and with what result the refresh ran is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (IR-14).
- **Evidence:** P07-S020, P07-S051, P07-S052, P07-S059, P07-S060, P07-S064; P05-S101; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). HISTORICAL; FACT; INTERPRETATION. The declaration (A16's) follows the latest sources; the docstring's rule and TASK3's weekly cadence no longer describe the system (Document 04, section 5.3, first kind). No KNOWN DEFECT (documents only).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none on behaviour established.
- **Owner:** A05 (the refresh's documents); A16 (the declaration).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 points 24 (the cache side, IN-05) and 36; RS-11; IR-09 (the reference files' renewal, a separate question); Record 01 R01-14 ("refreshed daily", while the declaration is Monday to Friday on market days; P11-S025), which part XC (XC-07) counts among the PARTLY AGREES disposed of by this record.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### IR-13 — The alias tool's own example is not reachable by its rules, nor is the first tracked alias from its key; it reads the header row as data and the file in the platform's default encoding, and a legacy audit called it an orphan without a later decision

- **Area and flow:** `scripts/find_symbol_aliases.py`: loading `config/instruments.csv`, scoring candidates, printing proposals for manual review.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a comment that does not describe the code); Weakness (input read without its format).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The docstring L3 (07-May-2026, `4fcf984`): "Find potential Zerodha symbol matches for failed Chartink symbols"; the output header L83 to L84: "Symbol alias candidates (YAML format)", "Review and add to config/symbol_aliases.yaml"; the comment L35: "TVSSCS -> TVSSRICHAK (starts with TVSSCS, then adds suffix)" (IS-99; CD-30). (2) The alias file and its commits (07-May-2026): Chartink names mapped to Zerodha names (IS-100). (3) AUDIT-05JUL L105, L127 (lines 06-Jul-2026): the tool among "Orphan scripts (zero code/doc/test references)", with a proposal to delete it (IS-101). (4) The comment L73: token "0" skipped as invalid (IS-178). No later source records a decision to keep or remove the tool (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`, section 6.2, question 33). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/find_symbol_aliases.py` L14 to L57 (`fuzzy_match`, `find_candidates`), L60 to L78 (the load), L81 to L105 (input and output); one commit, `4fcf984` (07-May-2026).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: four rules (equal 1.0; prefix 0.95; substring 0.9; a ratio above 0.8), at most five candidates, and a best score of at least 0.9 printed as a plain YAML line (L90 to L94); the file is opened with no encoding argument and read with `csv.reader`, keeping every row with at least two fields and a second field other than "0" (L68 to L74); the tool writes no file (P07-S066, P07-S068, P07-S070). INTERPRETATION (P07-S069, P07-S095; `csv`, `open` and `difflib` semantics): the header row is kept as the pair ("symbol", "instrument_token"), so "symbol" can be offered as a candidate and the printed count is one too high; the decoding is the platform default; rules 1 to 3 are case-sensitive and nothing normalises punctuation, spacing or a series suffix; prefix and substring matches count as high confidence, so a one- or two-letter input can match many symbols at 0.95 or 0.9, of which five are returned; the example at L35 is not a prefix, a substring or a ratio above 0.8 (ratio 0.625), and the first tracked alias is not reachable from its key by any rule, so the tool could not have proposed either; the second alias is reachable (prefix, 0.95) only from a file that lists its "-BE" value (IR-10). FACT: no test, no declaration and no code calls the tool; it is unchanged at the baseline (P07-S074). Whether it was ever run is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P07-S065 to P07-S070, P07-S073, P07-S074, P07-S095; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The comment's example contradicts the rules it illustrates (CD-30), and the load reads a non-symbol text as a symbol; the output is meant for manual review (IR-30, match), which limits the effect. The orphan listing is a record that was not acted on, not a defect by itself (STUDY question 33). KNOWN DEFECT not considered: the tool has no mechanism to skip the header ((a) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor; whether the tool was run, under which locale and on which file, is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (IR-14).
- **Owner:** A05 (the tool); A06 (the alias consumer); A02 (the alias file).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 29; P07 points 19 and 21; STUDY question 33; CD-30; part XC (the tool's working-directory path, STUDY 18.1 point 33).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 33 bears on treatment).

## 11. Part DC — The daily candles script, the `candles` table and the candle CSV (with the storage handoffs for candles)

### 11.1 Scope and method of this part

- **Scope.** STUDY sections 10 (the daily script) and 11 (the storage handoffs for candles) and evidence part P08 (`evidence/A05_DAILY_CANDLES_AND_FNO_BAN_SCRIPTS_2026-11-14.md`), the parts on `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` (P08-S005 to P08-S049, P08-S072 to P08-S076, P08-S079 to P08-S091, P08-S094 to P08-S098); evidence part P03 (the live writer) for comparison only. The daily script's run flow, outcomes and heartbeat; what it requests (index loop, traded-symbol query, stock loop, pacing, exchange and map); what it writes and hands over (`candles` rows through its own `INSERT OR IGNORE`, the CSV `data_store/candles/candle_data_<date>.csv`), the meaning of the stored stamp (the stored side of STUDY 18.1 point 14), and the readers of these outputs at the interface. STUDY 18.1 points 30 and 31; the stored side of point 14; the daily script's side of points 19 and 35 (the table) and of P02 points 9 to 11 (passed through parts LF and HA; section 11.2.3); the daily script's documents in point 36; point 37 for the inherited items below. P08 points 1 to 13 and the daily script's side of point 23 (point 14 was withdrawn in the check: D-07). STUDY 18.2 items for the daily script (3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, 11 and 13 in part). STUDY questions 8 (stored side) and 34 to 37. Inherited items assigned to this part: A03 D-05, Q-16, Y-06 with OIR A03-43, G-12, H-16; A04 CL-12, CL-15, RL-06 with OIR A04-28, RD-21. ### 11.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### DC-01 — The index fetch is called "FAIL-SAFE by construction" and said never to raise, but its database insert is outside its `try`, so a storage error while storing index rows also stops that day's stock candles, the CSV and the later dates of a backfill

- **Area and flow:** the daily run: `_fetch_single_day` calls `_fetch_indices` first; the index rows are stored by `_insert_into_candles_db`; only then the traded-symbol query, the stock loop, the CSV and the stock insert.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (an error path not isolated as stated); Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `_fetch_indices` docstring L72 to L74 (17-Jul-2026, `4e2df39`): "FAIL-SAFE by construction: a missing token or a failed fetch logs and continues; this function never raises, so it can never break the stock-candle ingestion below or the caller"; the comment L156 to L158 (same commit): "Fail-safe (never raises), so the traded-stock path below is reached and behaves exactly as before"; the comment L104: "fail-safe: never let an index fetch break stock ingestion". (2) Commit `4e2df39` (17-Jul-2026): "a missing/failed index fetch logs and continues, never raises, so it cannot break stock ingestion or the boot"; "Stock ingestion byte-identical". (3) The regime document L89 to L90 (17-Jul-2026): "Fail-safe by construction: a missing token or a failed fetch logs and continues; the function never raises". (4) Record 02 R02-50 (18-Sep-2026): `_fetch_indices` "never raises" (taken from the docstring). (5) The index test (17-Jul-2026) covers a failing fetch only. HISTORICAL (1) to (5). Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) and (2) IS-104, (3) IS-107, (4) IS-128, (5) IS-111; the contradiction CD-32.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L159 (first statement of `_fetch_single_day`, no `try`), L89 to L106 (the `try` covers the request and the row building only), L107 to L108 (the insert, outside it), L294 to L326 (only each `execute` is guarded, against `sqlite3.IntegrityError`; `db_connect.connect` L300, `commit` L324 and `close` L325 unguarded), L288 to L289 (the date loop, no `try`), L336 to L341 (the heartbeat block); `core/db_connect.py` L52 to L64 (A03's: a 30-second timeout and busy wait; the attach creates an empty `analytics.db` if it is missing but not its tables, P08-S040).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): a configured name missing from the dump is printed and skipped, and a failing request is printed and the loop goes on, as the docstring states (L85 to L88, L104 to L106); the stock path is then reached. ISSUE part (FACT and INTERPRETATION, Python and SQLite semantics): an exception raised while storing the index rows, for example `sqlite3.OperationalError` for a database locked beyond the 30-second wait or "no such table" when `analytics.db` lacks its tables, leaves `_fetch_indices` and `_fetch_single_day`: the day's traded-symbol query, stock requests, CSV and stock insert are not reached, none of the index call's rows are committed, the later dates of a backfill are not processed, and the run ends with a traceback, status 1 and a FAILED heartbeat with the exception's class name. A malformed date also raises at L80 before any stock work when the index list is not empty. So since 17-Jul-2026 the stock path, which before that date did not depend on any index storage, depends on the index insert not raising: the stock code text is unchanged ("byte-identical"), but its reachability is not ("behaves exactly as before" does not hold for this case).
- **Evidence:** P08-S024, P08-S025, P08-S036, P08-S039, P08-S040, P08-S090; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds only in part (the per-name `try` and the "fail-safe by construction" design are the mechanism for a failed fetch, but the `try` does not enclose the insert, and the insert's only handler covers `IntegrityError`, so no mechanism is implemented for a storage error itself); (b) holds (by Python semantics any exception other than `IntegrityError` from the connect, an execute or the commit propagates whenever it occurs); (c) not established: the docstring claims that the function never raises in any case, but whether a storage error (a lock held beyond 30 seconds, a missing analytics table, an I/O error) can arise under the documented set-up (the 15:40 run, with the analytics tables created by the trading process's schema step, P08-S040) is not shown; (d) holds (every source found states that the function never raises).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether the case occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. When it occurs the run is reported as FAILED (unlike DC-02), and that day's stock candles are missing from both the CSV and the table.
- **Owner:** A05 (the script); A03 (the connector and the schema creation, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 30; P08 points 1 and 13; CD-32; STUDY section 16, A03 Y-06 row (P08-S025), Y-06's classification unchanged; DC-02; DC-06; DC-12.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### DC-02 — A run in which symbols fail, nothing is fetched or nothing is stored ends with exit 0 and a SUCCESS heartbeat; the functional status that the 15-Jul-2026 monitoring fix recommends for this job ("candles fetched") is never set

- **Area and flow:** the daily run's outcomes: the per-symbol `failed` list, the return value of `main`, the cron entry's `HeartbeatTimer`, the per-job alert, and what the table shows afterwards.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (errors only printed; a degraded run reported as clean); Intended against actual (a stated, recommended behaviour not implemented).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) Commit `75d5e99` (18-Jun-2026, TASK #3): "8 silent jobs instrumented (holiday-skip + heartbeat + alert)", `fetch_daily_candles` among them; the alert "FAILED always; SUCCESS only for critical jobs" (`utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L110 to L113, same commit). (2) The heartbeat helper's usage text, `utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L100 to L108 (02-Jun-2026, `f1dc556`): the job sets `hb.status = "PARTIAL"` "if something_wrong". (3) F2, `utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L24 to L31 (15-Jul-2026, `795a417`): "A job whose script exits 0 but whose real function failed (empty CSV, undelivered email) records status=SUCCESS + functional_status=<the real outcome>, so a delivery/artifact failure can no longer read as SUCCESS"; commit `795a417` names the root cause "green heartbeat, dead work" and says it "does not convert all 44 jobs". (4) `docs/audit/monitoring_hardening_15jul2026.md` L71 to L90 (15-Jul-2026, `1c891b6`): the F2 list gives `fetch_daily_candles` the "Recommended functional criterion" "job-specific completion (candles fetched ...)", as an "incremental fast-follow", "one `timer.functional_status = ...` line each". (5) The registry (A16's declaration) L359 to L373: `critical: false`, `monitored: true`. HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-93, IS-122 (the helper's L110 to L113 are also in IS-201); (2) IS-201; (3) IS-200; (4) IS-202; (5) IS-204 (A16's declaration). STUDY question 35 narrowed there.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L85 to L88, L104 to L105 (index failures printed), L176 to L208 (`failed`), L222 to L223 (printed only), L226 to L291 (`main` returns None on every path that does not exit), L329 to L342; `utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L80 to L82 (nothing written when the database file is missing), L126 to L127 (status SUCCESS, functional status None by default), L140 to L173 (A16's helper, interface only). Search: no `functional_status` or `PARTIAL` assignment in the script; the non-test setters of a functional status are other jobs (`scripts/generate_screened_stocks_csv.py`, `scripts/monitoring_canary.py`, `scripts/forward_shadow_record.py`, `signals/webhook_receiver.py`, `reports/daily_trade_review.py`), and `scripts/backup_retention.py` L290 sets `PARTIAL`; `scripts/forward_shadow_record.py` L186 (25-Jul-2026, `804ab16`) is a job on the same F2 row as this one.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): the job is instrumented as TASK #3 intends: a non-trading day records SKIPPED; a `sys.exit(1)` or an uncaught exception records FAILED and, with `alert=True`, sends the per-job alert. ISSUE part (FACT and INTERPRETATION): a name not found, an empty answer or a failing request only puts the symbol in a printed list; `main` never returns a non-zero value and the script sets neither `PARTIAL` nor a functional status, so a run in which every symbol fails, nothing is fetched, or nothing is stored ends with exit 0 and a SUCCESS heartbeat, and under `critical: false` no alert (P08-S098's table). When the database file is missing, nothing is stored and no heartbeat row is written at all. The printed lines go to the declared cron log, which no code reads (P12-S053). INTERPRETATION (the readers are A15's): a day for which only index rows, or only some symbols, were stored also reads as present to the readers that test for rows (P08-S074). So the class of failure that F2 names, a clean execution status for a function that failed, remains for this job at the baseline, while F2's own list names this job and its criterion.
- **Evidence:** P08-S018 to P08-S021, P08-S028, P08-S074, P08-S098; P12-S045, P12-S053; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The later sources (15-Jul-2026) name this job and the criterion, as a planned "incremental fast-follow", not as done; the script was changed after them (17-Jul-2026, `4e2df39`; 09-Sep-2026, `b6df768`) without a functional status being set, while another job of the same F2 row was given one (25-Jul-2026). The ISSUE part rests on F2's stated aim that a failed function "can no longer read as SUCCESS" and on the weakness the code shows (failures only printed). KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism for reporting a functional failure exists in the script: condition (a) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether any run failed in part is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. Compared with FB-03 (ISSUE (minor)): both rest on the same 15-Jul-2026 monitoring basis, the F2 list (`docs/audit/monitoring_hardening_15jul2026.md` L71 to L90, `1c891b6`, IS-202; L83 names `fetch_fno_ban`, L87 `fetch_daily_candles`; with `utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L24 to L31 and commit `795a417`, IS-200). FB-03's reason for "minor", that the stored list has no reader (FB-14), does not hold for this job: the `candles` table and the CSV have readers (the daily report and the readers that test for rows; DC-06, P08-S074), and under `critical: false` a degraded daily run sends no alert, where a ban-list soft failure sends a WARN alert (FB-03, FB-12). So FB-03's severity word is not carried over; no severity word is given here, because the impact depends on runtime evidence that does not survive.
- **Owner:** A05 (the script's outcomes); A16 (the heartbeat helper, the officer), A14 (the per-job alert policy) and A15 (the readers), interface only.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 points 30 and 35 (table side); P08 point 2 (daily side); STUDY questions 35 and 37; part XC (P12 points 5, 11 and 12: the completion signals as a whole); part FB (the ban-list side of P08 point 2); DC-01; DC-06.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 35 bears on treatment).

##### DC-03 — The stored `ts` of the daily script is the broker's label written as naive wall-clock digits, the form a later source states; the schema comment calls `ts` the "ISO-8601 IST candle close", the current vendor examples read the label as the minute's start, and the live writer stores a close-time stamp under the same field and key (the stored side of A03 Y-06)

- **Area and flow:** the meaning and form of the time handed to storage: the `ts` of the `candles` rows and the `datetime` of the CSV written by the daily script, and the readers of them.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (one field, different meanings across writers); Logic and calculation (time and time zone); Intended against actual (a schema comment the code does not follow).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `core/analytics_schema.sql` L28 (14-Jun-2026, `f172171`, relocated; the comment first written 19-May-2026, `dfdeec2`, as A03 Y-06 records; A03's): `ts TEXT NOT NULL -- ISO-8601 IST candle close`. (2) `core/state_store.py` L2105 to L2113 (28-Jun-2026, `4d60ab4`; A03's): `candles.ts` "is stored naive-IST ('YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS', the 15:40 EOD backfill)"; "Candle STORAGE format is unchanged". (3) The other side of the conflict on what a candle's time marks: `resample` (12-Jul-2026): "the bar's `ts` = the bucket START"; the candle-math tests stamp candles at their start; the 24-Jul document reads stored one-minute labels as minute starts; the `Candle` type allows aware or naive IST (27-Jun-2026; A08's). (4) A03 Y-06 with OIR A03-43: the stored form is A03's; Rama's Phase 2 input: one canonical, time-zone-defined stored form, chosen in Phase 2, "the meaning of the time stays A05's". HISTORICAL (1) to (3); project records (4). Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-106; (2) IS-187 (A03 Y-06's source (2)); (3) IS-54, IS-59, IS-60, IS-74; the conflict IC-13; (4) IS-135, RS-04. STUDY question 8 OPEN QUESTION (conflict) narrowed there.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L97 and L193 (`c["date"].strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")`), L313 (the `ts` value), L211 to L216 (the CSV `datetime`); the published library's `_format_historical`, `kiteconnect/connect.py` L655 to L665 (`dateutil.parser.parse`, part P02); readers `reports/daily_report.py` L165 to L175 (`ts[11:16]`) and L396 (`datetime[11:16]`); `core/state_store.py` `_parse_ist_dt` L77 to L95 (a naive value taken as IST) (A03's and A15's, interface only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT and INTERPRETATION): the text is the naive `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` form that source (2) states as the stored form; for a server string with a `+0530` suffix its digits are IST wall-clock digits, and the state store's reader takes a naive value as IST. ISSUE part (INTERPRETATION): the text is not the ISO-8601 form with a `T` and a zone that the schema comment names, and it records no zone at all: the digits are those of whatever offset the server's string carried, the offset dropped, at whole seconds. Its meaning is the broker's label: on the vendor's current examples (a first candle labelled 09:15:00 at the open; current external context, read 14-Nov-2026) and on A18's `truncate.py` arithmetic it marks the start of the minute it covers, while the schema comment says "candle close" and the live writer's `ts` is the host's IST time when the close ran, after the minute ended (CA-04). The legacy-period convention of the labels is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, so the difference is not established for the legacy data; on the current reading the two writers' rows for one market minute would differ by about one minute plus the close's delay, and the text key would not merge them even with one text form. The report's `HH:MM` keys then name the minute's start for this writer and the minute after for a live-written row. No source states one meaning for the stored rows of both writers, or who handles a difference. A third writer, `scripts/reconstruct_excursions.py` L115 to L126 (A15's; recorded in A03 Y-06 and STUDY 18.1 point 35), writes the broker's label in the same naive form; it is not audited here. Whether the live writer ever wrote a row is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P08-S041, P08-S042, P08-S043, P08-S045, P08-S046, P08-S084; P02-S065, P02-S066; P03-S079, P03-S082, P03-S084; P06-S048 to P06-S052; P10-S086, P10-S087; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The daily script's form agrees with the later storage statement; the sources conflict on meaning across candle kinds (IC-13), and the two writers put different readings under one field and one key. This record is A05's side of A03 Y-06 for the daily script (the meaning, the source of the stamp); Y-06's classification, owners and treatment, and A03-43's status, are unchanged, and no stored format is chosen here. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism fails by semantics; the outcome depends on a server convention that is not established for the legacy period).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; the legacy labels' convention and whether any live-written row existed are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05 (the meaning and source of the stored stamp); A03 (the stored form and the key: Y-06, A03-43, unchanged); A15 (the readers' `HH:MM` keys); A18 (`truncate.py`).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 14 (stored side); P08 point 3; P02 point 9 (daily side); STUDY question 8; related A03 Y-06 and OIR A03-43; CA-04 (live side); HA-03 (historical side).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 8 bears on the intended meaning; A03-43 holds Rama's Phase 2 input on the stored form).

##### DC-04 — `INSERT OR IGNORE` skips a stored minute and a row with a null value alike, silently, and the printed "inserted" count includes the skipped rows; a re-run keeps the first-written values; the `IntegrityError` handler cannot be reached at the baseline schema (A03 Q-16's A05 side)

- **Area and flow:** `_insert_into_candles_db`: the statement, the counter and the message, for index and stock rows, in a first run and in a re-run or backfill over a stored date.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (rows dropped with no line; a count that misstates); Weakness (missing validation of the server's values).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The message L326 (19-May-2026, `b6cd512`): "Saved DB: {inserted} candle rows inserted into candles table". (2) The handler L322 to L323 (19-May-2026) and commit `7afbd25` (16-Jul-2026): "hoist the sqlite3 import so the IntegrityError handler cannot NameError": duplicates were meant to be tolerated without failing the run. (3) No source states whether a re-run should replace stored candles (STUDY question 36; `A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`, section 6.2: no source found). (4) A03 Q-16 (IS-135). HISTORICAL (1) and (2); project record (4). Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-184 (L326); (2) IS-184 (L322 to L323) and IS-185 (commit `7afbd25`); (4) IS-135.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L303 to L326 (statement L307 to L309; `inserted += 1` L321 after every execute that does not raise); L198 (`c["volume"]` passed unchanged for a stock); `core/analytics_schema.sql` L24 to L39 (NOT NULL columns, the UNIQUE key, no CHECK and no FOREIGN KEY; A03's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT and INTERPRETATION): a minute already stored does not fail the run; it is skipped, as the handler's presence shows was wanted. ISSUE part (INTERPRETATION, SQLite semantics, P08-S003): `OR IGNORE` skips without an exception both a row whose `(instrument_token, ts, 60)` is already stored and a row with a NULL in a NOT NULL column, so a `None` volume from the server for a stock, or a `None` price on either path, drops that candle with no line; the counter counts executes, so the printed number includes every skipped row; the `IntegrityError` handler is not reached by these cases at the baseline schema (HISTORICAL: commit `7afbd25`, 16-Jul-2026, itself records that "sqlite never raises IntegrityError and the handler is never evaluated"). A re-run for a stored date keeps the first-written values even where the server's values have changed (a correction, or a candle that was still forming at an earlier run, DC-05); whether that was intended is not established. Whether the server ever sent a null value is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P08-S036, P08-S038, P08-S082; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The ISSUE part rests on the silent drop and the miscount the code shows; the re-run rule has no intent source and is not an ISSUE by itself. This record is A05's side of A03 Q-16 for the daily script's own raw insert; Q-16's classification, owners and treatment are unchanged. KNOWN DEFECT considered for the count and not given: (a) holds (the counter and its message are the mechanism); (b) holds (it counts every execute that does not raise); (c) holds (a re-run or a backfill over a stored date is a documented use, `--backfill`); (d) not shown (the only text that states what the count reports is the mechanism's own output message, not an independent statement of intent; no other source states it). KNOWN DEFECT not considered for the null drop (no source says the script handles null values; condition (c) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; the server's null values and any re-run are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05 (the insert, the count, the input side); A03 (Q-16, the storage side, unchanged).

##### DC-05 — No check of the time of day: a run started before the session ends asks for a window that ends in the future, and any candle it stores is kept by the key when the scheduled run fetches the same minute again

- **Area and flow:** the run gate and the request windows of a default run and of an explicit date for today.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (missing validation); Flow and state (a partial day stored as final).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The module docstring L4 (18-May-2026, `044e5ba`): "Runs on the VM after market close (cron: 15:40 IST Mon-Fri)"; the declared schedule 15:40 (A16's; `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L622 to L626). (2) FIX-180, L229 to L232 (16-Jun-2026, `991f5c3`): the default run "fetches *today's* candles via the live broker session. Skip on weekend / Friday-after-17:30 when the Zerodha API is unavailable. Explicit historical fetches (--backfill or a date arg) are allowed any day — historical_data works for past dates". HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-103, IS-110; (2) IS-180.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L233 to L240 (the gate, default run only: `is_broker_api_available`), L253, L80 to L81, L167 to L168 (windows ending 15:31 of the trade date), L303 to L323.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the gate tests broker availability (weekends, Friday from 17:30, holiday rules), not whether the session has ended; an explicit date, including today's, is not gated at all, although FIX-180 gives "past dates" as the reason. INTERPRETATION: so a default run on a weekday before 15:30, or a run with today's date at any time of day, requests a window that ends in the future; whether the server then includes a still-forming candle is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P06-S041); whatever such a run stores is kept when a later run fetches the same minutes (DC-04), and the day then reads as present to the readers that test for rows (P08-S074). Under the declared 15:40 schedule the case does not arise.
- **Evidence:** P08-S013, P08-S014, P08-S031, P08-S038, P08-S074; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The stated intent places the run after the close and explicit fetches on past dates; no check enforces either. Latent: only a manual or misplaced run reaches the case. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism for the case exists: condition (a) fails; under the documented set-up the case does not arise).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether any early run occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 30; P08 point 5; DC-04; part HA (the still-forming candle on the closure, P06-S041).

##### DC-06 — Since 17-Jul-2026 index rows are stored in the same table under the date, so the daily report's CSV fallback, taken only when the table has no row for the date, is not taken when the stock insert failed after the CSV was written; the regime commit states that index rows are "safe for every candles-table reader" (A03 G-12's A05 side)

- **Area and flow:** the two handoffs of the stock candles (the CSV, then the table) and the daily report's choice between them.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (a stated property of the rows that one reader's condition does not have); Failure handling (a fallback that the stored rows can disable).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) Commit `b6cd512` (19-May-2026): "candle OHLC falls back to CSV (data_store/candles/); fetch_daily_candles.py now INSERTs into candles DB table"; the report's `_load_candle_csv` docstring L387 and the condition L177 (same commit; A15's): "Private fallback for when candles table is empty". (2) The `_fetch_indices` docstring L70 to L72 (17-Jul-2026, `4e2df39`): indices are not written to the CSV because "the daily report keys candles by trade symbol, so index rows would be dead keys there"; commit `4e2df39`: "the stock fetch/CSV/insert are untouched. Index rows are safe for every candles-table reader (all symbol/token-filtered; the daily report's get_candles_for_date map is keyed by trade symbol, so index rows are dead keys)". (3) A03 G-12 (IS-135): the CSV is written in place, before the insert. HISTORICAL (1) and (2); project record (3). Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-184 (the commit) and IS-188 (the report's L177 to L180, L387); (2) IS-104 (the docstring) and IS-190 (the commit's reader sentence); (3) IS-135.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L159 and L107 to L108 (index rows stored first), L210 to L218 (the CSV written in place with the platform default encoding, then the stock insert); `core/state_store.py` L2772 to L2779 (`get_candles_for_date`: every row of the date, no symbol filter); `reports/daily_report.py` L165 to L180 (the map built from every row; the CSV read only when the map is empty), L384 to L404 (A15's, interface only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): index rows are kept out of the CSV, and in the report's per-symbol lookups they are dead keys, as stated. ISSUE part (FACT and INTERPRETATION): the report's fallback condition is that the map built from all the date's rows is empty, and index rows make it non-empty; so for a date on which the index rows were stored and the stock insert failed (or the run stopped after the CSV was written), the report finds only index rows and does not read the CSV that holds the day's stock candles. The CSV fallback that `b6cd512` introduced for stock candles missing from the table is therefore disabled whenever index rows exist for the date, which the commit's "safe for every candles-table reader" does not cover. Independently of the index rows, after a re-run the CSV holds the latest run's rows alone while the table keeps every earlier row with its first-written values (DC-04), and an insert failure after the CSV write leaves a CSV with no matching rows (G-12, unchanged).
- **Evidence:** P08-S043 to P08-S046, P08-S085; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the fallback is the mechanism for stock candles missing from the table); (b) holds (whenever index rows exist for the date the fallback is not taken); (c) not established (the case needs a stock insert failure, or a stop after the CSV write, on a day whose index rows were stored; whether that can arise under the documented set-up is not shown); (d) fails: commit `4e2df39` states that index rows are safe for every reader, but the report's own docstring L387 states the fallback's condition as "when candles table is empty", which the code implements literally, so the outcome does not contradict every source. This record is A05's side of A03 G-12; G-12's classification, owners and treatment are unchanged.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; whether such a day occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05 (the writer and its two handoffs); A15 (the report's fallback condition); A03 (G-12, the write discipline, unchanged).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 30; P08 point 7; related A03 G-12; DC-01; DC-04; DC-07 (the documents about the CSV).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### DC-08 — "Traded symbols" are chosen by `date(triggered_at)`, which SQLite computes in UTC from the stored `+05:30` time, while the script's trade date is the IST date: a signal triggered between 00:00 and 05:29 IST is counted on the previous day

##### DC-10 — The daily script's broker calls are paced only by a fixed 0.35-second sleep after each attempted historical request, outside any limiter, with the library's 7-second default timeout and no retry; a failed symbol or date is not fetched again (A04 RL-06's A05 side)

- **Area and flow:** the requests of one run: `profile()`, `instruments("NSE")`, and one `historical_data` per found index and per found traded symbol for each date.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (no retry; a transient failure loses the symbol for the day); Consistency across components (pacing outside the shared limiter); Weakness (hardcoded pacing).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) G7 and RL2 (17-Apr-2026): a client-side token bucket per category that "Wraps every broker call"; "historical: 2/2"; "4-step backoff on 429: 1s/5s/30s/soft_kill"; "Independent buckets prevent a historical data burst from starving order placement". (2) Two later documents state about 3 requests per second for other historical fetchers (24-Jul-2026 and 13-Jul-2026). (3) A04 RL-06 with OIR A04-28: no limit is shared across processes (ISSUE, TG19); Rama's Phase 2 inputs on the combined budget (RS-05). Current external context (read 14-Nov-2026): the vendor's page states 3 requests per second for historical data. No source states the daily script's own pacing, timeout or retry. HISTORICAL (1) and (2); project records (3). Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-61; (2) IS-68, IS-69 with IC-17; (3) IS-136, RS-05.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L276 (`KiteConnect(api_key=...)` with no timeout: the library's 7 seconds, `connect.py` L38, L210), L278, L285, L90 to L93, L106, L183 to L188, L208, L288 to L289; no limiter import.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: no request of this process goes through the trading process's limiter; the only pacing is the 0.35-second sleep after each attempted historical request, none after `profile`, after `instruments`, after a name not found, or between the dates of a backfill; one attempt per request, no retry in the script or the library. INTERPRETATION: at most about 2.9 historical requests per second from this process (1 / 0.35 s, request time aside), about 5 percent below the vendor's current figure, with nothing that reacts to a rate-limit answer other than printing it and listing the symbol as failed; a failed symbol or date is not fetched again in the run, and nothing re-runs it later (the next scheduled run fetches its own date), so a transient failure leaves that symbol without stock candles for the day under a SUCCESS heartbeat (DC-02). Whether the 15:40 run overlapped the trading process's calls, and how the broker counted them together, is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P08-S016, P08-S032 to P08-S034, P08-S088; P02-S059, P02-S094; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. G7's "Wraps every broker call", and with it its 429 backoff, is not met by this process's calls, which is A04 RL-06's subject (no limit across processes; classification, owners and treatment unchanged, not counted here); this record's own part is the script's fixed pacing with no reaction to a rate error and no retry, whose effect lands on the stored data. Latent: whether any request failed is not established. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no retry mechanism exists: condition (a) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established.
- **Owner:** A05 (the script's requests); A04 (RL-06, the limiter and the budget across processes, unchanged).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 19 (daily side); P08 point 10; P02 points 10 and 11 (daily side); related A04 RL-06 and OIR A04-28; HA-13 (the trading process's bucket); DC-02.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (A04-28 holds Rama's Phase 2 inputs on the budget).

### 12.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### FB-01 — A list for the day can be stored in part with nothing to mark it: a row that fails to store is only logged, and rows the parser skips or a body cut after a complete line are not detected; the run ends with exit 0 and no sentinel, even with `fail_closed` on (A03 U-24's A05 side)

- **Area and flow:** a fresh list (header date equal to the run's date): `parse_secban_csv` builds the symbol list, then `store_fno_ban` stores it one symbol per transaction; `main` returns 0.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (errors only logged; no marker of an incomplete result); Weakness (missing validation of completeness); Flow and state (a stored state that does not say it is partial).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The module docstring L5 to L6 (lines 31-May-2026, `25c4a77`, and 22-Jun-2026, `5057425`): "Fetch today's NSE F&O ban list and store it in the DB"; L20 to L23 (22-Jun-2026, `5057425`): the format, a header line then "<serial>,<SYMBOL>" lines. (2) The failure policy L29 to L39 (22-Jun-2026): fail-open on "ANY soft failure (404, timeout, network, HTML block page, stale CSV date)"; `fail_closed` "preserves the old conservative 'block all F&O' sentinel behaviour for a future F&O era" (L40 to L42). (3) Commit `5057425` and `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L1540 to L1541 (22-Jun-2026): "Stale CSV (date≠today) is WARN + not stored", so a list that is not the day's list is not stored. (4) The tests: storing, querying and replacing rows; no test of a store failure or a cut body (P08-S092). (5) A03 U-24 (ISSUE (minor), owner A05): the fail-open policy covers fetch failures, not store failures. HISTORICAL (1) to (4); project record (5). Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) and (2) IS-112, (3) IS-113 (which lists SMAP L1531 to L1540; L1541 ends the same sentence), (4) IS-117, (5) IS-135; the point 32 row records that no source addresses a partial list (CD-34 withdrawn). No source states what a store failure or an incomplete body should do.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L124 to L139 (the parse: lines with fewer than two fields or an empty second field skipped with no count or log, L136 to L139), L171 to L194 (one `store.transaction()` per symbol; an exception is logged at ERROR, "fetch_fno_ban.store_failed", and the loop goes on, L192 to L193; the count is returned), L352 to L358 (the count goes only into an INFO record; `main` returns 0); L286 to L290 (the sentinel is written only from `_soft_fail`, never after a store failure); L220 to L237 (the query: the sentinel, else an exact row).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT and INTERPRETATION (Python and SQLite semantics): (a) a failure while storing one symbol (for example a database locked beyond the store's wait) leaves the rows before and after it stored, prints an ERROR line to standard error (FB-04: only WARNING and ERROR text is written) and ends with exit 0 and a SUCCESS heartbeat, with no alert and no sentinel; (b) the format as the code reads it has no row count or trailer, so a body cut after a complete line, or rows with one field or an empty second field, give fewer symbols than the file lists, with no line and no count; (c) nothing in the table, the log or the heartbeat marks either case. With `fail_closed` true (committed false), the sentinel still covers only fetch and stale-date failures, so a symbol missing from a partial store would read as not banned through `is_symbol_fno_banned`, the fail-open outcome that the knob is described as avoiding. Committed value `fail_closed: false`; effective value UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (overlays not read).
- **Evidence:** P08-S053, P08-S054, P08-S058, P08-S060, P08-S064, P08-S067, P08-S071, P08-S083; P10-S048, P10-S049; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The basis: a weakness the code itself demonstrates (a stored list that can be incomplete with nothing to show it), against the stated aim of storing "today's" list; the `fail_closed` part is latent (committed false). This record is A05's side of A03 U-24 (store failure) with the parse added; U-24's classification, owner and treatment (TG2) are unchanged. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: (a) the per-symbol `try` is a mechanism for a store failure, but it does what it is written to do (log and go on); no source says that a store failure or a cut body is handled otherwise, so (c) and (d) are not established.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline: the list has no reader (FB-14). Whether any store failed or any body was cut is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (FB-08).
- **Owner:** A05 (the script); A03 (the transaction helper, U-24's storage side, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 32; P08 point 15; STUDY section 16, A03 U-24 row (U-24 unchanged); FB-02 (the third way, a same-day re-run); FB-04; FB-07 (the parser's edge forms); FB-14.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (A03 U-24's treatment TG2 holds Rama's agreed direction).

##### FB-02 — A second successful run on the same date replaces the rows it lists and deletes none, so the stored list can be the union of two lists; a fail-closed sentinel written by an earlier failed run that day stays after a later success and keeps every symbol banned for the date

- **Area and flow:** more than one run for one `ban_date` (a manual re-run after the declared 08:35 run, or a re-run after a soft failure): `store_fno_ban`, `store_fetch_failed_sentinel` and the query.
- **Issue category:** Flow and state (a state not updated consistently: rows and the sentinel never removed by a later, complete result); Weakness.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The docstring L5 to L6: "today's NSE F&O ban list" (one list per day). (2) L40 to L42 (22-Jun-2026): the sentinel is the conservative behaviour for a fetch failure; `store_fetch_failed_sentinel` docstring L202 to L206 (22-Jun-2026): it "marks ALL F&O symbols banned for the day"; `_soft_fail` L282 to L283: "If fail_closed is ON (future F&O), also write the conservative sentinel". (3) The tests: storing, querying and "replacing rows", the sentinel blocking every symbol for its date only (P08-S092). (4) The failure alert's text L291 to L293: "If trading F&O today, verify the ban list manually at nseclearing.in" (a manual check, not a re-run, is named). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) and (2) IS-112 ((2)'s L202 to L206 and L282 to L283 IS-192), (3) IS-117, (4) IS-193. No source states what a second run on the same date should do with the rows or the sentinel already stored.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L180 to L190 (`INSERT OR REPLACE` per symbol; no `DELETE` for the date anywhere in the script), L209 to L213 (the sentinel row, `INSERT OR REPLACE`), L227 to L232 (the query returns True whenever the sentinel row exists for the date, before any symbol test); `core/schema.sql` L991 to L996 (key `(symbol, ban_date)`; A03's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT and INTERPRETATION (SQLite semantics): a later successful run for the same date replaces the rows of the symbols it lists (new `fetched_at`) and leaves every other row of that date, so a symbol listed by the earlier run but not by the later one stays stored; the stored rows then do not say which run listed them, beyond `fetched_at`. With `fail_closed` true (committed false), an earlier soft failure that day stores the sentinel, and a later successful run (for example a manual re-run once the file is available) does not remove it, so `is_symbol_fno_banned` still answers True for every symbol on that date: the recovery run cannot lift the conservative block. Whether NSE's file for one trade date ever changed between two runs is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; whether any re-run happened is UNKNOWN (FB-08).
- **Evidence:** P08-S058, P08-S060, P08-S063, P08-S064, P08-S071 (way 3), P08-S092; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor; latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The basis: the code demonstrates that a later, complete result for the date does not replace the earlier state (rows and sentinel), so the stored list need not be the day's list that the docstring names; latent because the sentinel is off at the committed value and the list has no reader. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: condition (a) fails (no mechanism exists to clear the date's rows or the sentinel).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline (no reader, FB-14; `fail_closed` false).
- **Owner:** A05 (the script); A03 (the table and key, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 32; P08 points 15 (third way) and 16; A03 U-24 (way 3 added on the A05 side, U-24 unchanged); FB-01; FB-13; FB-14.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### FB-03 — Every soft failure ends with exit 0 and a SUCCESS heartbeat, as the 22-Jun-2026 policy states; the later F2 list (15-Jul-2026) recommends for this job the functional criterion "ban list fetched (not the 404 fail-open fallback)", which the script never sets, and a day with no ban, a failed day and a day not run look the same in the table

- **Area and flow:** the run's outcomes as the operator's signals show them: the return value of `main`, the cron entry's `HeartbeatTimer`, the WARNING line, the WARN alert and the rows of `fno_ban`.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (a degraded run reported as clean); Intended against actual (a stated, later recommendation not implemented); Consistency across components (completion shown differently by different signals).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The failure policy L29 to L39 (22-Jun-2026, `5057425`): on any soft failure "log a WARNING", "send a WARN alert (Telegram only ...)", "record the heartbeat, and exit 0 — the job RAN; the data was just unavailable", which "keeps Cron Officer's 'failed' count for genuinely broken jobs"; exit codes L44 to L46. (2) Commit `5057425` and `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L1538 to L1540 (22-Jun-2026): "heartbeat + exit 0 (Cron Officer shows done, not failed/missed)". (3) The tests: the heartbeat "SUCCESS" with exit 0 on success and on a soft failure (P08-S092). (4) Commit `75d5e99` (18-Jun-2026, TASK #3): each scheduled job reports completion through a heartbeat with a per-job alert. (5) F2, `utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L24 to L31 (15-Jul-2026, `795a417`): "A job whose script exits 0 but whose real function failed ... records status=SUCCESS + functional_status=<the real outcome>, so a delivery/artifact failure can no longer read as SUCCESS". (6) `docs/audit/monitoring_hardening_15jul2026.md` L71 to L90 (15-Jul-2026, `1c891b6`): the F2 list gives `fetch_fno_ban` the "Recommended functional criterion" "ban list fetched (not the 404 fail-open fallback)" (L83), as an "incremental fast-follow", "one `timer.functional_status = ...` line each" (L89 to L90). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-112 and IS-122, (2) IS-113, (3) IS-117, (4) IS-93 and IS-122; (5) IS-200, (6) IS-202.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L273 to L297 (`_soft_fail`: WARNING, sentinel only with `fail_closed`, WARN alert, return 0), L328 to L331 and L342 to L349 (the fetch and the freshness guard call it), L361 to L374 (`_cron_main`: FAILED only for a non-zero result; no functional status set); `utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L24 to L31, L80 to L95 (A16's helper, interface only). The whole script (L1 to L378, re-read in the check) contains no `functional_status` and no `PARTIAL` assignment (the same finding as DC-02's search for the daily script).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): every soft failure (an HTTP error, a timeout, a connection error, an empty or HTML body, an unexpected header, a missing or other header date) writes a WARNING line, sends a WARN alert through A14's Telegram notifier (variables named `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` and `TELEGRAM_CHANNEL_PRIMARY`; names only) and returns 0, and the heartbeat records SUCCESS, exactly as sources (1) to (3) state; only a state-store open failure returns 1 (FB-12). ISSUE part (FACT and INTERPRETATION): the script never sets the functional status that sources (5) and (6), dated after the policy, recommend for this job, so at the baseline a run that stored nothing still reads as SUCCESS in the heartbeat, and the class of failure F2 names, "green heartbeat, dead work" (commit `795a417`), remains for this job. A store failure (FB-01) sends no alert at all. With `fail_closed` false (committed), a day with no ban, a day on which the run failed and a day on which it did not run all leave no row in `fno_ban` (P08-S063); the failed day differs only by the WARNING line in the declared cron log (which no code reads, P12-S053) and by the Telegram alert, whose delivery is A14's (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, FB-08).
- **Evidence:** P08-S063, P08-S065 to P08-S069, P08-S092; P12-S044, P12-S053, P12-S054; DC-02 (the search); code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The exit 0 and the SUCCESS heartbeat on a soft failure are the stated policy (MATCH); the later sources name this job and its functional criterion, and the code was not changed after them (ISSUE part, as DC-02 for the daily script). KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism for reporting a functional failure exists in the script: condition (a) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established at the baseline: the list has no reader (FB-14); whether any run failed is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A05 (the script's outcomes); A16 (the heartbeat helper, the officer), A14 (the notifier, the per-job alert policy) and A15 (monitoring), interface only.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 32; P08 point 2 (ban-list side); DC-02 (the daily side); part XC (the completion signals as a whole; P12 points 5, 11 and 12); FB-01; FB-04; FB-12.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### FB-04 — The script never sets up logging, so its INFO records, including `fetch_fno_ban.complete` with the date, the count and the symbols stored, are written nowhere: a successful run leaves no log line, while WARNING and ERROR text reaches standard error without its fields

- **Area and flow:** the script's log output: `get_logger("fetch_fno_ban")`, the INFO records of a fetch and of a completed store, and the WARNING and ERROR records of failures, under the declared command that appends standard output and standard error to `logs/cron-fno-ban.log`.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling and observability (outcome records that cannot be written); Weakness (a precondition of a shared component not met).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `core/logger.py` L266 to L271 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): `get_logger` returns a logger and "Handlers are NOT attached here; call setup_logging() at startup first". (2) The script's own records L160 to L163 and L353 to L356 (lines 22-Jun-2026, `5057425`; the call at L352 is 31-May-2026, `25c4a77`): `fetch_fno_ban.fetched` and `fetch_fno_ban.complete` at INFO, with `count`, `trade_date`, `url`, `date`, `stored` and `symbols` as extra fields, which show that the outcome was meant to be recorded. (3) The tests: success is "silent", meaning no alert (`tests/unit/test_fix135_fno_ban.py` L243 to L252; P08-S092). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-203, (2) IS-194; (3) IS-117. No source states where a successful run's outcome should appear other than the rows and the heartbeat.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L63, L302 (the logger, no `setup_logging` call anywhere in the script), L160 to L163, L353 to L356 (INFO), L193, L285, L290, L308 (ERROR and WARNING); `core/logger.py` L266 to L275, L452 (`setup_logging`, called only from entry points, P08-S068); `config/cron_registry.yaml` L291 (A16's log target).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT and INTERPRETATION (Python `logging` semantics, as P08-S068 records and checked): with no handler configured anywhere, INFO records are dropped and WARNING and ERROR records go to the last-resort handler on standard error as bare message text, without the extra fields. So a fully successful run writes no line about the list to the declared cron log; its only traces are the stored rows and the heartbeat; a successful run on a day with no ban leaves neither a row nor a log line, only the heartbeat. The ERROR lines of FB-01 and the WARNING lines of FB-03 are written, as text.
- **Evidence:** P08-S055, P08-S061, P08-S068, P08-S070, P08-S092; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The basis: a defect the code demonstrates against the stated precondition of the shared logger (source (1)); the script's structured outcome records cannot be written in any run. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the INFO calls are the mechanism for recording the outcome); (b) holds (by `logging` semantics the records are dropped whenever no handler is configured, which is every run under the declared command); (c) holds in part (the code itself records the outcome of every successful run, a case that arises on every trading day); (d) not established: no intent source states that a successful run's outcome is recorded in the log (the test's "silent" concerns alerts), and source (1) states a precondition for callers, not an intended outcome of this script, so the outcome does not contradict every source found.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established: the outcome is also in the rows and the heartbeat; the list has no reader (FB-14).
- **Owner:** A05 (the script's logging set-up); A15 (logging and observability, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 32; P08 point 18; part XC (whether other scripts share the pattern; observability hand-offs, P12-S069); FB-01; FB-03.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### FB-05 — The `--date` option can store only the date that the file's header names: any other value ends as a soft failure reported as "F&O ban list fetch failed" with the given date called "today", and with `fail_closed` on a sentinel is stored for a date whose list was never fetched; `--dry-run` stores no list, as tested for a successful fetch, but prints the file's list before the freshness guard, without saying whether it is the day's, and a dry run whose fetch fails goes through the full soft-failure path (a real WARN alert; the sentinel with `fail_closed` on)

- **Area and flow:** the command-line options of `main` (`--db`, `--date`, `--dry-run`, `--url`) on a run by hand; the declared run passes none (P08-S070).
- **Issue category:** Weakness (an option with no successful outcome for most values; no validation of the given date); Failure handling (a misleading failure text and alert).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The options L244 to L253 (lines 31-May-2026, `25c4a77` and `e55cb59`, to 22-Jun-2026): no help text and no docstring states their purpose. (2) The freshness comment L341 (22-Jun-2026, `5057425`): "CSV must be for today (else stale → WARN, no store)"; commit `5057425` (22-Jun-2026): "Stale CSV (date != today) → WARN, not stored". (3) The tests: `--dry-run` stores nothing, tested only with a successful fetch (`tests/unit/test_fix135_fno_ban.py` L324 to L334; P08-S092). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-195; (2) IS-113 (the commit) and IS-196 (the comment L341); (3) IS-117. The purpose of `--date` is not established; no source states what a dry run should do when the fetch fails.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L250, L311 (`ban_date = args.date or today_ist()`, the text not checked), L328 to L331 (a fetch failure goes to `_soft_fail` before the `--dry-run` test), L333 to L339 (`--dry-run` returns before the guard), L342 to L349 (the guard compares the header's date with `ban_date`; the reason text "stale CSV date ... != today ..."), L273 to L297 (`_soft_fail`: WARNING, sentinel with `fail_closed`, alert "F&O ban list fetch failed (<reason>)").
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): on a successful fetch, `--dry-run` prints the file's date and symbols and stores nothing, as the tests state. ISSUE part (FACT and INTERPRETATION, Python semantics): the source holds one file at a time, so with `--date` set to any date other than the one the file's header names, the run always ends in `_soft_fail`: a WARNING, a Telegram alert that says the fetch failed (although it succeeded) and calls the given date "today", exit 0 and a SUCCESS heartbeat; with `fail_closed` true (committed false) it also stores the sentinel for the given date, a date for which no list was fetched (and the text is not checked, so any string can become `ban_date`). With `--date` equal to the header's date the list is stored under it. `--dry-run` returns before the freshness guard, so a stale file is printed as the list with no warning that it is not the run's date; it still opens the live store first (FB-12). The `--dry-run` test comes after the fetch (L333), so a dry run whose fetch fails ends in `_soft_fail` like a real run: a WARNING, a real Telegram WARN alert, exit 0 and a SUCCESS heartbeat, and with `fail_closed` true (committed false) the sentinel row is written to the live store for the date, so a dry run can then make every symbol read as banned for that date (FB-02: a later success does not remove it). (Added in the check.)
- **Evidence:** P08-S057, P08-S060, P08-S061, P08-S066, P08-S067, P08-S092; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor; latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The basis: a weakness the code itself demonstrates (an option whose only outcomes for most values are a misreported failure and, with the knob on, a sentinel for an unfetched date; a dry run that can alert and, with the knob on, write the sentinel); latent because the declared run passes no option and `fail_closed` is false at the committed value. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: no source says what `--date` is for, or what a dry run should do on a failed fetch, so condition (c) fails.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline (no reader, FB-14; whether the options were ever used is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED).
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 32; P08 point 20 (`--date`); FB-02; FB-11; FB-12; FB-13.

##### FB-06 — Two legacy sources give different fetch times for the ban list: the schema comment says "Fetched at 08:30 IST"; the script's docstring, the schedule table and the declared cron entry say 08:35 (IC-16)

- **Area and flow:** the timing of the daily fetch as documented and as declared.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (documents disagreeing on a value); Intended against actual (a document that the declared schedule does not follow).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `core/schema.sql` L989 (31-May-2026, `25c4a77`; A03's): "Daily F&O ban list from NSE. Fetched at 08:30 IST". (2) The script's docstring L10 (22-Jun-2026, `5057425`): "Runs daily at 08:35 IST (before market open)". (3) `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L622 to L626 (18-Jun-2026; A16's table): `fetch_fno_ban.py` at 08:35 Monday to Friday. (4) A16's declaration `config/cron_registry.yaml` L278 to L291 (lines 18-Jun-2026, `ed08d27`, and 23-Jun-2026, `3a5dd03`): `schedule: 08:35 Mon-Fri`, `cron_expression: 35 8 * * 1-5`; commit `5057425`: "No crontab change (same 08:35 Mon-Fri schedule + job name)". HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-115, (2) IS-112, (3) IS-110, (4) IS-199 (the commit is IS-113); the conflict IC-16 ("Y is later; neither names the other ... Minor").
- **Actual code path:** the script has no time-of-day check of its own (`scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L300 to L374); the time is set only by the declared entry (A16's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the declared run is 08:35 Monday to Friday, which follows sources (2) to (4); the schema comment's 08:30 is followed by nothing. Whether the deployed crontab held this line is A16's and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (FB-08).
- **Evidence:** P08-S059, P08-S070, P08-S094; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL. A conflict between sources that the declaration resolves one way (08:35); no behaviour depends on the 08:30 text. KNOWN DEFECT not applicable (documentation only).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none (a documentation difference).
- **Owner:** A03 (the schema comment); A05 (the script's docstring); A16 (the declaration).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 32; P08 point 21; IC-16; FB-11; FB-16.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### FB-07 — UNKNOWN, with a MATCH part: the parser handles the documented format and rejects the documented bad bodies; what it does with forms outside that format (a leading byte-order mark, quoted fields, impossible dates, repeated or unknown symbols) matters only if NSE's file took those forms, which is not established

- **Area and flow:** `parse_secban_csv` and `_header_date_iso` on the body returned by the fetch; the stored text against the query.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (validation limited to the documented format); Logic (date parsing).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The docstring L20 to L23 (22-Jun-2026): a header line "Securities in Ban For Trade Date DD-MMM-YYYY:", then "<serial>,<SYMBOL>" lines, "header line only (no data rows)" when there is no ban; `parse_secban_csv` docstring L111 to L115: it "Raises RuntimeError on anything that is not a well-formed secban CSV (empty body, HTML error/block page, unexpected header)", and "The no-ban case (header only) returns (date, []), which is valid"; `_header_date_iso` L96: "Locale-independent: uses an explicit month map". (2) Commit `5057425` (22-Jun-2026): the parser, the HTML guard, "no-ban = header only", "verified live". (3) The tests: three months checked, symbols stripped and upper-cased, HTML and empty bodies and unexpected headers rejected (P08-S092). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-112 (the function docstrings L96 and L111 to L115 IS-197), (2) IS-113, (3) IS-117. No source states a requirement for other forms.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L86, L93 to L105, L117 to L141, L159; L227 to L237 (the exact-text query).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): the documented format is parsed as documented; an empty body, an HTML page, a body with no non-empty line and an unexpected first line each raise and become a soft failure (FB-11, FB-12); the month is looked up in an explicit map; each symbol is stripped and upper-cased. Current external context (read 14-Nov-2026 by the STUDY's drafting helper, P08-S057; not a legacy-period fact): the file began "Securities in Ban For Trade Date 16-NOV-2026: NIL", a form that passes the checks as an empty list; the docstring's "header line only" does not mention the trailing "NIL". UNKNOWN part (INTERPRETATION, Python semantics; the server's form not established): a decoded body starting with U+FEFF would fail the header test (`strip()` keeps it) and so end as a soft failure with nothing stored; a quoted field keeps its quote characters, so a stored symbol would carry them and would not match an exact query; the serial numbers are not checked; a repeated symbol is stored once (the key replaces it); a third field is ignored; the day and year are not range-checked (an impossible date then fails the freshness guard); no symbol is checked against any instrument list; the query compares exact, case-sensitive text. Whether NSE's file ever carried a byte-order mark, quotes or such values in the legacy period, and whether requests' decoding would keep a mark, are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P08-S052 to P08-S054, P08-S057, P08-S064, P08-S092; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The documented contract is met; the other forms depend on server behaviour that does not survive, and no source states a requirement for them, so no ISSUE is made (a lack of intent evidence alone does not make one). KNOWN DEFECT not considered.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline (no reader, FB-14).
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 32; P08 point 15 (quotes, U+FEFF); STUDY 18.2 item 7; FB-01 (silently skipped rows); FB-08; FB-11.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.
