### 13.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### XC-01 — The minimum instrument-row count of 1000 is written three times, as two parameter defaults in the start-up checks and a constant in the refresh, equal by value only

- **Area and flow:** configuration read by A05: the minimum size of `config/instruments.csv`, checked by the writer before it writes and by the trading process's start-up check after the boot load.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (one quantity defined separately in more than one place); Weakness (hard-coded values).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The refresh docstring RI7, `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L19 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "abort (exit 1) if output row count < 1000"; FIX-134 item 40, `_MIN_ROW_COUNT = 1000`, L65 (31-May-2026, `03ac3b0`) and `validate_instrument_rows`, L248 to L259. (2) BL-20, `utils/startup_checks.py` L919 to L924 (lines of 19-Apr-2026, `1e2d836`; A01's file): the start-up check "blocks startup if the loaded cache is too thin to be useful, forcing the operator to re-run `scripts/refresh_instruments.py`"; "A healthy full NSE universe contains ~2500-3000 rows". The two checks guard the same file for the same purpose, a usable universe, and BL-20 names the refresh as its remedy. (3) The foundation engineering rules 1.4 and 1.9, `docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md` L31 to L38, L66 to L69 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "No hardcoded ... settings"; "System behavior must be fully controlled via configuration." No source states that the two limits were meant to be one setting or two (STUDY section 19 did not propose the question). HISTORICAL. Intent sources (`A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`): (1) IS-89, IS-92; (2) IS-81; (3) IS-207.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L65, L248 to L259, L406; `utils/startup_checks.py` L911 to L915 (`check_instrument_cache_size(..., min_rows: int = 1000)`), L1520 to L1523 (`run_all_startup_checks(..., min_instrument_rows: int = 1000)`), L1606 to L1611 (passes `min_rows=min_instrument_rows`); `main.py` L2451 to L2474 (passes neither, A01's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the value 1000 is written in three places: the refresh's module constant (L65), the start-up check function's parameter default (L914) and the start-up runner's parameter default (L1523), which overrides the first when it calls it; `main.py` passes no value, so the runner's default governs at boot; no configuration key, option or environment variable sets any of them. MATCH part: each value equals its own source (RI7; BL-20's purpose), and at the baseline the writer's and the boot's limits are equal, so a file the writer accepts passes the boot check on count. INTERPRETATION (code reading): the three are linked by value only; a change to one would not change the others, and the writer could then write a file that the boot refuses (or the boot accept a file the writer would refuse).
- **Evidence:** P09-S025, P09-S043; P05-S052; P07-S041; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor; consistency: one limit defined in three places; weakness: hard-coded values), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (the values agree at the baseline; no mechanism fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline (the values are equal).
- **Owner:** A05 (the refresh's constant); A01 (the start-up checks and `main.py`'s call).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 33; P09 point 6; P09 draft question 2 (not proposed for Rama); IR-20 (match: the refresh's minimum), IN-14 (match: the boot's size check).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### XC-02 — Working directory and environment: the A05 programs resolve `config`, the heartbeat database and (for the ban list) the Telegram names from whatever launches them, while their own data paths are anchored to the repository; the declared cron lines supply both

- **Area and flow:** configuration read by A05 and the scripts' set-up: where each A05 program finds its configuration folder, its heartbeat database and its environment variables, as against its data files.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (two anchoring rules inside one process); Weakness (hard-coded relative paths); Failure handling (a run outside the declared set-up resolves silently to other files or to none).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The foundation engineering rule 1.4, `docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md` L33 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "No hardcoded paths ... Everything must come from: Configuration files, CLI arguments, Environment variables" (IS-207). (2) The scripts' own path constants anchor their data files to the repository: `ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent` (`scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L25, 18-May-2026, `044e5ba`), `_ROOT` (`scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L57, 31-May-2026, `25c4a77`; `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L47, 17-Apr-2026); DECLARATION, IS-208. (3) The declared cron lines, `deploy/cron/trading-system.cron` L66 to L67, L72 to L73, L81 to L82 (A16's file; machine paths and one identifier not reproduced), and its comment L8: each A05 job first changes to the repository folder and sources `.env` ("Python jobs source .env via `set -a && . ./.env && set +a`"); declarations, not evidence that any line ran (IS-206). (4) TASK #3, commit `75d5e99` (18-Jun-2026): heartbeats and per-job alerts through the shared wrapper (IS-93, IS-122). (5) This project's record, not a legacy intent: OIR A03-10 with A03 D-09, Rama's Phase 2 input that a run directed to a database copy writes no heartbeat or other record to the live database (RS-06; history OPEN). HISTORICAL (1) to (4).
- **Actual code path:** relative to the working directory: `main.py` L2099 (`Path("config")` without `--config`, 17-Apr-2026; the instrument file at L2441 follows it); `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L318 (`load_all(Path("config"))`, 31-May-2026, `e55cb59`); `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L238 (`current_holiday_set(Path("config"))`, 16-Jun-2026, `7f93c2f`); `scripts/find_symbol_aliases.py` L61 (`Path("config/instruments.csv")`, 07-May-2026, `4fcf984`); the shared cron wrapper `utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L119 (`db_path` `data_store/trading_system.db`, 02-Jun-2026, `f1dc556`), L122 (`config_dir` `config`, 18-Jun-2026, `75d5e99`), L178 to L179 (the holiday skip's defaults); `alerts/cron_alerts.py` L69 and `alerts/telegram_notifier.py` L274 (the alert's `config` folder; A14's). Anchored to the repository: `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L37 to L40 (token file, CSV folder, `DB_PATH`, `INDEX_UNIVERSE_PATH`); `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L304 (the database unless `--db`); `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L51 to L53 (the three file defaults). Environment: `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L31 to L32 (`load_dotenv(ROOT / ".env")`); `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L45 (`load_dotenv()` with no argument: python-dotenv's search from the calling file, as A02 records); `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` no `load_dotenv` (its Telegram names through `TelegramNotifier.from_env`, L266).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT, declarations): every declared A05 cron line changes to the repository folder before it starts the script and sources `.env` into the environment, so under the declared set-up the relative and the anchored paths name the same repository files, and all three scheduled scripts receive the `.env` names; the refresh line passes `--account` (identifier not reproduced). ISSUE part (FACT and INTERPRETATION, code reading): inside one process two anchoring rules apply: each scheduled script writes its data and reads its own inputs under the repository, but its holiday calendar, its heartbeat row and its alert configuration (and, for the ban list, the whole central configuration with `url` and `fail_closed`) are read relative to the working directory; the ban-list script alone loads no `.env`, so its Telegram names reach it only from its launcher; the alias tool reads the instrument file relative to the working directory while the writer writes it under the repository (IN-10 records the same for the trading process's loader). Started from another folder (a manual run, another scheduler, the pre-flight's fix, whose working directory is A20's), a scheduled script would still write its data to the repository while its heartbeat helper writes nothing (no database file at the relative path: nothing is written and no error raised, P12-S044) or writes to another database, its holiday skip falls back to a weekday check, the ban list's central load fails silently (A02 K-31) and the alias tool exits 1. Which folder and environment each actual run had is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (XC-08).
- **Evidence:** P09-S023, P09-S034, P09-S035, P09-S037, P09-S039, P09-S042, P09-S043, P09-S048, P09-S054, P09-S059, P09-S060, P09-S075; P12-S044; P10-S080, P10-S081; code and declaration lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Under the declared cron set-up the paths and the environment agree; the weakness is that correct behaviour depends on the launcher, inside processes that otherwise anchor their files, and that a divergence produces no message. Related: A02 K-31 (the relative `load_all`; ISSUE, Batch T5 group G2, AGREED, unchanged); A03 D-09 with OIR A03-10 (the `--db` copy against the relative heartbeat path; cross-reference only, unchanged). KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no source says the scripts handle another working directory; the declared set-up avoids the case).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; the declared lines set the folder and the environment.
- **Owner:** A05 (the four A05 programs' paths and environment reads); A16 (the cron wrapper's defaults and the declared lines); A17 (each launcher's working directory and environment); A01 (`main.py`'s `config` folder); A14 (the notifier's configuration folder); A20 (the pre-flight fix's sub-process).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 33; P09 points 9, 12, 14 and 17; IN-10 (loader against writer), IR-13 (the alias tool), DC-14 and DC-15 (daily side), FB-16 (ban-list side); related A02 K-31, A03 D-09 (OIR A03-10).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### XC-03 — Four separate symbol-to-token lookups, with different segment rules and different handling of a repeated symbol, and a fifth path for the index token; the scripts that call the broker do not use the instrument file at all

- **Area and flow:** producers and consumers of instrument identity: how each program turns a symbol into the broker's numeric token.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (competing sources of truth; duplicated logic).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The cache's IC11, `core/instrument_cache.py` L24 (17-Apr-2026): "token_map() ... used by CandleStore"; IC2 and the docstring: one read-only cache of the instrument file for the process (IS-76). (2) SR-F2, `sr_detector/fetch.py` module docstring (27-Jun-2026; A08's): the fetcher takes the token from the instrument cache, "not rebuilt via kite.instruments()" (IS-64). (3) The refresh docstring (17-Apr-2026): the file is built from three authoritative sources, the Kite dump giving the token (IS-89). Within the trading process the sources state one instrument source. No source states how the scripts were meant to resolve tokens (STUDY question 38: OPEN QUESTION narrowed in `A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`). HISTORICAL. (4) This project's record: OIR A03-41 with A03 U-15 (RS-07), the integrity check's `instruments` table (pointer only).
- **Actual code path:** the cache from the file (`main.py` L2439 to L2449; `core/instrument_cache.py`); the refresh's filtered join (`scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L154, L175 to L185, L306); the live dump mapped by `tradingsymbol` without a segment filter in `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L285 to L286 (A05), `scripts/forward_shadow_record.py` L113 to L114 (A18), `scripts/reconstruct_excursions.py` L294, L300 (A15) and `scripts/sr_corp_action_spotcheck.py` L45 to L49 (A08); the integrity check's database table (`scripts/gemini_data_integrity_check.py`, A15); the regime engine's index token from `regime.index_token` (`config/system_config.yaml` L550, committed 256265; A18's), passed through the closure (P06-S036).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): inside the trading process the candle store's token map and every `OhlcFetcher` symbol lookup come from the one cache loaded from the file, as IC11 and SR-F2 state (IN-19, HA-14). ISSUE part (FACT and INTERPRETATION): outside it there are three other lookups: the refresh keeps only the segments "NSE" and "NSE-EQ" and joins the security master by exact text; four scripts download the dump themselves, each in its own process, and map every row by `tradingsymbol` with no segment filter, three keeping the last row for a repeated text and the spot check the first; the integrity check reads a table that no tracked schema creates (so it stops at `NO_INSTRUMENT_TOKEN`, A03 U-15). The regime's index token is a configured number, and the daily script resolves the same indices by name from its own dump; the two paths share no code or data (P12-S041). INTERPRETATION: for one symbol text the lookups can give different tokens or none, for example when the dump holds rows of several segments for that text or the file is older than the dump; no code compares them. What the dump held in the legacy period, and so whether they ever disagreed, is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P12-S075).
- **Evidence:** P12-S020, P12-S021, P12-S041, P12-S057, P12-S058, P12-S075; P02-S093; P05-S064; P06-S036; P10-S084, P10-S085.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Intent is stated for the trading process and met there; for the scripts no intent is established, and the classification rests on the weakness the code shows, separate lookups of one identity with different rules (Document 04, section 5.3: duplicated logic; competing sources of truth). KNOWN DEFECT not considered (whether two lookups ever differed depends on the dump's content, UNKNOWN).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established.
- **Owner:** A05 (the cache, the refresh and the daily script's lookup); A15 (`reconstruct_excursions.py`, the integrity check), A18 (the shadow recorder, the regime token), A08 (the spot check); A03 for U-15.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 34; P12 points 1, 2 and 8; STUDY question 38; IR-16, IR-07 (the writer's join and duplicates), DC-18, DC-11 (the daily script's own dump), IN-19 (the token map); related A03 U-15 with OIR A03-41 (pointer only, unchanged); A04 RD-21 and RD-33 (MATCH, unchanged).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 38 bears on the intended design).

##### XC-04 — Symbol text, `NSE:<symbol>` quote keys and the 0.05 tick fallback are each handled separately at several sites; no shared helper normalises a symbol or builds a key

- **Area and flow:** duplication across the market-data code: symbol normalisation at each reader of a symbol; quote-key construction; the fallback tick.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (duplicated logic; the same value held as a constant and as literals).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The cache's field comments, `core/instrument_cache.py` L62 to L63 (17-Apr-2026): `symbol` is the "NSE trading symbol" (IS-77); A04 RD-33 (MATCH; A05 for the meaning of the fields): one text serves as signal symbol, cache key, `tradingsymbol` and quote key (IS-136). (2) FIX-181, `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L1414 to L1415 (23-Jun-2026): a missing or zero tick "failed OPEN"; the fix added a resolver with the 0.05 fallback and a per-symbol WARNING (IS-85); A04 CG-27 (MATCH; A04's). No source states a shared rule for normalising symbol text or one builder for quote keys. HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** normalisation: `core/instrument_cache.py` L143; `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L101, L127, L142; `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L60; `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L136 to L139; `scripts/find_symbol_aliases.py` L16; `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L276 to L281, L701, L792 (A06); `scripts/reconstruct_excursions.py` L300; `scripts/sr_corp_action_spotcheck.py` L47. Quote keys: the adapter's `get_quote` (A04) and each caller of `get_quote_raw` (A13, A10 at two sites, A11), and `scripts/t2_cnc_gtt_realtest.py` (A19), all with the literal exchange "NSE". Tick fallback: `orders/price_math.py` L27 (`DEFAULT_TICK`, A09's file) and its importers; literals in `orders/smart_tgt_manager.py` L747 (A11) and `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L181, L185 (A05); the emergency-exit paths `orders/eod_squareoff.py` L1542 to L1544 and `orders/order_reconciler.py` L2323 to L2325 pass the constant with no lookup (A12).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: each site normalises symbol text in its own way: the refresh and the cache strip; the ban-list parser strips and upper-cases; the webhook upper-cases the alias file and the raw symbol for the alias lookup only; the alias tool upper-cases only inside its ratio; the script callers compare the dump's text exactly; none removes a series suffix. Quote keys `NSE:<symbol>` are built at five or more sites, all with the same literal. The fallback tick is one shared constant at most sites and a separate literal 0.05 in the smart-target manager and the refresh writer. MATCH part (A04's records, unchanged): at the baseline the values agree (one exchange literal, one tick value), so no site produces a different key or tick from another for the same input. INTERPRETATION: the agreement is by repetition, not by shared code; a symbol that differs only in case or spaces is matched at some sites and not at others (the cache is exact and case-sensitive, IN-23 and IR-10). HISTORICAL: a reconciler comment records an earlier double prefix "NSE:NSE:SYM" (P12-S080).
- **Evidence:** P12-S062, P12-S063, P12-S080; P05-S047, P05-S089; code lines above as the evidence records them.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. No intent is established for a shared rule; the classification rests on duplicated logic that the code shows (Document 04, section 5.3), not on a demonstrated failure. KNOWN DEFECT not considered.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established at the baseline values.
- **Owner:** A05 (the cache, the refresh, the daily script, the ban-list parser and the alias tool); A04 (the adapter's keys); A06 (the webhook); A09 (`DEFAULT_TICK`); A10, A11, A12, A13 and A19 (their own keys, literals and fallbacks).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 34; P12 points 10, 13 and 14; IN-23, IR-10 (exact text; the "-BE" alias); related A04 RD-33 and CG-27 (MATCH, unchanged).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### XC-05 — The `candles` table and its completion signals: three programs write one-minute rows with no column naming the producer, and the readers judge the after-close fetch by three different signals, one of which counts any row with today's date

- **Area and flow:** producers and consumers of the `candles` table (in `analytics.db`) and the health signals of the daily fetch: the heartbeat row, the `.done` marker and the table's contents, with their readers.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (competing sources of truth; readers that disagree on one job's status); Flow and state (a status derived from data that any producer can write).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) TASK #3, commit `75d5e99` (18-Jun-2026): "universal heartbeat instrumentation + per-job alert policy" for the scheduled scripts; the completion of each scheduled market-data script is reported through a heartbeat (and a marker), so that the operator's officer shows done, failed or missed (IS-93, IS-122; commit `c3c1242`, 21-Jun-2026, pairs "heartbeat/marker" for the refresh, IS-94). (2) The registry's rule (`core/cron_registry.py` L132 to L147; A16's): a monitored job with no stated method is checked by its heartbeat row (DECLARATION; IS-205). (3) From 25-Jul-2026: the 15:40 fetch "covers the table" and every analytical need; the live writer is redundant and "must stay dark even if the tick feed ever wires" (DORMANCY L203 to L212, `config/expected_managers.yaml` L219 to L225, commit `e754c7e`; IS-108, IS-44; IC-07). (4) The candle store as the "Single source of truth for intraday candle data" (LF9, the store's docstring, 17-Apr-2026; Record 02 R02-08, 18-Sep-2026; IS-29, IS-125; CD-12). The sources do not name one completion signal for the daily fetch (STUDY question 37: OPEN QUESTION narrowed in `A05_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-15.md`). HISTORICAL. (5) This project's records: A03 E-48 (ISSUE; owners A15, A16, A03; TG9, AGREED 11-Nov-2026): the Control Tower's cron check counts any marker written today as seen while the Cron Officer reads the same marker as FAILED; TG9's treatment includes "monitoring reads each job's declared completion signal only" (a Phase 2 input, not a legacy intent).
- **Actual code path:** writers: `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` (the stock and index inserts; A05), `ensure_candles` (A15; also called by A18), `_persist_candle` in `main.py` L3926 to L3939 (A05 call site); the table `core/analytics_schema.sql` L24 to L39 (A03's: `symbol`, `instrument_token`, `ts`, `interval_sec`, the prices, `volume`, `is_synthetic`, a generated `date`; no column for the producer or the source). Signals: `HeartbeatTimer` in each script's `_cron_main` (A16's helper); the marker written by the declared line `deploy/cron/trading-system.cron` L82 (`fetch_daily_candles.done`). Readers: `scripts/check_cron_drift.py` L73 to L79 and `scripts/cron_officer.py` L390 to L422 (A16); `ops/control_tower/aggregator.py` L94 to L100, L123 (the cron adapter) and `ops/control_tower/freshness.py` L17, L39 to L44, L54 to L85 (the freshness stage) (A15); `scripts/reconstruct_excursions.py` L330 to L345 (A15, the marker).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): the daily job writes its heartbeat through the shared wrapper, and the drift check and the cron officer judge it by that row, as TASK #3 and the registry rule state (DC-15). ISSUE part (FACT and INTERPRETATION): one job, three signals, three readers: the drift check and the cron officer use only the heartbeat row; the Control Tower's cron adapter counts the job as seen when either a heartbeat row or the marker is today's (the marker is written after the run whatever its exit status, A03 E-48); the Control Tower's freshness stage calls `candles` "present" when `MAX(date)` is today, whichever producer wrote the row, and computes no delay when there is no marker. Because no column names the producer, rows from the daily script's index path (stored before its stock path, which a storage error can stop, DC-01), from `ensure_candles` or from the live writer each make the stage "present"; a day on which the stock fetch failed after the index rows were stored can therefore read as present in the freshness stage, as seen in the cron adapter (marker) and as FAILED or SUCCESS in the heartbeat readers, depending on the exit route (DC-02 records that per-symbol failures still give SUCCESS). The candle store's "single source" statement does not describe the table, which the daily script and `ensure_candles` also fill (XC-07 for Record 02). No reader names an A05 heartbeat job; the readers are generic, through the registry (XC-12). Which signals existed on any day is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (XC-09).
- **Evidence:** P12-S032, P12-S037, P12-S044 to P12-S049, P12-S065; P11-S034, P11-S172, P11-S192; P08-S025, P08-S072, P08-S074; P03-S069, P03-S084; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The heartbeat route matches its intent; the readers disagree on the same job's status and one of them derives it from data that any of three producers can write. Related: A03 E-48 (the marker readers in general; ISSUE, unchanged); A03 Y-06 (the two text forms; unchanged). KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no source says the freshness stage tells producers apart).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established; the Control Tower and the drift check are A15's and A16's reporting.
- **Owner:** A05 (the daily script's and the live writer's signals and rows); A15 (the Control Tower, `ensure_candles`, `reconstruct_excursions.py`); A16 (the drift check, the cron officer, the registry, the markers); A03 (the table).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 35; P12 points 5, 6 and 12; STUDY question 37; DC-01, DC-02, DC-03, DC-06, DC-15; CA-09; related A03 E-48 and Y-06 (unchanged).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 37 bears on which signal was meant).

##### XC-06 — Three consumers are documented as fed by the live feed or its candles, but nothing constructs them: the shadow engine's and the breach monitor's docstrings say "on_tick from live feed" and "Wired into the tick dispatcher"; the later registry calls the two `orders/` managers "expected-absent"

- **Area and flow:** producers and consumers of live ticks and candles: the consumers that the tick dispatcher and the candle store feed.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Weakness (code present but not used).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `capital/shadow_engine.py` docstring L14 to L18 (31-May-2026, `25c4a77`; A09's file): "Injected into signal_processor via set_shadow_engine()"; exits "checked via on_tick from live feed" (IS-26; CD-11). (2) `orders/sl_breach_monitor.py` docstring L13 (31-May-2026, `03ac3b0`; A11's file): "Wired into the tick dispatcher in main.py alongside candle_store / shadow_tracker" (IS-26; CD-11). (3) `orders/breakeven_manager.py` BM5, L16 (31-May-2026, `3315597`; A11's file): "Driven by candle close (high/low)" (IS-209). (4) Later: the module's own comment `orders/breakeven_manager.py` L394 to L396 (25-Jul-2026, `0a54c61`): "LATENT: main.py never constructs a BreakevenManager, so order_placer's reference is always None" (IS-210). (5) Later: `config/expected_managers.yaml` L335 to L342 (01-Aug-2026, `a5c3704`; A15's registry): `breakeven_manager` "expected-absent", "NEVER constructed — 0 refs in main.py (X6); exits thread CLOSED"; `sl_breach_monitor` "expected-absent", "zero importers repo-wide" (IS-211; the registry's L212 to L226 are IS-44). The registry is later than (2) and (3) and names both modules; no later source names the shadow engine (STUDY question 39: OPEN QUESTION narrowed). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L2963 to L2971 (the dispatcher calls `candle_store.on_tick` and `shadow_tracker.on_tick` only); non-test search: no construction of `ShadowEngine`, `SlBreachMonitor` or `BreakevenManager`, and no definition or call of `set_shadow_engine` anywhere outside the shadow engine's own docstring (L14; a search with tests and documents included finds no other occurrence); `orders/order_placer.py` L577, L622 (`breakeven_manager=None`, "None = disabled"; A10's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): the breach monitor and the breakeven manager are not constructed, as the later comment and registry state ("expected-absent"). ISSUE part (FACT): their docstrings still describe them as wired to the dispatcher or to candle closes; the shadow engine is not constructed, the injection method its docstring names does not exist, and so its exits are never checked by ticks; in the wired system these three receive nothing. INTERPRETATION: the feed's consumers are the candle store, the shadow tracker and the exit-retry callback only (P12-S008); the three classes are code present but unused. No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. Whether any deployed variant constructed `ShadowEngine` is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P12-S073).
- **Evidence:** P12-S008, P12-S009, P12-S010, P12-S014, P12-S073; P03-S091, P03-S092; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (nothing is constructed, so no mechanism runs).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none established.
- **Owner:** A09 (the shadow engine); A11 (the breach monitor and the breakeven manager); A01 (the dispatcher's wiring); A15 (the registry); A05 (the feed and the candle store as their producers, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 36 (CD-11); P12 point 3; STUDY question 39; CA-14 (the breach monitor's docstring named there as part XC's).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (STUDY question 39 bears on whether the shadow engine was meant for Live).

##### XC-07 — The five existing technical records against the code: no A05 claim differs, but two record statements describe market-data sources the code does not have (the instrument file as the broker's dump; the candle store as the single source of intraday candles), and no record treats market data in depth

- **Area and flow:** documents and records against the code: the A05 claims of `01_SYSTEM_OVERVIEW.md`, `02_REPOSITORY_AND_FILE_MAP.md`, `03_STARTUP_RUNTIME_AND_SCHEDULER.md`, `03A_CRON_REGISTRY_TABLE.md` and `LEGACY_SYSTEM_MANUAL_INDEX.md` (Document 08, section 9).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement; implemented behaviour that nothing describes).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** the records are descriptions, dated 18-Sep-2026, of a worktree at `e7bf477` with an uncommitted change set, described as "`970aabf` plus the S&R shadow files"; `e7bf477` is not in the clone (P11-S007, P11-S008). (1) Record 01 R01-07, L72: `config/instruments.csv` is the Zerodha instrument dump, "an HTTP fetch into `config/instruments.csv`" (IS-123; CD-36). (2) Record 02 R02-08, L765: the candle store is "the single source of intraday candle data for main.py's consumers" (IS-125; CD-12, record side; the module docstring is CA-14's). (3) The manual index RMI-01, L41: chapter 04, "Market data and data sources", NOT STARTED (IS-130). HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** for (1) `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L92 to L109, L112 to L145, L148 to L156, L176 to L185, L294 to L306 (the security master gives the universe, the index files the sectors and `is_fno`, the dump only the token, lot and tick); for (2) the `main.py` `OhlcFetcher` constructions over the historical closure (L3494 to L3788) and the daily script's inserts into `candles`.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): of the records' 149 A05 claims, 130 agree with the code at `970aabf`, none differs (eight PARTLY AGREES, six BASELINE DIFFERENCE, five NOT CHECKABLE: 130 + 8 + 6 + 5 = 149), and every A05 module and script has the line count the file map gives (P11-S179 to P11-S182, P11-S189; P11-S009). ISSUE part (FACT): (1) the file is a three-source merge in which the dump supplies three columns, and a symbol missing from the dump is still written, with token 0; (2) when their features are on (three of the six flags at the committed values), the `OhlcFetcher` consumers in `main.py` also receive broker intraday candles, and the daily script writes one-minute rows into the same table; (3) no existing record treats A05 beyond the overview, the file map and the schedule tables, so most of what this topic records (the feed's failure paths, the store's close rules, the cache's contract, the scripts' outcomes) is described by no record. The other six PARTLY AGREES are disposed of by their parts: R01-14 (IR-12), R02-31 (IN-11), R02-45 (IN-02; CD-37), R02-50 (DC-01; CD-32), R02-68 (IR-02; IS-129: the record omits that an empty series is also kept, a precision omission with no separate finding; IR-02 records it so), R01-01 (paper-only, not traced). The six BASELINE DIFFERENCE results come from causes the records state (the uncommitted change set; the S&R shadow files absent at the baseline) and are not differences of A05 behaviour (P11-S181). The five NOT CHECKABLE results stay UNKNOWN (XC-09).
- **Evidence:** P11-S007 to P11-S009, P11-S018, P11-S034, P11-S156, P11-S179 to P11-S189, P11-S190, P11-S202; code lines as the P11 claim rows record them.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION (the coverage gap). The records are not edited (Document 08, section 9). KNOWN DEFECT not applicable (documents).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none on behaviour; the records would mislead a reader about the instrument file's sources and the candle sources.

##### XC-14 — The operating values of the four A05 scripts are code literals and module constants with no configuration key, option or environment variable (added in the check)

- **Area and flow:** configuration read by A05: the settings of `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py`, `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py`, `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` and `scripts/find_symbol_aliases.py` other than the row minimum (XC-01) and the paths and environment (XC-02).
- **Issue category:** Weakness (hard-coded values).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The foundation engineering rules 1.4 and 1.9, `docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md` L31 to L38, L66 to L69 (17-Apr-2026, `bcf03b5`): "No hardcoded ... settings"; "Everything must come from: Configuration files, CLI arguments, Environment variables"; "System behavior must be fully controlled via configuration." (IS-207; LF-16 and CA-15 cite the same lines for the feed and the store). (2) Where a script's own text names a value, the literal equals it: the ban-list headers "to be safe" (IS-112; FB-09); the RI12 defaults for unmatched symbols (IS-89; IR-19); the index window (DC-16); the literal exchange (DC-18). No source states that these script values were meant to stay in code. HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L80 to L81, L92, L106, L167 to L168, L187, L208, L276, L285, L309; `scripts/fetch_fno_ban.py` L67, L72, L74 to L80, L147, L156; `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L55 to L63, L68, L72 to L85, L135, L154, L179 to L185, L197, L221 to L222, L268, L294, L304; `scripts/find_symbol_aliases.py` L36, L41, L46, L57, L72 to L73, L92.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (committed code values; no overlay can change them): the daily script's index window 09:15 to 15:31 and stock window 09:00 to 15:31, the interval `"minute"`, a fixed 0.35 s pause after each request, the exchange `"NSE"` for the dump, the stored `interval_sec` 60, and no client timeout (the library default applies, 7 s in the published 5.1.0 source; installed version UNKNOWN); the ban-list script's 15.0 s timeout, its two browser-style headers, the expected header prefix, the sentinel symbol and one request per run; the refresh's ten required symbols, five accepted series, twelve sector files in priority order, the F&O file name, the segments "NSE" and "NSE-EQ", the unmatched-symbol defaults (token 0, lot 1, tick 0.05), the written exchange "NSE", the post-write limits of 600 s and 1000 bytes, the zero-token limit of 20 percent and no client timeout; the alias tool's scores (0.95, 0.9, a ratio above 0.8), its top five and its 0.9 cut. MATCH part: each value equals what its own script's text states where a text names it (source (2)). ISSUE part (FACT): no configuration key, option or environment variable sets any of these values (the refresh's options set only its three paths, `--account`, `--dry-run` and `--verbose`; the ban list's `--url` and `--db` set its address and database only), so changing any of them requires a code change, against rules 1.4 and 1.9. Each value is recorded, with its own effects, by the part that owns the file (DC-09, DC-10, DC-16, DC-18, DC-20; FB-09; IR-07, IR-11, IR-19, IR-20; IR-13); this record judges only that they have no key.
- **Evidence:** P09-S036, P09-S041, P09-S043 to P09-S045, P09-S048; P02-S056; code lines above (spot-checked at `970aabf` in the check: L80 to L81, L92, L106, L167 to L168, L276, L309 of the daily script; L72 to L80, L147 of the ban-list script; L221 to L222, L268 of the refresh).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor; weakness: hard-coded values), with a MATCH part, as LF-16 and CA-15 classify the feed's and the store's literals. FACT; HISTORICAL. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism fails; the values work as written).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline.
- **Owner:** A05.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 33; P09 point 11; P09 draft question 3 (not proposed for Rama); the hand-offs of parts DC (point 33's windows, interval, pause and exchange), FB (FB-09's timeout and headers) and IR (the refresh's literals, "part XC owns the configuration point"); XC-01 (the row minimum), XC-02 (the paths); LF-16, CA-15 (the same rule for the feed and the store).
- **Rama:** not needed.

## 14. Cross-topic ownership and handoffs

Each part lists its handoffs, with what is handed over, in its own section .5 (sections 5.5 to 13.5). The index below gives, for each owner topic, the counted A05 items whose other side it receives (owners from the Master System Investigation Index; section 1.3). A05 keeps and counts its own side of each item; the owner traces its own logic and records any finding there. No topic is reassigned, and no other topic's ID, classification, treatment or status is changed. Items are collected by script from every part's handoff table; an entry that names more than one topic (for example "A10, A11, A13, A19") is listed under each topic it names. Prior-topic IDs named in the handoffs (for example A01 C37, A02 K-30, A03 Y-06, A04 RL-06) are kept unchanged and are listed in section 4, not here; handoff entries that cite only statement IDs (for example A12's one-shot backfill copy, P12-S079) are in the parts' tables.

Topics that receive no handoff from A05: none (every topic from A01 to A20 other than A05 is named above).

Dispositions: ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE 4 (questions 5, 12, 18 and 24, each at its date); split 4 (questions 7, 10, 11 and 21: one part answered by evidence at its date, the other an OPEN QUESTION narrowed); OPEN QUESTION as a conflict between sources, narrowed, 14 (questions 2, 3, 8, 9, 13, 16, 20, 22, 23, 25, 27, 28, 29 and 32); OPEN QUESTION narrowed 15 (questions 1, 4, 6, 14, 17, 19, 26, 30, 31, 33, 34, 35, 37, 38 and 39); OPEN QUESTION with no source found 2 (questions 15 and 36); none NO LONGER MATERIAL. For every question that touches subscriptions, ticks or live candles: No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. ## 16. Findings for discussion with Rama

Every finding is for the discussion with Rama after ChatGPT's review (Document 04, section 5.5); nothing is raised with Rama before ChatGPT has reviewed this AUDIT, and none is decided here.

(a) Established by evidence: the ISSUE items in part order (sections 5 to 13; their classifications are in each record; "(minor)" after an ID follows the record's own qualifier). There is no KNOWN DEFECT in A05. UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items are not findings established by evidence; they are listed after the ISSUEs, and those whose classification needs Rama are in (b).

- LF-01: Queue full: the newest tick is dropped with two CRITICAL lines per tick, against three intent sources that disagree with one another.
- LF-02: The feed's own kill-switch calls and notifier are never wired, although its comments say they are injected from `main.py`.
- LF-03 (minor): The dead consumer is restarted at every interval without limit or notification, against "restart it once"; nothing supervises the watchdog itself.
- LF-04: The watchdog's forced reconnect calls `close()`, which in the published library stops retrying before it closes; the feed's comments expect the library to reconnect.
- LF-05 (minor): After reconnects run out the feed stays down for the rest of the process, while the health readout is non-gating because "a feed disconnect auto-heals via reconnect"; the reconnect count meant "for metrics/alerting" has no reader.
- LF-08: Exceptions raised in three feed callbacks reach the library's thread, against the feed's own BL-11 rule that failures must not propagate into it.
- LF-09 (minor): The tick-age alarm checks only inside the entry window, while three sources say "market hours"; it is armed only after the first tick, as B2 intends.
- LF-10: `_on_connect` re-sends every tracked token in one unsliced message, bypassing FIX-059's slicing; on a failed send it logs "feed is live" while the library has begun to close.
- LF-11: `set_mode` overrides, which LF4 provides for, are not remembered and every re-subscription applies LTP; the feed's `set_mode()` and `unsubscribe()` have no caller.
- LF-12 (minor): LTP mode only: in the published parser an LTP tick has no exchange time, volume or depth, so the LF6 fallback key matches nothing, the dispatcher substitutes the dispatch time, and the bid and ask meant for the shadow tracker stay 0.0.
- LF-13: The callback registry's identity tests cannot match a bound method, so LF5's "register is no-op if already registered" fails for bound methods and a bound method cannot be unregistered.
- LF-14: `_ticker_thread_id` holds the consumer thread's identity, not the library thread's; FIX-088's guard that reads it is never given the feed; FIX-064's "own thread context" is not the library's thread, and the library hands nothing to its reactor thread.
- LF-15 (minor): Five shared feed flags are written from several threads without a lock; a connect between `subscribe`'s lock and its connected test can send a new token twice.
- LF-16 (minor): The feed's operating values are code literals with no key; the three `live_feed` keys reach no feed code (A02 K-30's A05 side); `reconnect_delay_sec` is the library's ceiling and also its minimum.
- LF-17: The "deliberately dormant" tick-feed decision against a live code path that can subscribe a token; the tripwire that "ENFORCES" the decision only counts calls and reports them at shutdown.
- LF-18 (minor): Documents, comments and tests about the feed that do not describe the code: the LF8 import list, stale line references, and two tests named for a forced reconnect they do not show.
- CA-01: Live candle volume: the store sums each tick's `volume` argument (FIX-135), against LF11's "not tracked" and comments that still say "always 0"; in LTP mode every real candle has volume 0, and for quote or full ticks the sum of a day figure would be neither the minute's volume nor the day's.
- CA-02 (minor): A tick joins the minute that is open when it is dispatched, not the minute in which it was received; the `ts` argument, `window_start` and `tick_count` are carried but never used.
- CA-03: The close does not check that the boundary was reached or which boundary it last closed: a long close can merge two minutes under one stamp, and an early wake can split one.
- CA-04: The live `ts` is a close-time stamp, as LF13, the store's field comment and the schema comment intend, but it is the moment the close ran (seconds and microseconds kept), and it shares one field and one key with candles whose `ts` other sources read as the minute's start (A03 Y-06's A05 side).
- CA-05: Synthetic flat candles are made as FIX-020 intends, but they continue without limit and with no session check, stay in the 500-candle history until they can push out every real candle, and reach every consumer and the table unmarked except by `is_synthetic`, which no in-memory consumer reads.
- CA-06 (minor): The synthetic-only guard meant to make "a starved/misconfigured feed" loud stays silent for a feed that never delivers a tick, and for a silent token while any other token ticks.
- CA-07: The candle timer thread has no exception guard, health check or restart; if it ended, ticks would keep accumulating with no close and no signal.
- CA-08 (minor): Failures of the close callbacks and of the live writer are nearly invisible: callback errors without a traceback, insert errors at DEBUG, and rows skipped by `OR IGNORE` not logged at all (A03 Q-16's A05 side).
- CA-09: The live candle writer is registered unconditionally, while the later sources say it "must stay dark even if the tick feed ever wires"; the "dormant tripwire" beside it only counts calls.
- CA-10 (minor): `stop()` says "Stop timer thread cleanly" but only sets an event: it neither joins the thread nor closes the open candles, and the shutdown then closes only the main thread's store connection (A01 C14's store side).
- CA-11 (minor): Reconnect: the open candles are discarded at the first attempt, as LF7, LF16 and G6 intend; the gap minutes become synthetic candles and nothing is backfilled, against G6's "Backfill the cache via REST historical API for missed minutes"; the next real candle may be partial and is not marked.
- CA-12: The store's token map is the boot copy for the whole process; `gc_sweep`, "Called on InstrumentsRefreshed event", has no caller, and the event carries no set of tokens for it (A01 C29's store side).
- CA-14 (minor): Documents and comments about the candle store that do not describe the code: LF11 to LF14, the "always 0" comments, the module's "Single source of truth", the registry's line references, and tests that disagree with one another on volume.
- CA-15 (minor): The candle interval, the history size and the guard's thresholds are code literals with no key.
- CM-01 (minor): The module header and two legacy documents describe an earlier, unwired two-helper module; the header's "single candle type" statement does not hold.
- CM-02 (minor): `adx`'s docstring and the system map say it reuses `true_range_series`; it computes the same true range inline.
- CM-03 (minor): `DAILY_TF` is defined and never used; `resample` tests a literal tuple instead.
- CM-04 (minor): Period defaults: `atr` and the EMA have none "baked in", while `adx` and `rsi` default to 14 in the module, and the periods of calls 1 and 2 are code literals with no setting.
- CM-06 (minor): The same indicator and period can take different values on different paths, because the Wilder recursion depends on where each caller's window starts.
- CM-08 (minor): `resample` reports slots not yet reached as gaps, does not check that it is given a lower timeframe, and does not check the one-zone convention it assumes (latent: no non-test caller).
- CM-09 (minor): `watchlist.entry_tf` and its schema comment say the retest timeframe is "built via candle_math.resample"; the code has no such build, and `resample` would reject the configured value "5minute" (A05 side of A02 K-26).
- CM-10 (minor): Input values are not validated: NaN, infinity, a high below the low and a negative volume pass unchecked and are treated differently by different functions (latent).
- HA-01 (minor): One "no candles" outcome for every failure, as SR-F4 intends; but a pacing timeout or a shutdown abort, in which no broker call is made, is logged as a WARNING that "historical_data failed", the abort against FIX-060's "INFO ... not a failure", and the closure adds no 429 path (A04 RL-05's A05 side).
- HA-02 (minor): Texts that state the historical request rate as "~3/s, never bursted" or "≤3 req/sec" through "the SAME" limiter, against G7's 2 per second with a burst of 2 and against the recorder's own limiter (IC-17).
- HA-03: The historical `Candle.ts` is the server's label unchanged, aware or naive, as the `Candle` type allows; but it shares the field name `ts` with the live close-time stamp, while the sources disagree on what a candle's `ts` marks (IC-13; the historical side of point 14).
- HA-06 (minor): The fetchers' cache (A08's, boundary): a `(symbol, interval)` cache with a TTL, as SR-F3 states; but its age is taken from the wall clock while its comment says "monotonic", its key leaves out the lookback window, and the regime fetcher takes its TTL from the `sr_detector` section.
- HA-07 (minor): The one-time check of whether the broker's candles are split- and bonus-adjusted was to be run once and its result "noted in SYSTEM_MAP"; no result is recorded anywhere, and no code adjusts for corporate actions.
- HA-08 (minor): `Candle.from_kite` turns a missing, `None` or zero volume into 0 and truncates a fractional one, so a consumer cannot tell a missing volume from no trade; a `Candle` carries no instrument, interval or source (latent).
- HA-09 (minor): Texts at the `main.py` call site that do not describe the code: "Both default OFF → nothing constructed" over a six-flag condition with `sr_detector.enabled` committed `true`; "the OhlcFetcher records a fetch_failed row"; "None if no token" for a handle that is never None in Live; and a "spec C" that is not in the repository.
- IN-01 (minor): `load` raises classes other than the two its contract names: a short row, a reader error and an `exists()` failure leave unwrapped, and an undecodable file is reported as "missing" (A05 side of A01 C9).
- IN-02: Token-0 placeholder rows load as ordinary instruments: they share one token entry, and three consumers treat token 0 in three different ways, one of them without a log line.
- IN-03: Other values `load` lets through without notice: negative tokens, any `exchange` text, unrecognised `is_fno` text read as False, `nan` and `inf` ticks, and repeated symbols or tokens ("later row wins").
- IN-04: `reload` returns a new cache that no holder would see and publishes a count-only event that nothing receives; FIX-092's refresh cannot reach the consumers as built (A05 side of A01 C29).
- IN-05: One cache for the life of the process: no refresh and no staleness check inside the trading process, while the sources disagree on when a refreshed file should reach it.
- IN-06 (minor): What `is_fno` marks and whether F&O contracts belong in the file: the cache's comments and MODULE 38 disagree, and the code follows the cache's reading (A05 side of A04 CG-04).
- IN-07: The exit-retry path tests `get_by_symbol(...)` for `None`, which the cache never returns: its "not in cache" branch cannot run, and a queued retry for a missing symbol makes the retry tick callback raise on every batch (consumer A10; A05's contract side).
- IN-08: The signal processor looks for cache methods named `sector_for` and `get_sector`, which do not exist, so with the real cache it returns "UNKNOWN" for every symbol and never reaches `sector` (consumer A06; A05's contract side).
- IN-11 (minor): Documents and comments about the cache that do not describe the code: two IC1 to IC15 lists, the "fail-fast via BL-20" sentence, the stub size, the "startup_checks does that" division, the design specification's refresh step and a record's consumer list.
- IR-01: Two designs for `instruments.csv` under the same labels RI1 to RI15: the code follows the refresh docstring's design and differs from TASK3 on the universe, the symbol text, the lot-size and F&O authorities, the sector labels and fallback, Kite-only symbols, the report, the log file and the default account, and from MODULE 38 on "NSE + NFO" (related A04 CG-04).
- IR-02 (minor): The refresh docstring's own counts and the alert's docstring do not describe the code: "13 sector files" against 12, four series against five plus every row with an empty series, and a "Telegram CRITICAL" alert sent as "ERROR" (related A02 F-35).
- IR-03 (minor): The refresh's own failure alert cannot fire: `main` calls `_send_refresh_alert(errors)` without a notifier, so the "[SYSTEM] INSTRUMENT REFRESH FAILED" message is never sent and the cron wrapper's per-job alert is the only alert path.
- IR-04 (minor): Exit codes follow neither RI14 nor TASK3: broker and token rejections end with 1, a missing or empty token file with 2, a malformed token file with 1, and 2 also means a missing library or an option error, so the status cannot tell an API or authentication failure from a file or configuration failure (related A04 CL-15).
- IR-05 (minor): Run signals that do not describe the run: `--help` and option errors record a FAILED heartbeat and an alert call, `--dry-run` records SUCCESS under the job's name although nothing is written, and a missing Twisted or autobahn is reported as "kiteconnect package not installed".
- IR-06: Missing sector files, a missing F&O list or a missing `index_members/` directory are skipped with no message, and the run can succeed with every sector blank and every `is_fno` "false", while TASK3 asks for a stop (related A02 F-35).
- IR-07: The output checks let through what they are meant to guard against: a required symbol written with token 0 counts as present, a smaller dump passes while at most 20 percent of symbols are missing from it, and repeated symbols, repeated dump rows and shared tokens are neither detected nor reported.
- IR-08: The writer and the loader disagree on what a valid file is: the refresh checks no tick or lot size, so a dump value of 0 or below for the tick, or below 1 for the lot, passes its validation and replaces the file, which the loader then refuses whole at the next start; `nan` and `inf` ticks pass both (related A04 CG-27).
- IR-10: An alias may point at a text the writer cannot produce: the refresh matches the security master's text exactly to the dump's `tradingsymbol`, with no series or suffix mapping, while the alias file maps a Chartink name to a Kite "-BE" text (IC-15).
- IR-11 (minor): The post-write check runs after the rename: it cannot catch the failure mode it was added for, it cannot fail in a normal run, and when it does fail the new file stays in place while the job is recorded FAILED (IC-12).
- IR-12 (minor): The documents disagree on how often the refresh runs: "on first setup and whenever NSE adds/removes symbols", "weekly", "daily" and "09:00 Mon-Fri"; the later declaration is daily at 09:00, while the docstring and TASK3 were not updated.
- IR-13 (minor): The alias tool's own example is not reachable by its rules, nor is the first tracked alias from its key; it reads the header row as data and the file in the platform's default encoding, and a legacy audit called it an orphan without a later decision.
- DC-01: The index fetch is called "FAIL-SAFE by construction" and said never to raise, but its database insert is outside its `try`, so a storage error while storing index rows also stops that day's stock candles, the CSV and the later dates of a backfill.
- DC-02: A run in which symbols fail, nothing is fetched or nothing is stored ends with exit 0 and a SUCCESS heartbeat; the functional status that the 15-Jul-2026 monitoring fix recommends for this job ("candles fetched") is never set.
- DC-03: The stored `ts` of the daily script is the broker's label written as naive wall-clock digits, the form a later source states; the schema comment calls `ts` the "ISO-8601 IST candle close", the current vendor examples read the label as the minute's start, and the live writer stores a close-time stamp under the same field and key (the stored side of A03 Y-06).
- DC-04 (minor): `INSERT OR IGNORE` skips a stored minute and a row with a null value alike, silently, and the printed "inserted" count includes the skipped rows; a re-run keeps the first-written values; the `IntegrityError` handler cannot be reached at the baseline schema (A03 Q-16's A05 side).
- DC-05 (minor): No check of the time of day: a run started before the session ends asks for a window that ends in the future, and any candle it stores is kept by the key when the scheduled run fetches the same minute again.
- DC-06: Since 17-Jul-2026 index rows are stored in the same table under the date, so the daily report's CSV fallback, taken only when the table has no row for the date, is not taken when the stock insert failed after the CSV was written; the regime commit states that index rows are "safe for every candles-table reader" (A03 G-12's A05 side).
- DC-07 (minor): Documents and comments about the daily script that do not describe the code: the `--candle-dir` option, the CSV as a "live SOURCE", the volume DEFAULT that "handles" index volume, the docstring silent on the table and the indices, and the schema's "for traded symbols".
- DC-08 (minor): "Traded symbols" are chosen by `date(triggered_at)`, which SQLite computes in UTC from the stored `+05:30` time, while the script's trade date is the IST date: a signal triggered between 00:00 and 05:29 IST is counted on the previous day.
- DC-10: The daily script's broker calls are paced only by a fixed 0.35-second sleep after each attempted historical request, outside any limiter, with the library's 7-second default timeout and no retry; a failed symbol or date is not fetched again (A04 RL-06's A05 side).
- FB-01 (minor): A list for the day can be stored in part with nothing to mark it: a row that fails to store is only logged, and rows the parser skips or a body cut after a complete line are not detected; the run ends with exit 0 and no sentinel, even with `fail_closed` on (A03 U-24's A05 side).
- FB-02 (minor): A second successful run on the same date replaces the rows it lists and deletes none, so the stored list can be the union of two lists; a fail-closed sentinel written by an earlier failed run that day stays after a later success and keeps every symbol banned for the date.
- FB-03 (minor): Every soft failure ends with exit 0 and a SUCCESS heartbeat, as the 22-Jun-2026 policy states; the later F2 list (15-Jul-2026) recommends for this job the functional criterion "ban list fetched (not the 404 fail-open fallback)", which the script never sets, and a day with no ban, a failed day and a day not run look the same in the table.
- FB-04 (minor): The script never sets up logging, so its INFO records, including `fetch_fno_ban.complete` with the date, the count and the symbols stored, are written nowhere: a successful run leaves no log line, while WARNING and ERROR text reaches standard error without its fields.
- FB-05 (minor): The `--date` option can store only the date that the file's header names: any other value ends as a soft failure reported as "F&O ban list fetch failed" with the given date called "today", and with `fail_closed` on a sentinel is stored for a date whose list was never fetched; `--dry-run` stores no list, as tested for a successful fetch, but prints the file's list before the freshness guard, without saying whether it is the day's, and a dry run whose fetch fails goes through the full soft-failure path (a real WARN alert; the sentinel with `fail_closed` on).
- FB-06 (minor): Two legacy sources give different fetch times for the ban list: the schema comment says "Fetched at 08:30 IST"; the script's docstring, the schedule table and the declared cron entry say 08:35 (IC-16).
- XC-01 (minor): The minimum instrument-row count of 1000 is written three times, as two parameter defaults in the start-up checks and a constant in the refresh, equal by value only.
- XC-02: Working directory and environment: the A05 programs resolve `config`, the heartbeat database and (for the ban list) the Telegram names from whatever launches them, while their own data paths are anchored to the repository; the declared cron lines supply both.
- XC-03: Four separate symbol-to-token lookups, with different segment rules and different handling of a repeated symbol, and a fifth path for the index token; the scripts that call the broker do not use the instrument file at all.
- XC-04 (minor): Symbol text, `NSE:<symbol>` quote keys and the 0.05 tick fallback are each handled separately at several sites; no shared helper normalises a symbol or builds a key.
- XC-05: The `candles` table and its completion signals: three programs write one-minute rows with no column naming the producer, and the readers judge the after-close fetch by three different signals, one of which counts any row with today's date.
- XC-06 (minor): Three consumers are documented as fed by the live feed or its candles, but nothing constructs them: the shadow engine's and the breach monitor's docstrings say "on_tick from live feed" and "Wired into the tick dispatcher"; the later registry calls the two `orders/` managers "expected-absent".
- XC-07 (minor): The five existing technical records against the code: no A05 claim differs, but two record statements describe market-data sources the code does not have (the instrument file as the broker's dump; the candle store as the single source of intraday candles), and no record treats market data in depth.
- XC-14 (minor): The operating values of the four A05 scripts are code literals and module constants with no configuration key, option or environment variable (added in the check).

Two notes on the owners' side, as the parts record them: IN-08 (the signal processor's search for cache methods that do not exist) is an A05 ISSUE at the interface; the KNOWN DEFECT conditions as read at the interface are offered to A06 for its consideration, and the classification of the failing code is A06's alone. HA-06's cache-key collision (a second window inside the TTL answered from the first) is in A08's `sr_detector/fetch.py` and is handed to A08 (KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given at the boundary: condition (c) holds only under a documented but not committed setting).

UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items (24; listed for completeness, not findings established by evidence):

- LF-06: whether `_on_noreconnect` could run more than once per outage, repeating the escalation.
- LF-07: the 600-second gap test repeats at every later attempt of one outage and measures from the latest close; whether it can be reached with 10 attempts and a 5-second ceiling.
- LF-19: how an authentication failure of the feed was meant to surface; `_mark_auth_failed()` cannot be reached from the feed (A04 P07-S216's A05 side).
- LF-20: the runtime and library behaviour on which the feed relies (including A01 W-24).
- CA-13: whether the first critical section of each close, which scans every mapped token under the lock, stayed "short" as LF17 intends.
- CA-16: the runtime behaviour of the candle store and the live writer.
- CM-05: whether the candles reached the functions oldest first, without duplicates; the module relies on its callers for order, as it states, and no step in the fetch chain sorts the rows.
- CM-07: two different values are called VWAP, and no source says which one the system meant.
- CM-11: whether any calculation call site ran in production, and whether any computed value was stored.
- HA-04: whether the broker's answers included a still-forming last candle; the fetch layer neither marks nor drops it, and only the A18 consumers state and apply a rule of their own.
- HA-05: whether the server accepted each window in one request; the comments say the 180-day window "fits one request per interval" and the 2-day window "respects the Zerodha minute cap", but no cap value is stated anywhere.
- HA-10: the runtime of historical access in the trading process.
- IN-09: whether one rule was intended for a symbol missing from the cache; the consumers handle a miss in five different ways and the cache raises no alert.
- IN-10: whether the loader and the writer used the same file; the loader resolves `config/instruments.csv` from the working directory, the writer from its own location.
- IN-12: the deployed instrument file and the cache's runtime behaviour.
- IR-09: whether the reference files were meant to be renewed after 17-Apr-2026; no code renews them or checks their age, and the output carries no as-of date or source identity, so a stale universe, sector list or F&O list cannot be seen in the file.
- IR-14: the refresh and the alias tool at runtime, the dump and the files of the legacy period, and each run's environment.
- DC-09: whether the daily fetch was meant to cover symbols whose signals ended in another status (for example `TIMEOUT`, where a position may have resulted), and why the stock window starts at 09:00 while the index window starts at 09:15.
- DC-11: UNKNOWN, with a MATCH part: a backfill requests every Monday-to-Friday date, as its docstring says, and resolves every past date with the one instrument dump taken on the day of the run.
- DC-12: the runtime behaviour of the daily script and the state of its outputs.
- FB-07: UNKNOWN, with a MATCH part: the parser handles the documented format and rejects the documented bad bodies; what it does with forms outside that format (a leading byte-order mark, quoted fields, impossible dates, repeated or unknown symbols) matters only if NSE's file took those forms, which is not established.
- FB-08: the runtime behaviour of the ban-list script and the state of its outputs.
- XC-08: the effective configuration and environment of every A05 program.
- XC-09: which producers and readers of the A05 artefacts ran, what data existed, and whether the records' tree held the same A05 code.

(b) Needing Rama's intent to classify (every record whose "Rama" field says his answer is needed to classify; each is marked OPEN FOR RAMA in its record and in its part's totals; the questions are put only after ChatGPT's review):

1. CM-07 (STUDY question 16): whether `session_vwap` (a typical-price VWAP over caller-scoped candles) was meant to give the same value as the broker quote's `average_price`, which the secondary screener passes as "VWAP", or to be a separate measure; the sources conflict without naming each other (IC-08); the item stays UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED without the answer.
2. IN-09 (STUDY question 26): whether one rule was intended for a symbol missing from the instrument cache, and whether a miss was meant to raise an alert; the consumers handle a miss in five different ways; no source states a rule; the item stays UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED without the answer.
3. IR-09 (STUDY question 30): whether the files under `config/reference_data/` were meant to be renewed after they were placed on 17-Apr-2026, and if so by whom and how often; no source states a renewal, an owner or a frequency; the item stays UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED without the answer.
4. DC-09 (STUDY question 34): whether the daily fetch was meant to cover symbols whose signals ended in another status than `PROCESSED` (commit `044e5ba` states the `PROCESSED` filter, with no reason), and why the stock window starts at 09:00 while the index window starts at 09:15; the item stays UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED without the answer.

Not listed: every other record whose "Rama" field names a STUDY question, which classifies without it (section 15). No item about credential validity, history or exposure is listed. No KNOWN DEFECT is given in A05; whether any of these behaviours occurred in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. For the live feed: No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.

## 18. Coverage gaps

- Runtime: no tick, candle, ban-list row, refresh output, heartbeat, log, crontab, unit state or database row of the legacy machines survives, and `config/instruments.csv` is not tracked. Whether any A05 path ran, with which commit (whether production ran any commit after `7d4970a`), with which configuration values and environment, whether any tick was received, any candle built or stored, any refresh, daily fetch or ban-list fetch ran and with what outcome, and what the broker's servers sent (packet lengths, candle labels and adjustment, volume meaning, the dump's content, the ban file's timing and form), are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (section 2.4; for example LF-20, CA-16, CM-11, HA-10, IN-12, IR-14, DC-12, FB-08, XC-08, XC-09). No token is subscribed at boot. A later subscription depends on the exit-retry path. Whether that path ever ran in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. A schedule or unit declaration is never proof that a job ran (A16, A17). The legacy documents' runtime counts (row counts of `candles`, "0 ticks ever", "NEVER RAN in production", log counts) are HISTORICAL and weighed for intent or impact only.
- Synthetic tests: one generic Python test was written for this AUDIT (part LF, T1: identity tests on bound methods under `weakref`, on Python 3.13.18; no legacy code imported, copied or run), used in LF-13 as INTERPRETATION; the other parts ran none and relied on the Python, `logging` and SQLite semantics that the STUDY's evidence states and checked. The legacy machines' Python, `kiteconnect`, Twisted, autobahn, `requests`, `python-dateutil`, python-dotenv and PyYAML versions are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (only the pin `kiteconnect==5.1.0` is in the repository).
- Sources not in the repository: the installed `kiteconnect` package (the published 5.1.0 wheel was read from PyPI as data only, Rama's decision G, its digest compared with PyPI's; it is not proof of the installed package); Twisted and autobahn, on which the ticker's reconnection and threads depend, not read (LF-04, LF-06, LF-07, LF-20); the broker's documentation, limits and data of the legacy period (current vendor pages were read only as current external context, with their dates); "spec C" of the SNR-DETECTOR-V1 specification (HA-05, HA-07); the records' worktree at `e7bf477` with its uncommitted change set (XC-09); `data/live_feed_manager.py`, the file G6 names, which does not exist at the baseline; the renewal history of the files under `config/reference_data/` (IR-09); who made the 25-Jul-2026 dormancy decision (intent-sources evidence file, section 7).
- Consumers not traced: the internals of the owner topics' consumers of ticks, candles, historical candles and instrument data (for example the exit-retry path and the order placer, A10; the smart-target and structure-exit managers and the shadow tracker, A11; the S&R detector and its fetchers, A08; the regime and PB-01 consumers, A18; the signal processor and the webhook receiver, A06; the Control Tower, reports and dashboard, A15; the heartbeat helper and the cron registry, A16; the pre-flight, A20) are handed to their owners (section 14) as boundary items or OPEN QUESTIONs for that topic; A05 audited only its own side of each.
- Each part's own list of what it could not establish, with the evidence that would settle each item: sections 5.7 (LF), 6.7 (CA), 7.7 (CM), 8.7 (HA), 9.7 (IN), 10.7 (IR), 11.7 (DC), 12.7 (FB) and 13.7 (XC); and the intent-sources evidence file, section 7.
- Security restriction (D-07; Rama's standing instructions): no `.env` file, token file, key file, `*.local.yaml` overlay or `config/accounts.csv` was opened; every repository-wide search excluded these files by pathspec; credential variables are recorded by name only, as configuration; passages that hold an account identifier (the usage line of `scripts/refresh_instruments.py`'s docstring, the `refresh_instruments` command in `config/cron_registry.yaml`, an older version of `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py`) or absolute machine paths are cited by line only or written with `<ACCOUNT_ID>`; no credential-validity, credential-history or exposure investigation was made and no security finding, issue, alert or register entry of any kind is recorded. A03 G-19 is cited by its ID only and was not examined. Effective configuration values (overlays, the environment) were not read; every configuration value cited is the committed one.
- Checks done: each of the nine parts and the intent-sources evidence file was drafted by one helper and checked against the code and the sources by a second, with the legacy clone at the baseline (`970aabf`, empty `git status --short`) before and after every pass (each part's sections .1 and .7). A cross-part check then completed the intent-sources evidence file (IS-140 to IS-211 added, each re-read at `970aabf` with its date; four existing rows corrected for line ranges, dates and citations), checked the nine parts against each other (the same fact in the same wording and labels; the same inherited item with the same ID, owners, classification and treatment; ownership per the Master System Investigation Index, among them the heartbeat helper as A16's and `alerts/` as A14's; every cross-reference ID existing and saying what is claimed; the dispositions of STUDY 18.1 points 1 to 37, 18.2 items 1 to 13 and questions 1 to 39; the four items needing Rama), and mapped every part's citation of an added source to its IS number; no result, count or classification was changed. The clone was at `970aabf` with an empty `git status --short` before and after the cross-part work. Claude assembled this document from the checked parts (the parts' check logs are working material, not kept: D-17), re-counted the totals by script (85 MATCH, 89 ISSUE, 24 UNKNOWN, 0 KNOWN DEFECT, 198 counted), checked that every record ID of the nine parts appears once as a record heading or match row, and ran the redaction scan on the assembled file before submission. A further helper then checked the header, sections 1 to 4 and 14 to 18 and Appendix A against sections 5 to 13, the intent-sources evidence file, the STUDY and its evidence, the Topic Completion Register's A05 entry, the Open Issues Register and the Decision Register (every record ID, count, question disposition and cross-reference; section 2.2 read for loose matches); it corrected listings and cross-references only, changed no result, count or classification, and found the clone at `970aabf` with an empty `git status --short`.

## Appendix A — STUDY statements that the AUDIT refines

The independent check of the STUDY corrected its statements before this AUDIT began (STUDY section 1.5). The AUDIT corrects, narrows or adds to these STUDY statements (found in the parts' section .2 tables and records, and in the cross-part work, whose working records are not kept: D-17):

- STUDY section 18.2 item 11 and P08-S065 name A15 for the Telegram notifier (`alerts/telegram_notifier.py`) that the ban-list script calls; the Master System Investigation Index gives `alerts/` (with `telegram_notifier.py`) to A14, and P12-S054 agrees (A14). Corrected here: the notifier and the delivery of the ban-list script's alerts are A14's (FB-08, FB-12, section 12.2.4 and section 12.7; DC-02 and DC-15 name A14 for the per-job alert policy, A16 for the heartbeat helper); the STUDY's "A15" is quoted in the parts as the STUDY's attribution.
- P05-S108 dates `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L243 ("the NIFTY index is NOT in the instrument cache") as a line of 2026-07-11; `git blame` at `970aabf` gives commit `536172c`, 12-Jul-2026 04:13 IST (+05:30), so the line is of 12-Jul-2026 (the intent-sources evidence file, IS-73, gives L243 and L251 as 12-Jul-2026, `536172c`, and L286 as 13-Jul-2026, `5a71fb6`).
- STUDY 18.1 point 14 (one field `ts`, several meanings) and point 24 (one cache for the process), which the STUDY groups across several sections, are each given an owning part: point 14 part DC (DC-03; related CA-04, HA-03, CM-08, CM-19), point 24 part IN (IN-05, IN-04; related CA-12) (section 2.3).
- XC-14 (added in the check): STUDY 18.1 point 33's literals of the four A05 scripts are judged against the foundation rules as one record (no configuration key, option or environment variable for any of them), beside the values recorded by the parts that own each script (DC-09, DC-10, DC-16, DC-18, DC-20; FB-09; IR-07, IR-11, IR-19, IR-20).
- IR-05, FB-12 and DC-15: the STUDY's point 26 records for the refresh that `--help` and option errors record a FAILED heartbeat; the AUDIT adds that the same A16 helper fact holds for the ban-list and daily scripts (any `SystemExit`, `--help` included, recorded as FAILED through `_cron_main`), weighed once, in IR-05 (ISSUE, minor).
- FB-02: to STUDY section 16's A05 side of A03 U-24 (an unmarked partial list), the AUDIT adds the same-day re-run as a third way to a stored list that is not the day's list (the union of two lists; a fail-closed sentinel left after a later success); U-24 is unchanged.
- IN-08: the STUDY's point 25 (the signal processor's search for methods the cache does not define) is an A05 ISSUE at the interface; the KNOWN DEFECT conditions as read at the interface are recorded for A06, whose classification it is.
- HA-06 and HA-08 (the corrections in the check of part HA): the KNOWN DEFECT conditions for the fetchers' cache key and for `Candle.from_kite`'s volume are restated (for HA-06, condition (c) holds only under a documented but not committed setting and (d) fails; for HA-08, (c) is not shown and (d) fails).
- XC-07 and IR-12: of the records check's eight PARTLY AGREES (STUDY section 15), R01-07 and R02-08 are recorded as statements that only the code contradicts (CD-36, CD-12) in XC-07; R01-14's "refreshed daily" is weighed with the other refresh-frequency texts in IR-12; R02-68 is counted by XC-07 among the PARTLY AGREES disposed of by IR-02, a precision omission with no separate finding.
- DC-09 and STUDY question 34: commit `044e5ba` (18-May-2026), which added the daily script, states the `PROCESSED` filter ("reads PROCESSED symbols from DB"), as the docstring L115 does (IS-179); neither gives a reason, so the question stays OPEN FOR RAMA for the other statuses and the 09:00 window.
